\Theta^{-1}\operatorname{diag}\{\mu_{1u}(Q)-\mu_{0u}(Q):1\le u\le k\}\Theta  \\
&=
\Theta^{-1}\operatorname{diag}\{\tau_u(Q):1\le u\le k\}\Theta ,
\end{aligned}
\]
where the last equality is \cref{obj:def:latent-effect}.  Thus \(R:=\Theta^{-1}\) is a real diagonalizing basis for \(\Delta Q(Q)\), its inverse is \(R^{-1}=\Theta\), and the eigenvalue vector is \((\tau_u(Q))_u\).
% lean: publishedMomentIdentity_penrose_left_inverse_of_injective
% lean: genuinePenroseInverse_eq_penroseInverse_of_injective
% lean: modelCompressedSpectralFacts_exists

\item
It remains to identify the spectral anchors.  The target-proxy mean factors through the latent masses:
\[
m_X(Q)=B\,p(Q).
\]
Indeed, for each target-proxy coordinate \(i\),
\[
m_X(Q)_i
=
E_Q[X_i]
=
\sum_{u=1}^k E_Q[X_i\mid U=u]\,Q(U=u)
=
\sum_{u=1}^k B(i,u)\,p_u(Q),
\]
using \cref{obj:def:target-feature,obj:def:latent-mass}.  The anchor condition in \cref{obj:ass:anchor}, together with \cref{obj:def:first-basis,obj:def:target-feature}, gives
\[
B^\top e_1=\mathbf 1_k,
\]
because for each latent class \(u\),
\[
(B^\top e_1)_u=B(1,u)=E_Q[X_1\mid U=u]=1 .
\]
By \cref{obj:def:compressed-operator},
\[
a(Q)=V^\top m_X(Q),
\qquad
c(Q)=V^\top e_1 .
\]
Using \(B=V\Upsilon\), \(V^\top V=I_k\), and \(R^{-1}=\Theta=\Upsilon^\top\),
\[
a(Q)=V^\top B\,p(Q)=\Upsilon p(Q),
\]
and hence
\[
a(Q)^\top=p(Q)^\top\Upsilon^\top=p(Q)^\top R^{-1}.
\]
Similarly,
\[
R^{-1}c(Q)
=
\Theta V^\top e_1
=
\Upsilon^\top V^\top e_1
=
B^\top e_1
=
\mathbf 1_k .
\]
These are the claimed left and right spectral-anchor identities.
% lean: publishedMomentIdentity_obsSummary_mX_factorization
% lean: publishedMomentIdentity_targetFeature_transpose_firstBasis
% lean: modelCompressedSpectralFacts_exists
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\begin{lemmav}{lem:diagonalization-condition-number-bound}[Diagonalization condition bound]
Let \(d_x\ge 1\) and \(k\ge 1\).  Let \(B\in\mathbb R^{d_x\times k}\), and let \(F\) be a thin signal factorization of \(B\), written \(B=V\Upsilon\), with coordinate inverse \(\Upsilon^{-1}\).  Let \(\tau\in\mathbb R^k\).  Suppose that
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Feature bound.)} For a constant \(L\ge 0\) as in \cref{obj:ass:bounded-x}, \(|B_{iu}|\le L\) for every row \(i\) and latent index \(u\).
\item \textbf{(Coordinate-inverse bound.)} For a constant \(\sigma_0>0\) as in \cref{obj:ass:proxy-rank-margin}, \(|(\Upsilon^{-1})_{ru}|\le \sigma_0^{-1}\) for every pair of coordinate indices \(r,u\).
\end{itemize}
Define
\[
K_{\mathrm{forw}}
:=
\operatorname{Ent}_{d_x,k}\operatorname{Ent}_{k,k}\operatorname{Ent}_{k,d_x}\sigma_0^{-1}
+
\bigl(\operatorname{Ent}_{d_x,d_x}
+
\operatorname{Ent}_{d_x,k}\operatorname{Ent}_{k,d_x}\bigr),
\]
\[
K_{\mathrm{back}}
:=
\operatorname{Ent}_{d_x,k}\operatorname{Ent}_{k,k}\operatorname{Ent}_{k,d_x}(d_xL)
+
\bigl(\operatorname{Ent}_{d_x,d_x}
+
\operatorname{Ent}_{d_x,k}\operatorname{Ent}_{k,d_x}\bigr),
\]
and
\[
\kappa_\star
:=
\operatorname{Ent}_{d_x,d_x}^{\,2}K_{\mathrm{forw}}K_{\mathrm{back}},
\]
where \(\operatorname{Ent}_{r,c}\) is the entrywise-to-operator norm constant of \cref{obj:lem:summary-metric-coordinate-comparison}.  Then the real diagonalization associated with \(F\) and \(\tau\) has condition number at most \(\kappa_\star\).
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:diagonalization-condition-number-bound}]
\begin{enumerate}
\item Write
\[
E_{r,c}:=\operatorname{Ent}_{r,c}.
\]
For these constants we use the following finite-dimensional entrywise comparison. If \(\mathsf A_{\mathrm{ent}}\in\mathbb R^{r\times c}\), \(0\le M_{\mathrm{ent}}\), and \(|(\mathsf A_{\mathrm{ent}})_{ab}|\le M_{\mathrm{ent}}\) for all entries, then
\[
\|\mathsf A_{\mathrm{ent}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le E_{r,c}M_{\mathrm{ent}} .
\]
Indeed, writing \(e_j^{(c)}\) for the standard coordinate vectors and \(J_c\) for the coordinate identification in the definition of \(\operatorname{Ent}_{r,c}\), each column satisfies
\[
\|\mathsf A_{\mathrm{ent}}e_j^{(c)}\|_2\le rM_{\mathrm{ent}} .
\]
Thus, for every \(x\in\mathbb R^c\),
\[
\begin{aligned}
\|\mathsf A_{\mathrm{ent}}x\|_2
&\le \sum_{j=1}^c |(J_cx)_j|\,\|\mathsf A_{\mathrm{ent}}e_j^{(c)}\|_2 \\
&\le c\,r\,M_{\mathrm{ent}}\|J_cx\|_2 \\
&\le E_{r,c}M_{\mathrm{ent}}\|x\|_2,
\end{aligned}
\]
which gives the displayed operator-norm bound. In particular \(E_{r,c}\ge0\). Since the columns of \(V\) are orthonormal, every coordinate of \(V\) has absolute value at most one, and therefore
\[
\|V\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le E_{d_x,k},
\qquad
\|V^\top\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le E_{k,d_x}.
\]
The same comparison gives
\[
\|I_{d_x}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le E_{d_x,d_x}.
\]
% lean: matrixNorm_le_entryBound

\item Let \(\Upsilon\) and \(\Upsilon^{-1}\) be the coordinate matrix and coordinate inverse from the thin factorization \(B=V\Upsilon\). Since \(V^\top V=I_k\),
\[
\Upsilon=V^\top B .
\]
Thus, for coordinate indices \(r,u\),
\[
|\Upsilon_{ru}|
=
\left|\sum_{i=1}^{d_x}V_{ir}B_{iu}\right|
\le
\sum_{i=1}^{d_x}|V_{ir}|\,|B_{iu}|
\le
\sum_{i=1}^{d_x}L
=
d_xL .
\]
Consequently,
\[
\|\Upsilon^\top\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le E_{k,k}(d_xL),
\qquad
\|(\Upsilon^{-1})^\top\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le E_{k,k}\sigma_0^{-1}.
\]
% lean: ThinSignalFactorization.coord_entry_bound

\item Define the forward and backward ambient maps
\[
\Phi_{\mathrm{forw}}
:=
V(\Upsilon^{-1})^\top V^\top+(I_{d_x}-VV^\top),
\qquad
\Phi_{\mathrm{back}}
:=
V\Upsilon^\top V^\top+(I_{d_x}-VV^\top).
\]
Using submultiplicativity, the triangle inequality, and the bounds above,
\[
\begin{aligned}
\|\Phi_{\mathrm{forw}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
&\le
\|V(\Upsilon^{-1})^\top V^\top\|_{\mathrm{op}}
+
\|I_{d_x}-VV^\top\|_{\mathrm{op}}\\
&\le
E_{d_x,k}E_{k,k}E_{k,d_x}\sigma_0^{-1}
+
\bigl(E_{d_x,d_x}+E_{d_x,k}E_{k,d_x}\bigr)
=
K_{\mathrm{forw}},
\end{aligned}
\]
and similarly
\[
\begin{aligned}
\|\Phi_{\mathrm{back}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
&\le
\|V\Upsilon^\top V^\top\|_{\mathrm{op}}
+
\|I_{d_x}-VV^\top\|_{\mathrm{op}}\\
&\le
E_{d_x,k}E_{k,k}E_{k,d_x}(d_xL)
+
\bigl(E_{d_x,d_x}+E_{d_x,k}E_{k,d_x}\bigr)
=
K_{\mathrm{back}} .
\end{aligned}
\]
Both constants are nonnegative because \(L\ge0\), \(\sigma_0>0\), and \(E_{r,c}\ge0\). % lean: ThinSignalFactorization.forward_norm_bound; ThinSignalFactorization.backward_norm_bound

\item Let \((q_j)_{j=1}^{d_x}\) be the ambient orthonormal extension of the signal columns used in the associated real diagonalization, and let the diagonalizing basis vectors be
\[
s_j:=\Phi_{\mathrm{forw}}q_j,\qquad j=1,\ldots,d_x .
\]
The backward map is the inverse of the forward map. To see this, put \(P_\perp:=I_{d_x}-VV^\top\). Then
\[
V^\top P_\perp=0,\qquad P_\perp V=0,\qquad P_\perp^2=P_\perp,
\]
and the identities \(\Upsilon\Upsilon^{-1}=I_k\) and \(\Upsilon^{-1}\Upsilon=I_k\) give
\[
\Phi_{\mathrm{forw}}\Phi_{\mathrm{back}}
=
VV^\top+P_\perp
=
I_{d_x},
\qquad
\Phi_{\mathrm{back}}\Phi_{\mathrm{forw}}
=
VV^\top+P_\perp
=
I_{d_x}.
\]
Hence the inverse-coordinate matrix of the basis \((s_j)_j\) is computed by first applying \(\Phi_{\mathrm{back}}\) and then taking coordinates in the orthonormal basis \((q_j)_j\). % lean: ThinSignalFactorization.forward_mul_backward; ThinSignalFactorization.backward_mul_forward; ThinSignalFactorization.linearEquiv

\item Let \(\mathsf{Basis}_{\mathrm{diag}}\) be the matrix with columns \(s_j\), and let \(\mathsf{Basis}_{\mathrm{diag}}^{-1}\) be its coordinate inverse. For every pair of ambient indices \(i,j\),
\[
|(\mathsf{Basis}_{\mathrm{diag}})_{ij}|
=
|(s_j)_i|
\le
\|s_j\|_2
=
\|\Phi_{\mathrm{forw}}q_j\|_2
\le
\|\Phi_{\mathrm{forw}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|q_j\|_2
\le
K_{\mathrm{forw}},
\]
because \(\|q_j\|_2=1\). Therefore
\[
\|\mathsf{Basis}_{\mathrm{diag}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
\le
E_{d_x,d_x}K_{\mathrm{forw}} .
\]
Likewise, for the inverse-coordinate entries,
\[
|(\mathsf{Basis}_{\mathrm{diag}}^{-1})_{ij}|
=
|\langle q_i,\Phi_{\mathrm{back}}e_j\rangle|
\le
\|q_i\|_2\|\Phi_{\mathrm{back}}e_j\|_2
\le
K_{\mathrm{back}},
\]
so
\[
\|\mathsf{Basis}_{\mathrm{diag}}^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
\le
E_{d_x,d_x}K_{\mathrm{back}} .
\]
The condition number of the associated real diagonalization is the product of these two operator norms, hence
\[
\operatorname{cond}
\le
\bigl(E_{d_x,d_x}K_{\mathrm{forw}}\bigr)
\bigl(E_{d_x,d_x}K_{\mathrm{back}}\bigr)
=
E_{d_x,d_x}^{\,2}K_{\mathrm{forw}}K_{\mathrm{back}}
=
\kappa_\star .
\]
% lean: ThinSignalFactorization.diagonalization_conditionNumber_le
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

The definition matches the ordering convention in \cref{obj:def:ordered-mass-target} and \cref{obj:def:labeled-weight-estimator}. The invariance lemma records the compatibility needed to read ordered masses from any valid representative of the same aggregated atomic law.


\section{Spectral repair and finite-net benchmark}

This appendix collects two auxiliary constructions used to connect the population modulus with sample-level procedures. The first is a spectral repair handle that turns empirical spectral information into a positive real atomic law even when eigenvalues are locally coalescent. The second is a class-advised finite-net benchmark that selects a stored admissible summary and then runs the exact spectral construction at that representative. Both constructions use finite-dimensional perturbation ideas familiar from matrix spectral analysis \citep{BauerFike1960,Kato1995,DavisKahan1970,StewartSun1990} and the moment-recovery perspective behind Wasserstein estimation from moments \citep{WuYang2020}.

The repair handle records the finite moment target and the positive-law replacement used in the modulus-to-estimation route.

\begin{algorithmv}{def:constructive-repair-handle}[Constructive repair handle \(m\)]
The constructive repair handle is defined for laws in \cref{obj:def:model-class} as follows.
\begin{enumerate}
\item At the realized empirical summary, define the first \(2k\) spectral moments by
\[
m=(m_j)_{0\le j<2k}.
\]
\item Among all valid positive \(k\)-atomic laws \(\sum_i w_i\delta_{x_i}\), choose a minimizer of
\[
\sum_{j=0}^{2k-1}\left|m_j-\sum_i w_i x_i^j\right|.
\]
\item Extract the repaired real probability measure from the minimizing positive \(k\)-atomic law, transporting aggregate projector mass across each cluster diameter after replacing individual eigenvectors inside overlapping localization discs by Kato contour projectors.
\item For the labeled-weight converse, differentiate the Bernoulli factorization of \(P_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{wit}}\) and solve the linear score-cancellation equations for the proxy and treatment primitives so that a weight displacement \(h\) preserves the factorization and the observed score has order \(g h\).
\end{enumerate}
\end{algorithmv}

\Cref{obj:def:constructive-repair-handle} isolates the spectral information needed for positive real-law repair. The moment vector \(m\) contains the first \(2k\) empirical spectral moments, and the minimizing positive atomic law supplies a real probability measure compatible with those moments. The contour-projector clause expresses the standard perturbation treatment of clustered spectra: inside overlapping localization discs, aggregate eigenspaces carry the stable law-level mass.

The finite-net benchmark makes this repair logic an exact-real finite search after a class-dependent representative library has been fixed.

\begin{algorithmv}{def:finite-net-program}[Finite-net program \(\operatorname{NetProgram}(A,\omega)\)]
The inputs are a stored representative library \(A=(A_i)_i\) and an observed sample \(\omega\).
\begin{enumerate}
\item For each representative \(A_i\), let \(S_i=(M_{0,i},M_{1,i},N_{0,i},N_{1,i},m_{X,i})\) be its stored five-block summary, and let
\[
\operatorname{rank}_{\mathrm{lex}}(i)
\]
be its position in the fixed lexicographic library order.

\item Define the spectral law attached to representative \(A_i\) by
\[
\lambda^{\mathrm{spec}}(A_i),
\]
the real atomic effect law returned by the thresholded exact-real spectral run for \(A_i\).

\item For the empirical five-block summary
\[
\widehat S_n(\omega)
=
(\widehat M_{0,n},\widehat M_{1,n},\widehat N_{0,n},\widehat N_{1,n},\widehat m_{X,n}),
\]
define the comparison criterion
\[
\Delta_i(\omega)
:=
\lVert M_{0,i}-\widehat M_{0,n}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}
+
\lVert M_{1,i}-\widehat M_{1,n}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}
+
\lVert N_{0,i}-\widehat N_{0,n}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}
+
\lVert N_{1,i}-\widehat N_{1,n}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}
+
\lVert m_{X,i}-\widehat m_{X,n}\rVert_2.
\]

\item If the library is nonempty, define the selected representative index by
\[
\widehat i(\omega)
\in
\operatorname*{arg\,min}_{i}
\bigl(\Delta_i(\omega),\operatorname{rank}_{\mathrm{lex}}(i)\bigr),
\]
with the minimum taken in lexicographic order.

\item The finite-library spectral program returns the selected representative and its spectral law,
\[
\operatorname{NetProgram}(A,\omega)
:=
\bigl(A_{\widehat i(\omega)},\lambda^{\mathrm{spec}}(A_{\widehat i(\omega)})\bigr),
\]
when the library is nonempty, and returns the canonical zero law when the library is empty. Its returned effect-law component is
\[
\operatorname{law}(\operatorname{NetProgram}(A,\omega)).
\]
\end{enumerate}
\end{algorithmv}

\Cref{obj:def:finite-net-program} defines the library selector at the level of stored summaries. The criterion is the same five-block \(d_S\)-type comparison used throughout the paper, and lexicographic tie-breaking makes the selected representative a total deterministic function of the sample and the library.

The estimator below specifies how the representative library is built from an \(\epsilon_n\)-net of admissible summaries and how the selected representative is converted into an atomic law.

\begin{algorithmv}{def:finite-net-law-estimator}[Finite-net estimator \(\widehat\nu_n^{\mathrm{net}}\)]
The input is the empirical summary \(\widehat S_n\); the tuning constants are
\[
D:=4d_zd_x+d_x,\qquad
b:=4\sqrt{d_zd_x}+\sqrt{d_x},\qquad
\epsilon_n:=n^{-1/2}.
\]
\begin{enumerate}
\item Partition the coordinate box \([-L,L]^D\) into a deterministic lexicographically ordered grid of half-open cubes whose side length is at most
\[
\epsilon_n/b.
\]

\item If \(\mathscr S=\varnothing\), set
\[
\widehat\nu_n^{\mathrm{net}}:=\delta_0
\]
and stop.

\item For each grid cube that intersects \(\mathscr S\), choose one representative
\[
q_{n,\ell}\in\mathscr S.
\]
Let \(\widehat q_n\) be the smallest-index minimizer
\[
\widehat q_n
\in
\arg\min_{\ell} d_S(q_{n,\ell},\widehat S_n).
\]

\item At \(\widehat q_n\), form the vertically stacked proxy moment and retain the \(k\) right singular directions whose singular values exceed \(\pi_0\sigma_0^2/2\). Denote the resulting orthonormal basis by
\[
V.
\]
Using this basis, form the compressed effect operator, left anchor, and right anchor
\[
\Delta,\qquad a,\qquad c
\]
from the compressed-operator formulas.

\item For each distinct eigenvalue \(\lambda\) of \(\Delta\), define the spectral projector
\[
E_\lambda
:=
\prod_{\gamma\in\operatorname{spec}(\Delta):\,\gamma\ne\lambda}
\frac{\Delta-\gamma I_k}{\lambda-\gamma},
\]
with the empty product equal to \(I_k\).

\item Output the atomic law
\[
\widehat\nu_n^{\mathrm{net}}
:=
\sum_{\lambda\in\operatorname{spec}(\Delta)}
a^\top E_\lambda c\,\delta_\lambda .
\]
\end{enumerate}
\end{algorithmv}

The estimator \(\widehat\nu_n^{\mathrm{net}}\) is class-advised through the choice of admissible representatives \(q_{n,\ell}\). Once the nearest representative is selected, the construction returns to the compressed-operator formula from \cref{obj:def:compressed-operator}: thresholding fixes the rank-\(k\) signal space, and the spectral projectors aggregate the anchor weights attached to each distinct eigenvalue.

\begin{theoremv}{thm:polynomial-net-law-estimator}[Polynomial finite-net estimator]
Fix integers \(k,d_x,d_z\) and real constants \(L,\pi_0,\sigma_0\). Suppose
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Dimensions.)} \(2\le k\), \(k\le d_x\), and \(k\le d_z\).
\item \textbf{(Bounds and margins.)} \(1\le L\), \(0<\pi_0\le (2k)^{-1}\), and \(0<\sigma_0\le 1\).
\end{itemize}
Then there are constants \(C_{\mathrm{mod}}>0\) and \(C>0\) such that, for every integer \(n\ge 1\), there is a class-dependent finite library \(A=(A_i)_i\) of representative summaries as in \cref{obj:def:finite-net-law-estimator} with the following properties. For every uniform provider of the fixed-parameter exact-real primitives on the admissible core parameter domain, the finite-net estimator \(\widehat\nu_n^{\mathrm{net}}\) is Borel measurable, and
\[
|A|\le C n^{(4d_zd_x+d_x)/2}.
\]
For every observed sample \(\omega=(O_1,\ldots,O_n)\in O_{d_x,d_z}^n\), the operation count of the same exact-real execution that returns \(\widehat\nu_n^{\mathrm{net}}(\omega)\) is at most
\[
C\{n+n^{(4d_zd_x+d_x)/2}\}.
\]
Its trace is exactly the empirical-summary trace, followed by the exhaustive finite-library search trace, and followed by the selected spectral-run trace when a representative is selected; if no representative is selected, the final spectral-run trace is empty.

If the program selects index \(i\) on sample \(\omega\), then \(S_i\) is a nearest library summary to the empirical summary \(\widehat S_n(\omega)\) of \cref{obj:def:empirical-summary}:
\[
d_S(S_i,\widehat S_n(\omega))\le d_S(S_j,\widehat S_n(\omega))
\qquad\text{for every }j.
\]
Among all tied nearest summaries, \(i\) has minimal lexicographic rank:
\[
d_S(S_i,\widehat S_n(\omega))=d_S(S_j,\widehat S_n(\omega))
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\operatorname{rank}_{\mathrm{lex}}(i)\le \operatorname{rank}_{\mathrm{lex}}(j).
\]
For the exact spectral run at \(S_i\), the estimator equals the run's returned effect law. For each treatment value \(t\), the observed proxy moment at \(S_i\), multiplied by the run's basis matrix, defines an injective Euclidean linear map. The stacked proxy moment at \(S_i\) satisfies the exact threshold characterization
\[
\frac{\pi_0\sigma_0^2}{2}\le \sigma_j(\operatorname{stack}(M_0(S_i),M_1(S_i)))
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
j<k,
\]
for every singular-value index \(j\). Every eigenvalue of the compressed operator formed at \(S_i\) with the run's basis and spans appears among the eigenvalues returned by that run.

For every stored representative \(S_i\) and every rectangular perturbation \(H\in\mathbb R^{2d_z\times d_x}\),
\[
\|H\|_{\mathrm{op}}<\frac{\pi_0\sigma_0^2}{2}
\]
implies that singular-value thresholding at \(\pi_0\sigma_0^2/2\) recovers exactly the \(k\)-dimensional matrix signal space for
\[
\operatorname{stack}(M_0(S_i),M_1(S_i))+H.
\]
For every index \(i\), there is a model law \(Q\in\mathcal M\) from \cref{obj:def:model-class} whose observable summary is \(S_i\), and the exact spectral run at \(S_i\) returns the quotient law \(\nu_Q\). Each returned representative effect law is a valid atomic law.

For every \(P\in\mathcal M\), every observed sample \(\omega\in O_{d_x,d_z}^n\) satisfies
\[
W_1\!\left(\widehat\nu_n^{\mathrm{net}}(\omega),\nu_P\right)
\le
C_{\mathrm{mod}}\left\{2d_S(\widehat S_n(\omega),S(P))+n^{-1/2}\right\}.
\]
Finally, for every tail level \(\eta\) with \(0<\eta<1/2\) and every \(P\in\mathcal M\),
\[
Q_P^{(n)}\!\left(
W_1\!\left(\widehat\nu_n^{\mathrm{net}},\nu_P\right)
>
C\sqrt{\frac{\log(C/\eta)}{n}}
\right)
\le \eta .
\]
\end{theoremv}

\Cref{obj:thm:polynomial-net-law-estimator} establishes the finite-net benchmark. For fixed dimensions and fixed conditioning constants, the representative library has polynomial size in \(n\), the online exact-real operation count is polynomial in the same sense, and the selected summary is the lexicographically first nearest library representative. The deterministic \(W_1\) inequality converts empirical summary error plus the \(n^{-1/2}\) mesh scale into quotient-law error, and the final tail bound applies the same concentration scale used for the main law estimator.

The intuition is that the admissible summary image is compact and finite-dimensional, so an \(n^{-1/2}\)-mesh supplies a representative close enough for the gap-free modulus to absorb the discretization error. At the chosen representative, the proxy-rank margin makes singular-value thresholding recover the correct \(k\)-dimensional signal space under perturbations below \(\pi_0\sigma_0^2/2\). The exact spectral run then returns the quotient law for a model law with that stored summary, and the modulus transfers the distance from the empirical summary to \(W_1\) distance between effect laws.


\section{Proofs and verification note}

This appendix collects the proof layer for the paper's formal results. The arguments establish the population stability statement in \cref{obj:thm:gap-free-positive-measure-modulus}, the measurable repair and estimation claims in \cref{obj:prop:summary-repair-total-borel,obj:prop:polynomial-net-law-estimator,obj:thm:collision-uniform-root-n}, the confidence and reporting guarantees in \cref{obj:thm:honest-root-n-confidence,obj:thm:cluster-adaptive-report,obj:thm:labeled-weight-upper}, and the local converse statements in \cref{obj:thm:matching-local-lower-bounds,obj:prop:same-class-quotient-minimax,obj:prop:same-class-labeled-minimax,obj:thm:published-vmw-converse-transfer}.

The proof of \cref{obj:thm:gap-free-positive-measure-modulus} uses the compact summary closure from \cref{obj:prop:summary-closure-compact} and the quotient moment representation in \cref{obj:def:summary-closure}. The finite-dimensional moment map gives a Lipschitz modulus from the observable summary coordinates to the \(W_1\) law target on the compact domain. The extension clause follows by completing the admissible image under \(d_S\), with \cref{obj:def:summary-space-repair} and \cref{obj:prop:summary-repair-total-borel} supplying the measurable nearest-summary selection needed to evaluate the extended map at empirical summaries.

The estimation proofs combine this deterministic modulus with concentration for the empirical five-block summary in \cref{obj:def:empirical-summary}. For the repaired estimator, nearest-summary projection keeps the evaluated summary within the same concentration scale as the population summary. For the structured-lattice estimator, \cref{obj:def:structured-lattice-program} and \cref{obj:prop:polynomial-net-law-estimator} give a duplicate-free finite search whose deterministic error is bounded by the empirical summary error and the \(n^{-1/2}\) lattice scale. These two routes yield the joint root-\(n\) tail statement in \cref{obj:thm:collision-uniform-root-n}.

The confidence-set proofs use the same concentration event with the objects in \cref{obj:def:wasserstein-confidence-set}. The summary-inversion set inherits its diameter from the modulus on \(\mathcal K\), while the algorithmic outer set inherits its radius from the deterministic lattice bound. The transport-plan formulation in \cref{obj:synth_10} gives the finite representation used in \cref{obj:thm:honest-root-n-confidence}. The cluster-report argument then applies the atom floor and \(W_1\) transport budget in \cref{obj:def:cluster-report} to associate true atoms with empirical components, yielding the support containment, mass-coverage, and interval-length conclusions in \cref{obj:thm:cluster-adaptive-report}.

For ordered weights, the proof of \cref{obj:thm:labeled-weight-upper} works on the gap-local stratum in \cref{obj:def:gap-stratum}. The positive effect gap converts law-level transport error into an \(\ell_1\) error bound for the effect-ordered mass vector defined in \cref{obj:def:ordered-mass-target} and estimated by \cref{obj:def:labeled-weight-estimator}. This gives the clipped inverse-gap upper rate stated in the theorem.

The lower-bound proofs use the explicit two-class witness in \cref{obj:def:two-class-witness}, its validity certificate in \cref{obj:prop:two-class-witness-valid}, and the local experiments in \cref{obj:def:local-quotient-experiment,obj:def:local-weight-experiment}. Standard two-point testing inequalities \citep{LeCam1986,PolyanskiyWu2019} convert the displayed Kullback--Leibler bounds and target separations into the local risk lower bounds in \cref{obj:thm:matching-local-lower-bounds}. The same comparisons give the same-class minimax rates in \cref{obj:prop:same-class-quotient-minimax,obj:prop:same-class-labeled-minimax}. The transfer statement in \cref{obj:thm:published-vmw-converse-transfer} expresses the same witness-pair logic for comparator classes stated in the published VMW vocabulary \citep{VirkMazaheriWu2026}.

\section{Proofs of the main results}\label{sec:deferred-proofs}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:prop:observed-vmw-margin-inclusion}]
Fix \(P\in\mathcal M\) satisfying the displayed hypotheses, and set
\[
L_\tau:=\frac{4L\sqrt{d_z}}{\sigma_0}.
\]
All model restrictions used below are the components of \(\mathcal M\) in
\cref{obj:def:model-class}.

\begin{enumerate}
\item For each treatment arm \(t\in\{0,1\}\), \cref{obj:lem:arm-mass-lower-bound} gives
\[
P(T=t)\ge k\pi_0 .
\]
Since \(k\ge2\) and \(\pi_0>0\), this arm probability is positive.  Define
\[
\Gamma_t
:=
\operatorname{diag}\left\{
\frac{P(U=u,T=t)}{P(T=t)}:1\le u\le k
\right\}.
\]
The diagonal entries are
\[
\frac{P(U=u,T=t)}{P(T=t)}
=
P(U=u\mid T=t),
\]
so \cref{obj:lem:observed-proxy-moment-factorization} yields
\[
M_t(P)=A_t\Gamma_tB^\top
=
A_t\operatorname{diag}\{P(U=u\mid T=t):1\le u\le k\}B^\top .
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.ProxyEffectlawEigencollisionFrontier.observedProxyMoment_factorization

\item Latent-arm positivity gives, for every latent class \(u\),
\[
\frac{P(U=u,T=t)}{P(T=t)}
\ge
P(U=u,T=t)
\ge
\pi_0,
\]
because \(P(T=t)\le1\).  Hence, for every \(\beta\in\mathbb R^k\),
\[
\|\Gamma_t\beta\|_2^2
=
\sum_{u=1}^k \Gamma_t(u,u)^2\beta_u^2
\ge
\pi_0^2\sum_{u=1}^k\beta_u^2
=
\pi_0^2\|\beta\|_2^2,
\]
and therefore
\[
\sigma_k(\Gamma_t)\ge\pi_0 .
\]
The proxy-rank margin in \cref{obj:ass:proxy-rank-margin} gives
\[
\sigma_k(A_t)\ge\sigma_0,
\qquad
\sigma_k(B)\ge\sigma_0 .
\]
Using the product lower bound for full-column-rank factors in the factorization above,
\[
\sigma_k\{M_t(P)\}
=
\sigma_k(A_t\Gamma_tB^\top)
\ge
\sigma_k(A_t)\sigma_k(\Gamma_t)\sigma_k(B)
\ge
\pi_0\sigma_0^2 .
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.ProxyEffectlawEigencollisionFrontier.observedProxyMoment_minSingular_of_factorization

\item Let
\[
\mathcal H(P):=
\operatorname{row}\!\left(\operatorname{stack}(M_0(P),M_1(P))\right)
\subseteq\mathbb R^{d_x},
\]
and let \(V\in\mathbb R^{d_x\times k}\) have orthonormal columns spanning \(\mathcal H(P)\).  For the fixed arm \(t\), the row space of \(M_t(P)\) is contained in \(\mathcal H(P)\):
\[
\operatorname{range}\{M_t(P)^\top\}\subseteq \mathcal H(P)=\operatorname{range}(V).
\]
The lower bound from the previous step gives \(\sigma_k\{M_t(P)\}>0\), so this row space has dimension at least \(k\).  Since \(\operatorname{range}(V)\) has dimension \(k\), the two spaces are equal.  The map \(r\mapsto Vr\) is an isometry from \(\mathbb R^k\) onto this row space, and composing \(M_t(P)\) with that isometry preserves its positive singular values.  Thus
\[
\sigma_k\{M_t(P)V\}
=
\sigma_k\{M_t(P)\},
\qquad
\pi_0\sigma_0^2\le \sigma_k\{M_t(P)V\}.
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.ProxyEffectlawEigencollisionFrontier.observedProxyMoment_compression_margin

\item The stacked proxy signal margin is supplied by
\cref{obj:lem:stacked-proxy-moment-signal-margin}.  Applied with the present model membership and numerical hypotheses, it gives
\[
\pi_0\sigma_0^2
\le
\sigma_k\!\left(\operatorname{stack}(M_0(P),M_1(P))\right),
\]
with the displayed one-based singular-value indexing of the statement.
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.ProxyEffectlawEigencollisionFrontier.stackedProxyMoment_minSingular

\item By \cref{obj:def:observable-population-moments},
\[
M_t(P)=E_P[ZX^\top\mid T=t],
\qquad
N_t(P)=E_P[YZX^\top\mid T=t],
\qquad
m_X(P)=E_P[X].
\]
The envelope components of \(\mathcal M\) give
\[
\|ZX^\top\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le L,
\qquad
\|YZX^\top\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le L,
\qquad
\|X\|_2\le L
\quad P\text{-a.s.}
\]
Since \(P(T=t)>0\), Jensen's inequality for the two conditional matrix means and for the unconditional vector mean yields
\[
\|M_t(P)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le L,
\qquad
\|N_t(P)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le L,
\qquad
\|m_X(P)\|_2\le L .
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.ProxyEffectlawEigencollisionFrontier.observedSummary_envelopes_of_model

\item Fix \(u\in\{1,\ldots,k\}\) and \(t\in\{0,1\}\), and put
\[
\mathsf{Cell}_{u,t}:=\{U=u,T=t\},
\qquad
\mathsf{Class}_u:=\{U=u\}.
\]
Latent-arm positivity gives
\[
P(\mathsf{Cell}_{u,t})\ge\pi_0>0.
\]
Let \(e_u^{(k)}\) be the \(u\)th canonical vector in \(\mathbb R^k\).  The proxy-rank margin implies
\[
\|A_te_u^{(k)}\|_2
\ge
\sigma_k(A_t)\|e_u^{(k)}\|_2
\ge
\sigma_0 .
\]
Since \(d_z\ge k\ge2\), some coordinate \(\jmath_{u,t}\in\{1,\ldots,d_z\}\) satisfies
\[
\left|E_P[Z_{\jmath_{u,t}}\mid \mathsf{Cell}_{u,t}]\right|
=
|A_t(\jmath_{u,t},u)|
\ge
\frac{\sigma_0}{\sqrt{d_z}} .
\]
Define
\[
\zeta_{u,t}:=\frac{\sigma_0}{2\sqrt{d_z}},
\qquad
\mathsf{Large}_{u,t}:=\{|Z_{\jmath_{u,t}}|\ge \zeta_{u,t}\}.
\]
If \(P(\mathsf{Large}_{u,t}\mid\mathsf{Cell}_{u,t})=0\), then
\[
\left|E_P[Z_{\jmath_{u,t}}\mid \mathsf{Cell}_{u,t}]\right|
\le
E_P[|Z_{\jmath_{u,t}}|\mid \mathsf{Cell}_{u,t}]
\le
\zeta_{u,t}
<
\frac{\sigma_0}{\sqrt{d_z}},
\]
a contradiction.  Hence
\[
P(\mathsf{Large}_{u,t}\mid\mathsf{Cell}_{u,t})>0 .
\]
By \cref{obj:lem:proxy-coordinate-envelopes},
\[
|YZ_{\jmath_{u,t}}|\le L
\quad P\text{-a.s. on }\mathsf{Cell}_{u,t}.
\]
The reference-proxy separation component in \cref{obj:ass:reference-proxy-separation} gives \(Z_{\jmath_{u,t}}\perp Y\mid \mathsf{Cell}_{u,t}\).  Therefore positive conditional probability for
\[
\mathsf{Bad}_{u,t}:=
\left\{|Y|>\frac{L}{\zeta_{u,t}}\right\}
\]
would give
\[
P(\mathsf{Large}_{u,t}\cap\mathsf{Bad}_{u,t}\mid\mathsf{Cell}_{u,t})
=
P(\mathsf{Large}_{u,t}\mid\mathsf{Cell}_{u,t})
P(\mathsf{Bad}_{u,t}\mid\mathsf{Cell}_{u,t})
>0.
\]
On this intersection,
\[
L<|Y|\,|Z_{\jmath_{u,t}}|=|YZ_{\jmath_{u,t}}|,
\]
contradicting the product envelope.  Thus
\[
|Y|
\le
\frac{L}{\zeta_{u,t}}
=
\frac{2L\sqrt{d_z}}{\sigma_0}
=
\frac{L_\tau}{2}
\quad P\text{-a.s. conditional on }\mathsf{Cell}_{u,t}.
\]
On \(\{T=t\}\), the consistency component in \cref{obj:ass:consistency} gives \(Y=Y(t)\) almost surely.  Inside the conditional law given \(\mathsf{Class}_u\), the event \(\{T=t\}\) has positive probability, and the latent-ignorability component in \cref{obj:ass:latent-ignorability} gives \(Y(t)\perp T\mid U\).  If
\[
\left\{|Y(t)|>\frac{L_\tau}{2}\right\}
\]
had positive conditional probability given \(\mathsf{Class}_u\), independence would give a positive-probability violation on \(\mathsf{Cell}_{u,t}\), contradicting the displayed cell bound.  Hence
\[
|Y(t)|\le\frac{L_\tau}{2}
\quad P\text{-a.s. conditional on }U=u .
\]
Finally, \cref{obj:lem:latent-mean-envelope,obj:lem:latent-effect-envelope} give
\[
|\mu_{tu}|\le \frac{L_\tau}{2},
\qquad
|\tau_u|\le L_\tau .
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.ProxyEffectlawEigencollisionFrontier.ae_abs_potential_le_on_latentClass

\item It remains to identify membership in the published VMW parameter class.  The scope in \cref{obj:def:published-vmw-regimes}, together with the cited VMW correspondence recorded in \cref{obj:synth_11} and \citep{VirkMazaheriWu2026}, identifies \(\mathsf{Model}_{\mathrm{VMW}}(k,d_x,d_z,P)\) with the qualitative structural conditions summarized there.  Those conditions are supplied as follows.  The proxy independences, consistency, and armwise latent ignorability are components of \(\mathcal M\).  The proxy-rank margin and \(\sigma_0>0\) give full column rank:
\[
\sigma_k(A_0)\ge\sigma_0>0,\qquad
\sigma_k(A_1)\ge\sigma_0>0,\qquad
\sigma_k(B)\ge\sigma_0>0.
\]
Latent-arm positivity gives
\[
P(U=u,T=t)\ge\pi_0>0
\]
for every \(u\) and \(t\), and hence the corresponding latent treatment probabilities are strictly positive.  Together with \(2\le k\), \(k\le d_x\), and \(k\le d_z\), these are exactly the qualitative conditions for \(P\in\mathsf{Model}_{\mathrm{VMW}}(k,d_x,d_z)\).
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.ProxyEffectlawEigencollisionFrontier.observed_vmw_margin_inclusion
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:prop:summary-closure-compact}]
\begin{enumerate}
\item First record the coordinate estimate used to pass from operator envelopes to coordinate boxes.  For any \(H\in\mathbb R^{d_z\times d_x}\), any \(\iota_z\in\{1,\ldots,d_z\}\), and any \(\iota_x\in\{1,\ldots,d_x\}\),
\[
        |H_{\iota_z\iota_x}|
        =
        \|(H e_{\iota_x})_{\iota_z}\|
        \le
        \|H e_{\iota_x}\|_2
        \le
        \|H\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|e_{\iota_x}\|_2
        =
        \|H\|_{\mathrm{op}},
\]
where \(e_{\iota_x}\) is the \(\iota_x\)th canonical basis vector of \(\mathbb R^{d_x}\). % lean: matrixEntry_abs_le_operatorNorm

\item Define the finite coordinate boxes
\[
\mathcal H_{\mathrm{mat}}(L)
=
\Bigl\{H\in\mathbb R^{d_z\times d_x}:
        -L\le H_{\iota_z\iota_x}\le L
        \text{ for all }\iota_z,\iota_x\Bigr\},
\]
\[
\mathcal H_X(L)
=
\Bigl\{h\in\mathbb R^{d_x}:
        -L\le h_{\iota_x}\le L
        \text{ for all }\iota_x\Bigr\},
\]
and
\[
\mathcal H_S(L)
=
\mathcal H_{\mathrm{mat}}(L)^4\times \mathcal H_X(L).
\]
Each factor is a finite product of closed intervals, hence compact, and therefore \(\mathcal H_S(L)\) is compact.  For a five-block summary \(s=(M_0,M_1,N_0,N_1,m_X)\), define the coordinate map
\[
        \Phi(s)=(M_0,M_1,N_0,N_1,m_X).
\]
This map is a homeomorphism from the space of five-block summaries onto this finite product coordinate space.  Thus
\[
        \Phi^{-1}\bigl(\mathcal H_S(L)\bigr)
\]
is compact in the summary topology. % lean: summarySpaceBox_compact

\item Let \(P\in\mathcal M\).  For \(t\in\{0,1\}\), put
\[
        \mathsf{Arm}_t(P)=\{T=t\}.
\]
By \cref{obj:ass:latent-arm-positivity}, together with \(2\le k\) and \(0<\pi_0\), the arm \(\mathsf{Arm}_t(P)\) has positive mass.  Let \(P^{\mathrm{arm}}_t\) be the probability law obtained by restricting \(P\) to \(\mathsf{Arm}_t(P)\) and normalizing by this positive mass.  By \cref{obj:def:observable-population-moments}, the armwise blocks in \(S(P)\) are conditional means; on this positive arm, equivalently,
\[
        M_t(P)=\int ZX^\top\,dP^{\mathrm{arm}}_t,
        \qquad
        N_t(P)=\int YZX^\top\,dP^{\mathrm{arm}}_t,
\]
and \(m_X(P)=\int X\,dP\).  By \cref{obj:ass:bounded-proxy-product}, the proxy-product envelope restricts to \(P^{\mathrm{arm}}_t\): \(\|ZX^\top\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le L\) \(P^{\mathrm{arm}}_t\)-almost surely.  Since \(1\le L\), the matrix-valued integral is integrable and
\[
\begin{aligned}
\|M_t(P)\|_{\mathrm{op}}
&=\left\|\int ZX^\top\,dP^{\mathrm{arm}}_t\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}  \\
&\le \int \|ZX^\top\|_{\mathrm{op}}\,dP^{\mathrm{arm}}_t
 \le \int L\,dP^{\mathrm{arm}}_t
 = L .
\end{aligned}
\]
The same normalized-restriction argument with \cref{obj:ass:bounded-outcome-proxy-product} gives
\[
        \|N_t(P)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le L .
\]
Likewise, \cref{obj:ass:bounded-x} gives
\[
\|m_X(P)\|_2
=\left\|\int X\,dP\right\|_2
\le
\int \|X\|_2\,dP
\le L .
\]
Hence every coordinate of \(m_X(P)\) has absolute value at most \(L\).  Combining these inequalities with the coordinate estimate from the first step and the summary tuple in \cref{obj:def:observable-summary} gives
\[
        S(P)\in \Phi^{-1}\bigl(\mathcal H_S(L)\bigr).
\]
Since \(P\in\mathcal M\) was arbitrary,
\[
        \mathscr S\subseteq \Phi^{-1}\bigl(\mathcal H_S(L)\bigr).
\] % lean: admissibleImage_subset_summarySpaceBox

\item The same block envelopes give the stated uniform \(d_S\)-bound.  For \(s=S(P)\in\mathscr S\),
\[
\begin{aligned}
d_S(s,0)
&=
\|M_0(P)\|_{\mathrm{op}}
+\|M_1(P)\|_{\mathrm{op}}
+\|N_0(P)\|_{\mathrm{op}}
+\|N_1(P)\|_{\mathrm{op}}
+\Bigl(\sum_{\iota_x} m_X(P)_{\iota_x}^2\Bigr)^{1/2}.
\end{aligned}
\]
For the final term,
\[
\Bigl(\sum_{\iota_x} m_X(P)_{\iota_x}^2\Bigr)^{1/2}
=
\Bigl\|\sum_{\iota_x} m_X(P)_{\iota_x} e_{\iota_x}\Bigr\|_2
\le
\sum_{\iota_x}|m_X(P)_{\iota_x}|
\le
d_x L.
\]
Therefore
\[
        d_S(S(P),0)\le 4L+d_xL=(4+d_x)L.
\]
Taking the scalar bound
\[
        B_{\mathrm{sum}}=(4+d_x)L
\]
and then instantiating the existential bound with \(B_{\mathrm{sum}}\) proves the boundedness claim. % lean: admissibleImage_dS_bounded

\item Since \(\Phi^{-1}(\mathcal H_S(L))\) is compact and closed and contains \(\mathscr S\), it also contains
\[
        \mathcal K=\overline{\mathscr S}.
\]
The set \(\mathcal K\) is closed by definition as a closure, so it is a closed subset of the compact set \(\Phi^{-1}(\mathcal H_S(L))\).  Hence \(\mathcal K\) is compact. % lean: summaryClosure_compact_of_block_bounds

\item Finally,
\[
        \mathcal K\neq\varnothing
        \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
        \mathscr S\neq\varnothing,
\]
because a closure has the same nonemptiness as the set it closes.  By the definition
\[
        \mathscr S=\{S(P):P\in\mathcal M\},
\]
the condition \(\mathscr S\neq\varnothing\) is equivalent to \(\mathcal M\neq\varnothing\): a law \(P\in\mathcal M\) gives \(S(P)\in\mathscr S\), and any element of \(\mathscr S\) is \(S(P)\) for some \(P\in\mathcal M\).  Thus \(\mathcal K\) is nonempty if and only if \(\mathcal M\) is nonempty. % lean: summary_closure_compact
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:uniform-summary-concentration}]
Fix \(k,d_x,d_z,\pi_0,\sigma_0\) satisfying the displayed hypotheses.  Let
\(\mathcal I_{d_x,d_z}\) be the finite disjoint union of the following scalar
summary coordinates:
\[
\{\operatorname{prox},\operatorname{out}\}\times\{0,1\}
\times\{1,\ldots,d_z\}\times\{1,\ldots,d_x\},
\qquad
\operatorname{xcoord}\times\{1,\ldots,d_x\},
\qquad
\operatorname{arm}\times\{0,1\}.
\]
For \(O=(T,X,Z,Y)\in O_{d_x,d_z}\) from
\cref{obj:def:observed-record-space}, define
\[
\varphi_{\operatorname{prox},t,\mathfrak z,\mathfrak x}(O)
:=
\mathbf 1\{T=t\}Z_{\mathfrak z}X_{\mathfrak x},
\qquad
\varphi_{\operatorname{out},t,\mathfrak z,\mathfrak x}(O)
:=
\mathbf 1\{T=t\}YZ_{\mathfrak z}X_{\mathfrak x},
\]
\[
\varphi_{\operatorname{xcoord},\mathfrak x}(O):=X_{\mathfrak x},
\qquad
\varphi_{\operatorname{arm},t}(O):=\mathbf 1\{T=t\}.
\]
Set
\[
\varsigma_{\iota}(L):=
\begin{cases}
L, & \iota\text{ is a proxy, outcome, or target-proxy coordinate},\\
1, & \iota\text{ is an arm coordinate}.
\end{cases}
\]
The bounded target-proxy, bounded proxy-product, and bounded outcome-proxy
components of \cref{obj:def:model-class}, namely
\cref{obj:ass:bounded-x,obj:ass:bounded-proxy-product,obj:ass:bounded-outcome-proxy-product},
give, for every \(\iota\in\mathcal I_{d_x,d_z}\),
\[
|\varphi_\iota(O)|\le \varsigma_\iota(L)
\quad\text{for \(P_O\)-almost every }O,
\]
where \(P_O\) is the observed-data marginal of
\cref{obj:def:observed-margin}.  % lean: summaryCoordStat_ae_bound

Let \(\kappa_{d_z,d_x}\ge0\) be a finite coordinate-to-operator-norm constant
such that, for every \(d_z\times d_x\) real matrix \(H\) and every
\(u_{\mathrm{coord}}\ge0\),
\[
\max_{\mathfrak z,\mathfrak x}|H_{\mathfrak z\mathfrak x}|
\le u_{\mathrm{coord}}
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\|H\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le \kappa_{d_z,d_x}u_{\mathrm{coord}}.
\]
Such a constant exists because, if each coordinate of a vector in
\(\mathbb R^r\) has absolute value at most \(u_{\mathrm{coord}}\), then its
Euclidean norm is at most \(r u_{\mathrm{coord}}\); applying this to each
column and then taking the supremum over unit input vectors gives a finite
constant depending only on \(d_z,d_x\).  We shall also use the corresponding
vector estimate
\[
\max_{\mathfrak x}|v_{\mathfrak x}|\le u_{\mathrm{coord}}
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\|v\|_2\le d_xu_{\mathrm{coord}} .
\]  % lean: eucNorm_le_card_mul_bound, matrixNorm_le_entryBound

Put
\[
\chi_{\mathrm{sum}}:=|\mathcal I_{d_x,d_z}|=4d_zd_x+d_x+2,
\]
\[
K_{\mathrm{sm}}:=\frac{16\kappa_{d_z,d_x}}{k\pi_0}+d_x,
\qquad
K_{\mathrm{lg}}:=4(\kappa_{d_z,d_x}+1)+d_x+1,
\]
and choose
\[
C_0:=
\max\left\{\chi_{\mathrm{sum}}+1,\ 2K_{\mathrm{sm}},\
\frac{4K_{\mathrm{lg}}}{k\pi_0}\right\}.
\]
Then \(1\le C_0\).  Since \(2\le k\) and \(0<\pi_0\), the product
\(k\pi_0\) is positive, so all denominators in this definition are positive.  % lean: uniform_summary_concentration

Now fix \(L\ge1\), \(n\ge1\), \(P\in\mathcal M\), and
\(0<\eta<1/2\).  Define
\[
\zeta_{\eta,n}:=
\sqrt{\frac{\log(C_0/\eta)}{n}},
\qquad
\beta_{\eta,n}:=2\zeta_{\eta,n}.
\]
For each \(\iota\in\mathcal I_{d_x,d_z}\), Hoeffding's inequality under
\(Q_P^{(n)}=P_O^{\otimes n}\) gives
\[
Q_P^{(n)}
\left\{
\varsigma_\iota(L)\beta_{\eta,n}
\le
\left|
\frac1n\sum_{\mathfrak r=1}^n\varphi_\iota(O_{\mathfrak r})
-
\int \varphi_\iota\,dP_O
\right|
\right\}
\le
2\left(\frac{\eta}{C_0}\right)^2 .
\]
Indeed, \(\varphi_\iota\in[-\varsigma_\iota(L),\varsigma_\iota(L)]\)
\(P_O\)-almost surely, the interval length is \(2\varsigma_\iota(L)\), and the
choice \(\beta_{\eta,n}=2\sqrt{\log(C_0/\eta)/n}\) reduces the exponential
factor to \(\exp\{-2\log(C_0/\eta)\}\).  % lean: product_hoeffding, summaryCoord_deviation_probability

Let
\[
\mathcal B_{\mathrm{dev}}
:=
\left\{\omega:\exists\iota\in\mathcal I_{d_x,d_z},\
\varsigma_\iota(L)\beta_{\eta,n}
\le
\left|
\frac1n\sum_{\mathfrak r=1}^n\varphi_\iota(O_{\mathfrak r})
-
\int \varphi_\iota\,dP_O
\right|\right\}.
\]
By the union bound and \(\chi_{\mathrm{sum}}\le C_0\),
\[
Q_P^{(n)}(\mathcal B_{\mathrm{dev}})
\le
\chi_{\mathrm{sum}}\,2\left(\frac{\eta}{C_0}\right)^2
\le
\frac{2\eta^2}{C_0}
\le
2\eta^2
\le
\eta .
\]
Also set
\[
\mathcal B_{\mathrm{supp}}
:=
\left\{\omega:\exists \mathfrak r,\exists\iota\in\mathcal I_{d_x,d_z},\
|\varphi_\iota(O_{\mathfrak r})|>\varsigma_\iota(L)\right\}.
\]
The \(P_O\)-almost-sure coordinate envelope transported to the product
experiment gives
\[
Q_P^{(n)}(\mathcal B_{\mathrm{supp}})=0 .
\]  % lean: measureReal_iUnion_fintype_le, sample_summaryCoordSupport_ae

The population summary blocks obey
\[
\|M_t(P)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le L,\qquad
\|N_t(P)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le L,\qquad
\|m_X(P)\|_2\le L .
\]
For the armwise blocks, \cref{obj:lem:arm-mass-lower-bound} gives positive
arm probabilities, and Jensen's inequality applied to the conditional moments
in \cref{obj:def:observable-population-moments} transfers the almost-sure
bounds on \(ZX^\top\) and \(YZX^\top\).  For \(m_X(P)\), Jensen's inequality
applies directly to the unconditional target-proxy mean.  % lean: observedSummary_envelopes_of_model

It remains to prove a deterministic implication off the two bad events.  Write
\[
\theta_t:=P_O\{O:T=t\},
\qquad
\widehat\theta_t(\omega):=\frac{N_{t,n}(\omega)}{n}.
\]
By \cref{obj:lem:arm-mass-lower-bound}, transported through
\cref{obj:def:observed-margin},
\[
k\pi_0\le \theta_t
\qquad(t\in\{0,1\}).
\]
First suppose that
\[
\beta_{\eta,n}<\frac{k\pi_0}{2}.
\]
On \(\mathcal B_{\mathrm{dev}}^c\),
\[
|\widehat\theta_t-\theta_t|<\beta_{\eta,n},
\qquad
\widehat\theta_t>\frac{k\pi_0}{2}.
\]
For \(\bullet\in\{\operatorname{prox},\operatorname{out}\}\), put
\[
\overline\Phi^\bullet_{t,\mathfrak z,\mathfrak x}(\omega)
:=
\frac1n\sum_{\mathfrak r=1}^n
\varphi_{\bullet,t,\mathfrak z,\mathfrak x}(O_{\mathfrak r}).
\]
The coordinate integral identities are
\[
\int\varphi_{\operatorname{prox},t,\mathfrak z,\mathfrak x}\,dP_O
=
\theta_t\,M_t(P)_{\mathfrak z\mathfrak x},
\qquad
\int\varphi_{\operatorname{out},t,\mathfrak z,\mathfrak x}\,dP_O
=
\theta_t\,N_t(P)_{\mathfrak z\mathfrak x}.
\]
Because \(\widehat\theta_t>0\), the total empty-arm convention in
\cref{obj:def:empirical-summary-primitives} gives
\[
(\widehat M_{t,n}-M_t(P))_{\mathfrak z\mathfrak x}
=
\frac{
\{\overline\Phi^{\operatorname{prox}}_{t,\mathfrak z,\mathfrak x}
-\theta_tM_t(P)_{\mathfrak z\mathfrak x}\}
+
(\theta_t-\widehat\theta_t)M_t(P)_{\mathfrak z\mathfrak x}
}{\widehat\theta_t},
\]
and the same identity with \(\widehat N_{t,n},N_t(P)\) and
\(\overline\Phi^{\operatorname{out}}\).  Since each population entry is bounded
by \(L\), the preceding displays imply, for both matrix types,
\[
\left|
(\widehat M_{t,n}-M_t(P))_{\mathfrak z\mathfrak x}
\right|
\le
\frac{4L\beta_{\eta,n}}{k\pi_0},
\qquad
\left|
(\widehat N_{t,n}-N_t(P))_{\mathfrak z\mathfrak x}
\right|
\le
\frac{4L\beta_{\eta,n}}{k\pi_0}.
\]
The matrix norm conversion and the mean-coordinate bounds
\[
|(\widehat m_{X,n}-m_X(P))_{\mathfrak x}|<L\beta_{\eta,n}
\]
therefore yield
\[
d_S(\widehat S_n,S(P))
\le
\left(\frac{16\kappa_{d_z,d_x}}{k\pi_0}+d_x\right)L\beta_{\eta,n}
=
2K_{\mathrm{sm}}L\zeta_{\eta,n}
\le
C_0L\zeta_{\eta,n}.
\]  % lean: summaryCoordStat_arm_sampleMean, summaryCoordStat_integral, empiricalArmMatrix_entry_error, empSummary_error_of_small_deviations

Now suppose that
\[
\frac{k\pi_0}{2}\le \beta_{\eta,n}.
\]
On \(\mathcal B_{\mathrm{supp}}^c\), every empirical arm matrix has entries
bounded by \(L\): if \(N_{t,n}=0\), the convention in
\cref{obj:def:empirical-summary-primitives} gives the zero matrix, and if
\(N_{t,n}>0\), the matrix is an average of entries whose absolute values are at
most \(L\).  Hence each empirical matrix block has operator norm at most
\(\kappa_{d_z,d_x}L\), and
\[
\|\widehat m_{X,n}\|_2\le d_xL .
\]
Combining these empirical bounds with the population envelopes gives
\[
d_S(\widehat S_n,S(P))
\le
\{4(\kappa_{d_z,d_x}+1)+d_x+1\}L
=
K_{\mathrm{lg}}L .
\]
Since \(k\pi_0/2\le\beta_{\eta,n}=2\zeta_{\eta,n}\),
\[
\frac{k\pi_0}{4}\le \zeta_{\eta,n},
\]
and therefore
\[
K_{\mathrm{lg}}L
\le
\frac{4K_{\mathrm{lg}}}{k\pi_0}L\zeta_{\eta,n}
\le
C_0L\zeta_{\eta,n}.
\]  % lean: abs_fin_average_le, empiricalArmMatrix_entry_bound, empSummary_error_on_support

The two cases imply
\[
\left\{
d_S(\widehat S_n,S(P))>
C_0L\sqrt{\frac{\log(C_0/\eta)}{n}}
\right\}
\subseteq
\mathcal B_{\mathrm{dev}}\cup\mathcal B_{\mathrm{supp}}.
\]
Consequently,
\[
Q_P^{(n)}
\left\{
d_S(\widehat S_n,S(P))>
C_0L\sqrt{\frac{\log(C_0/\eta)}{n}}
\right\}
\le
Q_P^{(n)}(\mathcal B_{\mathrm{dev}})
+
Q_P^{(n)}(\mathcal B_{\mathrm{supp}})
\le
\eta .
\]  % lean: uniform_summary_concentration

Finally fix \(t\in\{0,1\}\).  The event \(N_{t,n}=0\) is the event that all
\(n\) observed records have \(T\ne t\).  Since
\(Q_P^{(n)}=P_O^{\otimes n}\),
\[
Q_P^{(n)}\{N_{t,n}=0\}
=
\bigl(P_O\{O:T\ne t\}\bigr)^n
=
\bigl(1-P_O\{O:T=t\}\bigr)^n .
\]
The lower bound from \cref{obj:lem:arm-mass-lower-bound}, again through
\cref{obj:def:observed-margin}, gives
\[
k\pi_0\le P_O\{O:T=t\}\le 1.
\]
Thus
\[
0\le 1-P_O\{O:T=t\}\le 1-k\pi_0,
\]
and monotonicity of \(x\mapsto x^n\) on \([0,\infty)\) gives
\[
\bigl(1-P_O\{O:T=t\}\bigr)^n
\le
(1-k\pi_0)^n .
\]
This proves both asserted conclusions.  % lean: uniform_summary_concentration, arm_mass_lower_of_latentArmPositivity
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:thm:gap-free-positive-measure-modulus}]
\begin{enumerate}
\item Put
\[
s_\star:=\pi_0\sigma_0^2,
\qquad
L_\tau:=\frac{4L\sqrt{d_z}}{\sigma_0}.
\]
For matrix sizes \(r_{\mathrm{row}},r_{\mathrm{col}}\), write
\[
\operatorname{Ent}_{r_{\mathrm{row}},r_{\mathrm{col}}}
:=
r_{\mathrm{col}}r_{\mathrm{row}}\,
\|J_{r_{\mathrm{col}}}\|_{\mathrm{op}},
\]
with \(J_{r_{\mathrm{col}}}\) as in \cref{obj:lem:summary-metric-coordinate-comparison}.  Define
\[
\begin{aligned}
K_{\mathrm{forw}}
&:=
\operatorname{Ent}_{d_x,k}\operatorname{Ent}_{k,k}
\operatorname{Ent}_{k,d_x}\,\sigma_0^{-1}
+\operatorname{Ent}_{d_x,d_x}
+\operatorname{Ent}_{d_x,k}\operatorname{Ent}_{k,d_x},\\
K_{\mathrm{back}}
&:=
\operatorname{Ent}_{d_x,k}\operatorname{Ent}_{k,k}
\operatorname{Ent}_{k,d_x}\,(d_xL)
+\operatorname{Ent}_{d_x,d_x}
+\operatorname{Ent}_{d_x,k}\operatorname{Ent}_{k,d_x},
\end{aligned}
\]
and
\[
\kappa_\star
:=
\operatorname{Ent}_{d_x,d_x}^{\,2}K_{\mathrm{forw}}K_{\mathrm{back}} .
\]
The assumptions \(L\ge1\) and \(\sigma_0>0\), together with nonnegativity of the entry-norm constants, give
\[
0\le \kappa_\star .
\]
Set
\[
C_{\mathrm{base}}
:=
\kappa_\star L_\tau
+
L\,d_x^2\kappa_\star^2
\{3s_\star^{-2}L+s_\star^{-1}\},
\qquad
C_{\mathrm{mod}}:=C_{\mathrm{base}}+1 .
\]
Since \(s_\star>0\), \(L_\tau\ge0\), and \(\kappa_\star\ge0\), we have
\[
0\le C_{\mathrm{base}},
\qquad
0<C_{\mathrm{mod}} .
\]
% lean: modelRealDiagonalization_conditionBound_nonneg

\item Fix \(P\in\mathcal M\).  Let \(B(P)\) be the target-proxy feature matrix of \cref{obj:def:target-feature}.  The proxy-rank margin gives
\[
\sigma_0\le \sigma_k(B(P)).
\]
Choose a thin singular-value factorization
\[
B(P)=V_P\Upsilon_P,
\qquad
V_P^\top V_P=I_k,
\qquad
\Upsilon_P^{-1}\Upsilon_P=\Upsilon_P\Upsilon_P^{-1}=I_k .
\]
The singular-value lower bound implies
\[
|(\Upsilon_P^{-1})_{ab}|\le \sigma_0^{-1}
\qquad(1\le a,b\le k).
\]
For every coordinate \(i\) and latent class \(u\), latent-arm positivity gives
\[
0<P(U=u,T=0)\le P(U=u).
\]
Under the normalized restriction of \(P\) to \(\{U=u\}\), \cref{obj:lem:proxy-coordinate-envelopes} gives
\[
|X_i|\le L\quad\text{almost surely}.
\]
Therefore
\[
\begin{aligned}
|B(P)_{iu}|
&=\left|E_P[X_i\mid U=u]\right|\\
&=\left|\int X_i\,dP(\,\cdot\mid U=u)\right|\\
&\le \int |X_i|\,dP(\,\cdot\mid U=u)
\le L .
\end{aligned}
\]
Thus \cref{obj:lem:diagonalization-condition-number-bound} applies to \(B(P)=V_P\Upsilon_P\).

Define the ambient contrast
\[
\mathfrak D_P
:=
M_1(P)^\dagger N_1(P)-M_0(P)^\dagger N_0(P).
\]
By \cref{obj:lem:observed-proxy-moment-factorization,obj:lem:observed-outcome-proxy-moment-factorization}, for each \(t\in\{0,1\}\),
\[
M_t(P)=A_t(P)\Gamma_t(P)B(P)^\top,
\qquad
N_t(P)=A_t(P)\Gamma_t(P)
\operatorname{diag}\{\mu_{tu}(P)\}_{u=1}^kB(P)^\top ,
\]
where
\[
\Gamma_t(P)=
\operatorname{diag}\left\{
\frac{P(U=u,T=t)}{P(T=t)}:1\le u\le k
\right\}.
\]
The proxy-rank margin and \cref{obj:lem:latent-arm-weight-invertibility} make
\(A_t(P)\Gamma_t(P)\) injective, and the proxy-rank margin makes \(B(P)\) injective.  Hence
\cref{obj:lem:moore-penrose-product-cancellation} yields
\[
M_t(P)^\dagger N_t(P)
=
(B(P)^\dagger)^\top
\operatorname{diag}\{\mu_{tu}(P)\}_{u=1}^kB(P)^\top .
\]
Subtracting the two arms gives
\[
\mathfrak D_P
=
(B(P)^\dagger)^\top
\operatorname{diag}\{\tau_u(P)\}_{u=1}^kB(P)^\top .
\]

Let
\[
\mathsf{Forw}_P
:=
V_P(\Upsilon_P^{-1})^\top V_P^\top+(I_{d_x}-V_PV_P^\top),
\qquad
\mathsf{Back}_P
:=
V_P\Upsilon_P^\top V_P^\top+(I_{d_x}-V_PV_P^\top).
\]
Then
\[
\mathsf{Forw}_P\mathsf{Back}_P
=
\mathsf{Back}_P\mathsf{Forw}_P
=
I_{d_x}.
\]
Extending the columns of \(V_P\) to an orthonormal basis of \(\mathbb R^{d_x}\), the columns obtained by applying \(\mathsf{Forw}_P\) form a real eigenbasis for \(\mathfrak D_P\).  The signal eigenvalues are \(\tau_u(P)\), and the remaining eigenvalues are \(0\).  Consequently
\begin{equation}
\mathfrak D_P
=
\mathcal S_P\operatorname{diag}(\alpha_{P,1},\ldots,\alpha_{P,d_x})
\mathcal S_P^{-1},
\label{eq:gap-free-model-diagonalization}
\end{equation}
with
\[
\|\mathcal S_P\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|\mathcal S_P^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
\le \kappa_\star,
\qquad
|\alpha_{P,j}|\le L_\tau .
\]
The eigenvalue bound uses \cref{obj:lem:latent-effect-envelope} on the signal coordinates and \(0\in[-L_\tau,L_\tau]\) on the remaining coordinates.

It remains to record the two anchor identities used by the spectral representation.  For each coordinate \(j\),
\[
\begin{aligned}
m_X(P)_j
&=\int X_j\,dP\\
&=\sum_{u=1}^k\int_{\{U=u\}}X_j\,dP\\
&=\sum_{u=1}^k E_P[X_j\mid U=u]\,P(U=u)\\
&=\sum_{u=1}^k B(P)_{ju}p_u(P),
\end{aligned}
\]
where the latent classes form a finite measurable partition and \(X_j\) is integrable by \cref{obj:lem:proxy-coordinate-envelopes}.  Hence
\[
m_X(P)=B(P)p(P).
\]
Also, since \(X_1=1\) almost surely and \(P(U=u)>0\),
\[
B(P)_{1u}=E_P[X_1\mid U=u]=1
\qquad(1\le u\le k),
\]
so the first canonical basis vector \(e_1\in\mathbb R^{d_x}\) satisfies
\[
B(P)^\top e_1=\mathbf 1_k .
\]
Therefore, for every one-Lipschitz \(\varphi:\mathbb R\to\mathbb R\) satisfying
\(\varphi(0)=0\),
\[
\varphi(\mathfrak D_P)
=
(B(P)^\dagger)^\top
\operatorname{diag}\{\varphi(\tau_u(P))\}_{u=1}^kB(P)^\top
\]
and
\[
\int \varphi\,d\nu_P
=
\sum_{u=1}^kp_u(P)\varphi(\tau_u(P))
=
\left\langle m_X(P),\varphi(\mathfrak D_P)e_1\right\rangle .
\]
% lean: model_realDiagonalization_certificate

\item Fix \(P,Q\in\mathcal M\).  For each arm \(t\), \cref{obj:prop:observed-vmw-margin-inclusion} supplies
\[
s_\star\le\sigma_k(M_t(P)),
\qquad
s_\star\le\sigma_k(M_t(Q)),
\qquad
\|N_t(P)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le L .
\]
Together with the proxy factorization, these inequalities give
\[
\operatorname{rank}M_t(P)=\operatorname{rank}M_t(Q)=k .
\]
We first record the armwise perturbation estimate.  Let \(\mathsf H,\mathsf H'\in\mathbb R^{d_z\times d_x}\) have the same rank \(k\), and suppose
\[
s_\star\le \sigma_k(\mathsf H),
\qquad
s_\star\le \sigma_k(\mathsf H') .
\]
Then
\[
\|\mathsf H^\dagger\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le s_\star^{-1},
\qquad
\|\mathsf H'{}^\dagger\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le s_\star^{-1},
\]
because the operator norm of the Moore--Penrose inverse is the reciprocal of the smallest nonzero singular value.  The Penrose equations, applied with the common rank condition, give the exact moving-space identity
\[
\begin{aligned}
\mathsf H^\dagger-\mathsf H'{}^\dagger
={}&-\mathsf H^\dagger(\mathsf H-\mathsf H')\mathsf H'{}^\dagger
+\mathsf H^\dagger(\mathsf H^\dagger)^\top
  (\mathsf H-\mathsf H')^\top(I-\mathsf H'\mathsf H'{}^\dagger)\\
&+(I-\mathsf H^\dagger\mathsf H)(\mathsf H-\mathsf H')^\top
  (\mathsf H'{}^\dagger)^\top\mathsf H'{}^\dagger .
\end{aligned}
\]
Here \(I-\mathsf H'\mathsf H'{}^\dagger\) and \(I-\mathsf H^\dagger\mathsf H\) are orthogonal projection residuals, so their operator norms are at most one.  Taking norms term by term gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
\|\mathsf H^\dagger(\mathsf H-\mathsf H')\mathsf H'{}^\dagger\|_{\mathrm{op}}
&\le s_\star^{-2}\|\mathsf H-\mathsf H'\|_{\mathrm{op}},\\
\|\mathsf H^\dagger(\mathsf H^\dagger)^\top
  (\mathsf H-\mathsf H')^\top(I-\mathsf H'\mathsf H'{}^\dagger)\|_{\mathrm{op}}
&\le s_\star^{-2}\|\mathsf H-\mathsf H'\|_{\mathrm{op}},\\
\|(I-\mathsf H^\dagger\mathsf H)(\mathsf H-\mathsf H')^\top
  (\mathsf H'{}^\dagger)^\top\mathsf H'{}^\dagger\|_{\mathrm{op}}
&\le s_\star^{-2}\|\mathsf H-\mathsf H'\|_{\mathrm{op}}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Hence
\[
\|\mathsf H^\dagger-\mathsf H'{}^\dagger\|_{\mathrm{op}}
\le
3s_\star^{-2}\|\mathsf H-\mathsf H'\|_{\mathrm{op}} .
\]
For matrices \(\mathsf N,\mathsf N'\in\mathbb R^{d_z\times d_x}\), the algebraic split
\[
\mathsf H^\dagger\mathsf N-\mathsf H'{}^\dagger\mathsf N'
=
(\mathsf H^\dagger-\mathsf H'{}^\dagger)\mathsf N
+\mathsf H'{}^\dagger(\mathsf N-\mathsf N')
\]
therefore implies, whenever \(\|\mathsf N\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le L\),
\[
\|\mathsf H^\dagger\mathsf N-\mathsf H'{}^\dagger\mathsf N'\|_{\mathrm{op}}
\le
3s_\star^{-2}L\,\|\mathsf H-\mathsf H'\|_{\mathrm{op}}
+s_\star^{-1}\|\mathsf N-\mathsf N'\|_{\mathrm{op}} .
\]
Applying this estimate with
\[
\mathsf H=M_t(P),\quad \mathsf H'=M_t(Q),\quad
\mathsf N=N_t(P),\quad \mathsf N'=N_t(Q)
\]
gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\|M_t(P)^\dagger N_t(P)-M_t(Q)^\dagger N_t(Q)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\\
&\qquad\le
3s_\star^{-2}L\,\|M_t(P)-M_t(Q)\|_{\mathrm{op}}
+s_\star^{-1}\|N_t(P)-N_t(Q)\|_{\mathrm{op}} .
\end{aligned}
\]
Summing over \(t=0,1\) and using the definition of \(d_S\) yields
\[
\|\mathfrak D_P-\mathfrak D_Q\|_{\mathrm{op}}
\le
\{3s_\star^{-2}L+s_\star^{-1}\}\,
d_S(S(P),S(Q)).
\]
Moreover,
\[
\|m_X(P)-m_X(Q)\|_2\le d_S(S(P),S(Q)),
\qquad
\|m_X(Q)\|_2\le L,
\qquad
\|e_1\|_2=1 .
\]
% lean: ambientOperatorBridge_norm_ambientEffectOperator_sub_le

\item Let \(\varphi\) be the normalized attaining Kantorovich--Rubinstein potential for
\((\nu_P,\nu_Q)\) from \cref{obj:lem:kantorovich-rubinstein-attaining-potential}.  Then
\[
\varphi(0)=0,
\qquad
\operatorname{Lip}(\varphi)\le1,
\qquad
W_1(\nu_P,\nu_Q)
=
\left|
\int\varphi\,d\nu_P-\int\varphi\,d\nu_Q
\right|.
\]
Use the diagonalizations in \cref{eq:gap-free-model-diagonalization} for \(P\) and \(Q\), written
\[
\mathfrak D_P
=
\mathcal S_P\operatorname{diag}(\alpha_{P,1},\ldots,\alpha_{P,d_x})\mathcal S_P^{-1},
\qquad
\mathfrak D_Q
=
\mathcal S_Q\operatorname{diag}(\alpha_{Q,1},\ldots,\alpha_{Q,d_x})\mathcal S_Q^{-1}.
\]
Let
\[
\Omega_P:=\{\alpha_{P,j}:1\le j\le d_x\},
\qquad
\Omega_Q:=\{\alpha_{Q,j}:1\le j\le d_x\}.
\]
For any real number \(\zeta\), define the aggregate projectors
\[
\mathsf{Proj}_P(\zeta)
:=
\mathcal S_P\operatorname{diag}\{\mathbf 1(\alpha_{P,j}=\zeta):1\le j\le d_x\}\mathcal S_P^{-1},
\]
and
\[
\mathsf{Proj}_Q(\zeta)
:=
\mathcal S_Q\operatorname{diag}\{\mathbf 1(\alpha_{Q,j}=\zeta):1\le j\le d_x\}\mathcal S_Q^{-1}.
\]
The diagonal \(0\)-\(1\) matrices have operator norm at most one, so for
\(\zeta_P\in\Omega_P\) and \(\zeta_Q\in\Omega_Q\),
\[
\|\mathsf{Proj}_P(\zeta_P)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le\kappa_\star,
\qquad
\|\mathsf{Proj}_Q(\zeta_Q)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le\kappa_\star.
\]
The projectors extract their spectral values on the appropriate side:
\[
\mathsf{Proj}_P(\zeta_P)\mathfrak D_P
=
\zeta_P\mathsf{Proj}_P(\zeta_P),
\qquad
\mathfrak D_Q\mathsf{Proj}_Q(\zeta_Q)
=
\zeta_Q\mathsf{Proj}_Q(\zeta_Q).
\]
Hence
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\mathsf{Proj}_P(\zeta_P)(\mathfrak D_P-\mathfrak D_Q)\mathsf{Proj}_Q(\zeta_Q)\\
&\qquad=
(\zeta_P-\zeta_Q)\mathsf{Proj}_P(\zeta_P)\mathsf{Proj}_Q(\zeta_Q).
\end{aligned}
\]
In particular, when \(\zeta_P=\zeta_Q\),
\[
\mathsf{Proj}_P(\zeta_P)(\mathfrak D_P-\mathfrak D_Q)\mathsf{Proj}_Q(\zeta_Q)=0.
\]
Functional calculus with the two diagonalizations gives
\[
\varphi(\mathfrak D_P)
=
\sum_{\zeta_P\in\Omega_P}\varphi(\zeta_P)\mathsf{Proj}_P(\zeta_P),
\qquad
\varphi(\mathfrak D_Q)
=
\sum_{\zeta_Q\in\Omega_Q}\varphi(\zeta_Q)\mathsf{Proj}_Q(\zeta_Q).
\]
Since the aggregate projectors sum to the identity for each diagonalization, subtraction and expansion over
\(\Omega_P\times\Omega_Q\) yield
\[
\varphi(\mathfrak D_P)-\varphi(\mathfrak D_Q)
=
\sum_{\zeta_P\in\Omega_P}\sum_{\zeta_Q\in\Omega_Q}
\Xi(\zeta_P,\zeta_Q),
\]
where
\[
\Xi(\zeta_P,\zeta_Q)
:=
\begin{cases}
0,
&\zeta_P=\zeta_Q,\\[2mm]
\dfrac{\varphi(\zeta_P)-\varphi(\zeta_Q)}{\zeta_P-\zeta_Q}\,
\mathsf{Proj}_P(\zeta_P)(\mathfrak D_P-\mathfrak D_Q)\mathsf{Proj}_Q(\zeta_Q),
&\zeta_P\ne\zeta_Q .
\end{cases}
\]
The equal-spectral-value branch is zero by the preceding vanishing identity.  In the unequal branch, the one-Lipschitz property gives
\[
\left|
\frac{\varphi(\zeta_P)-\varphi(\zeta_Q)}{\zeta_P-\zeta_Q}
\right|\le1,
\]
and therefore
\[
\|\Xi(\zeta_P,\zeta_Q)\|_{\mathrm{op}}
\le
\kappa_\star^2\|\mathfrak D_P-\mathfrak D_Q\|_{\mathrm{op}}.
\]
Because \(|\Omega_P|\le d_x\) and \(|\Omega_Q|\le d_x\), the triangle inequality gives
\[
\|\varphi(\mathfrak D_P)-\varphi(\mathfrak D_Q)\|_{\mathrm{op}}
\le
d_x^2\kappa_\star^2
\|\mathfrak D_P-\mathfrak D_Q\|_{\mathrm{op}}.
\]
Also,
\[
\|\varphi(\mathfrak D_P)\|_{\mathrm{op}}
\le \kappa_\star L_\tau,
\]
because \(\varphi(0)=0\), \(\varphi\) is one-Lipschitz, and every eigenvalue of \(\mathfrak D_P\) lies in \([-L_\tau,L_\tau]\).

Using the representation identity for \(P\) and \(Q\),
\[
\begin{aligned}
W_1(\nu_P,\nu_Q)
&\le
\|m_X(P)-m_X(Q)\|_2\,\|\varphi(\mathfrak D_P)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|e_1\|_2\\
&\quad+
\|m_X(Q)\|_2\,
\|\varphi(\mathfrak D_P)-\varphi(\mathfrak D_Q)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|e_1\|_2\\
&\le
C_{\mathrm{base}}\,d_S(S(P),S(Q))
\le
C_{\mathrm{mod}}\,d_S(S(P),S(Q)).
\end{aligned}
\]
This proves the asserted modulus on model summaries:
\begin{equation}
W_1(\nu_P,\nu_Q)
\le C_{\mathrm{mod}}d_S(S(P),S(Q)).
\label{eq:gap-free-model-summary-control}
\end{equation}
% lean: ambientOperatorBridge_modelLaw_wass1_le_dS_of_certificates

\item Let
\[
\mathscr S:=\{S(P):P\in\mathcal M\}.
\]
For \(q\in\mathscr S\), choose a representative law \(P_q\in\mathcal M\) with
\[
S(P_q)=q,
\]
and define
\[
F_{\mathscr S}(q):=\nu_{P_q}.
\]
This definition is independent of the representative.  Indeed, if another \(Q\in\mathcal M\) has \(S(Q)=q\), then the modulus just proved gives
\[
0\le W_1(\nu_{P_q},\nu_Q)
\le C_{\mathrm{mod}}\,d_S(q,q)=0.
\]
A zero finite transport cost between quotient atomic laws forces the same aggregated mass at each support point, so the two quotient laws are equal.  Hence
\[
F_{\mathscr S}(S(Q))=\nu_Q
\qquad(Q\in\mathcal M),
\]
and, for all \(q,q'\in\mathscr S\),
\[
W_1(F_{\mathscr S}(q),F_{\mathscr S}(q'))
\le
C_{\mathrm{mod}}\,d_S(q,q').
\]
% lean: gap_free_positive_measure_modulus

\item Let \(\rho_{\mathrm{sum}}\) be the ambient product metric on the five summary blocks: each matrix block has its Frobenius norm, the mean block has its Euclidean norm, and the finite product uses the maximum of the five block distances.  Set
\[
\Lambda_{\mathrm{sum}}:=4\operatorname{Ent}_{d_z,d_x}+d_x .
\]
By \cref{obj:lem:summary-metric-coordinate-comparison},
\[
d_S(q,q')
\le
\Lambda_{\mathrm{sum}}\rho_{\mathrm{sum}}(q,q')
\qquad\text{for all summaries }q,q'.
\]
For a matrix block \(A\in\mathbb R^{d_z\times d_x}\), each entry obeys
\[
|A_{ab}|\le\|A\|_{\mathrm{op}},
\]
so summing the squared entries gives
\[
\|A\|_{\mathrm F}\le\sqrt{d_zd_x}\,\|A\|_{\mathrm{op}}.
\]
Since \(\rho_{\mathrm{sum}}\) is the maximum of the four Frobenius block distances and the Euclidean mean-block distance, while \(d_S\) is the sum of the four operator-norm distances and that same mean-block distance,
\[
\rho_{\mathrm{sum}}(q,q')
\le
\max\{1,\sqrt{d_zd_x}\}\,d_S(q,q')
\qquad\text{for all summaries }q,q'.
\]
(The assumptions \(2\le k\le d_x,d_z\) cover the positive-dimensional case used here; in a zero-dimensional matrix block the unique empty matrix contributes zero.)  Thus \(d_S\) and \(\rho_{\mathrm{sum}}\) induce the same topology and the same closures.

The first comparison and the \(d_S\)-control on \(F_{\mathscr S}\) give
\[
W_1(F_{\mathscr S}(q),F_{\mathscr S}(q'))
\le
C_{\mathrm{mod}}\Lambda_{\mathrm{sum}}\rho_{\mathrm{sum}}(q,q')
\qquad(q,q'\in\mathscr S).
\]
Hence \(F_{\mathscr S}\) is Lipschitz on the summary image in the ambient metric.

Inside \(\mathcal K=\overline{\mathscr S}\), put
\[
\mathscr S_{\mathcal K}:=\{q\in\mathcal K:q\in\mathscr S\}.
\]
The equality of closures just proved makes \(\mathscr S_{\mathcal K}\) dense in \(\mathcal K\).  The quotient atomic-law space \(\mathcal P_k([-L_\tau,L_\tau])\) is complete for \(W_1\): in labelled coordinates it is the metric quotient of the compact set
\[
\Delta_{k-1}\times[-L_\tau,L_\tau]^k.
\]
Apply the dense-set extension theorem to the ambient-Lipschitz map
\[
\widetilde F_{\mathscr S}(q):=F_{\mathscr S}(q),
\qquad q\in\mathscr S_{\mathcal K}.
\]
The dense-set extension construction gives a Lipschitz, hence continuous, map
\[
\overline F:\mathcal K\to\mathcal P_k([-L_\tau,L_\tau])
\]
that agrees with \(F_{\mathscr S}\) on \(\mathscr S\), and the uniqueness property of this construction makes it the unique continuous extension with that agreement.

It remains to transfer the sharper \(d_S\)-control.  Fix \(q,q'\in\mathcal K\).  Choose sequences \(q_m,q'_m\in\mathscr S\) converging in the ambient metric to \(q,q'\), respectively.  Continuity of \(\overline F\) and \cref{obj:lem:summary-metric-continuity} give
\[
W_1(\overline F(q_m),\overline F(q'_m))
\longrightarrow
W_1(\overline F(q),\overline F(q')),
\qquad
d_S(q_m,q'_m)\longrightarrow d_S(q,q').
\]
For every \(m\), agreement on \(\mathscr S\) and the model-summary control in \cref{eq:gap-free-model-summary-control} yield
\[
W_1(\overline F(q_m),\overline F(q'_m))
=
W_1(F_{\mathscr S}(q_m),F_{\mathscr S}(q'_m))
\le
C_{\mathrm{mod}}d_S(q_m,q'_m).
\]
Passing to the limit proves
\[
W_1(\overline F(q),\overline F(q'))
\le
C_{\mathrm{mod}}d_S(q,q')
\qquad(q,q'\in\mathcal K).
\]
Continuity of \(\overline F\), together with the Borel structure on the quotient atomic-law space, makes \(\overline F\) Borel measurable.
% lean: gapFreeClosureAssembly_exists_unique_extension_with_control

\item For every \(Q\in\mathcal M\), the summary \(S(Q)\) belongs to \(\mathscr S\), and hence
\[
\overline F(S(Q))
=
F_{\mathscr S}(S(Q))
=
\nu_Q .
\]
Let
\[
G:\mathcal K\to\mathcal P_k([-L_\tau,L_\tau])
\]
be continuous, satisfy the stated \(C_{\mathrm{mod}}\)-Lipschitz bound, and agree with \(Q\mapsto\nu_Q\) on all model summaries.  For any \(q\in\mathcal K\), choose \(q_m\in\mathscr S\) with
\[
q_m\to q .
\]
Continuity and agreement on \(\mathscr S\) imply
\[
G(q)
=
\lim_m G(q_m)
=
\lim_m F_{\mathscr S}(q_m)
=
\overline F(q).
\]
Thus \(G(q)=\overline F(q)\) for all \(q\in\mathcal K\).  Combining the model-summary modulus, the continuous extension, the Borel measurability, and this uniqueness proves the theorem.
% lean: gap_free_positive_measure_modulus
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:prop:summary-repair-total-borel}]
\begin{enumerate}
\item Write
\[
\mathcal K=\overline{\mathscr S}^{\,d_S}
       =\operatorname{cl}_{d_S}\{S(P):P\in\mathcal M\}.
\]
By \cref{obj:prop:summary-closure-compact}, \(\mathcal K\) is compact.  Also \(2\le k\) gives \(0<k\), and \cref{obj:def:effect-radius} with \(L\ge 1\) and \(0<\sigma_0\le 1\) gives \(L_\tau\ge 0\).  These two inequalities supply the positivity data needed for the atomic-law fallback \(\delta_0\).  From \cref{obj:thm:gap-free-positive-measure-modulus} we take a continuous, hence Borel, extension
\[
\overline F:\mathcal K\longrightarrow \mathcal P_k([-L_\tau,L_\tau])
\]
satisfying, for every admissible model law \(P\),
\[
\overline F(S(P))=\nu_P .
\]
\hfill{}% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.ProxyEffectlawEigencollisionFrontier.summary_repair_total_borel

\item First suppose \(\mathcal K=\varnothing\).  Define the selector on the full five-block summary space by
\[
\Pi(s)=0\qquad\text{for every summary }s .
\]
This map is Borel.  The extension condition over model summaries is vacuous, because any admissible \(P\) would give
\[
S(P)\in\mathscr S\subseteq \mathcal K,
\]
contradicting \(\mathcal K=\varnothing\).  The nonempty-\(\mathcal K\) nearest-point clause in \cref{obj:def:summary-space-repair} likewise has a false premise.  Thus these choices define admissible repair data \(R\).  For every observed sample \(\mathsf o_{1:n}\in O_{d_x,d_z}^n\), \cref{obj:def:summary-space-repair} gives
\[
\widehat\nu_n(\mathsf o_{1:n})
=\delta_0,
\]
so \(\widehat\nu_n\) is the constant Borel map.  In the same case, \cref{obj:def:wasserstein-confidence-set} gives the retained set
\[
\{\delta_0\},
\]
which is nonempty. \hfill{}% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.ProxyEffectlawEigencollisionFrontier.summary_repair_total_borel

\item Now suppose \(\mathcal K\ne\varnothing\).  Let
\[
d_{\mathrm{sum}}=4d_zd_x+d_x
\]
be the number of real coordinates in a five-block summary, and let
\[
\Psi_{\mathrm{sum}}(s)
=\bigl(s.M_0,s.M_1,s.N_0,s.N_1,s.m_X\bigr)\in\mathbb R^{d_{\mathrm{sum}}}
\]
be the coordinate homeomorphism from the summary space to Euclidean space.  Set
\[
\mathcal K^\Psi=\Psi_{\mathrm{sum}}(\mathcal K)
\]
and, for \(x_{\mathrm{Euc}},y_{\mathrm{Euc}}\in\mathbb R^{d_{\mathrm{sum}}}\), define the transported loss
\[
\ell_\Psi(x_{\mathrm{Euc}},y_{\mathrm{Euc}})
=
d_S\!\left(\Psi_{\mathrm{sum}}^{-1}(y_{\mathrm{Euc}}),
           \Psi_{\mathrm{sum}}^{-1}(x_{\mathrm{Euc}})\right).
\]
The set \(\mathcal K^\Psi\) is nonempty and compact, and \(\ell_\Psi\) is continuous because \(\Psi_{\mathrm{sum}}\) is a homeomorphism and \cref{obj:lem:summary-metric-continuity} gives continuity of \(d_S\).  Applying \cref{obj:lem:borel-nearest-point-selector} to \((\mathcal K^\Psi,\ell_\Psi)\), then transporting the selector back through \(\Psi_{\mathrm{sum}}\), gives a Borel map
\[
\Pi:\text{summary space}\longrightarrow \text{summary space}
\]
such that, for every summary \(s\),
\[
\Pi(s)\in\mathcal K
\quad\text{and}\quad
\forall q_{\mathrm{sum}}\in\mathcal K,\qquad
d_S(\Pi(s),s)\le d_S(q_{\mathrm{sum}},s).
\]
Together with the \(\overline F\) from the first step, this defines repair data \(R\) as in \cref{obj:def:summary-space-repair}; the fallback clause has premise \(\mathcal K=\varnothing\), which is false in the present case. \hfill{}% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.ProxyEffectlawEigencollisionFrontier.compactLoss_selector_of_homeomorph

\item For \(\mathcal K\ne\varnothing\), the repaired estimator is
\[
\widehat\nu_n(\mathsf o_{1:n})
=
\overline F\!\left(\Pi(\widehat S_n(\mathsf o_{1:n}))\right),
\qquad
\mathsf o_{1:n}\in O_{d_x,d_z}^n.
\]
The empirical summary map \(\widehat S_n\) is Borel by hypothesis, \(\Pi\) is Borel by the preceding step, and \(\overline F\) is Borel because it is continuous.  Hence \(\widehat\nu_n\) is Borel measurable. \hfill{}% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.ProxyEffectlawEigencollisionFrontier.summary_repair_total_borel

\item It remains in the nonempty case to verify nonemptiness of the retained summary-inversion set.  By \cref{obj:def:summary-radius},
\[
r_{n,\alpha}
=
C_0L\sqrt{\frac{\log(C_0/\alpha)}{n}} .
\]
Since \(n\ge 1\), \(0<\alpha<1/2\), \(C_0\ge 1\), and \(L\ge 1\), one has \(C_0/\alpha>1\), hence \(\log(C_0/\alpha)>0\), and therefore
\[
0<r_{n,\alpha}
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
0\le 2r_{n,\alpha}.
\]
For a fixed sample \(\mathsf o_{1:n}\), put
\[
q_{\mathrm{ret}}=\Pi(\widehat S_n(\mathsf o_{1:n})) .
\]
The selector property gives \(q_{\mathrm{ret}}\in\mathcal K\), and the defining formula for \(d_S\) gives
\[
d_S(q_{\mathrm{ret}},q_{\mathrm{ret}})=0\le 2r_{n,\alpha}.
\]
Moreover,
\[
\widehat\nu_n(\mathsf o_{1:n})
=
\overline F(q_{\mathrm{ret}}).
\]
Thus \(q_{\mathrm{ret}}\) witnesses membership of \(\widehat\nu_n(\mathsf o_{1:n})\) in the displayed retained set, so that set is nonempty for every sample. \hfill{}% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.ProxyEffectlawEigencollisionFrontier.summaryRadius_pos

\item The two cases \(\mathcal K=\varnothing\) and \(\mathcal K\ne\varnothing\) cover all possibilities.  In each case the constructed repair data \(R\) has Borel selector \(\Pi\), continuous extension \(\overline F\), Borel repaired estimator \(\widehat\nu_n\), the asserted \(\delta_0\) fallback when \(\mathcal K=\varnothing\), and a nonempty retained summary-inversion set for every sample. \hfill{}% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.ProxyEffectlawEigencollisionFrontier.summary_repair_total_borel
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:prop:polynomial-net-law-estimator}]
Set
\[
s_0:=\pi_0\sigma_0^2,\qquad
L_\tau:=\frac{4L\sqrt{d_z}}{\sigma_0},
\]
and order singular values nonincreasingly.

\begin{enumerate}
\item Let \(H_n,q_n,\ell_n\) and
\[
\mathcal A_n=\mathcal V_n\times\mathcal R_n\times\mathcal P_n\times\mathcal T_n
\]
be the structured lattice of \cref{obj:synth_32}.  Its four factors have cardinalities bounded by fixed-parameter constants times
\[
H_n^{d_xk},\qquad H_n^{k^2},\qquad H_n^{k-1},\qquad H_n^k .
\]
Since \(n\ge1\), the lattice height satisfies
\[
H_n\le C_H\sqrt n
\]
for a finite \(C_H=C_H(k,d_x,\pi_0,\sigma_0)\).  Hence there is a constant
\(C_\#>0\), depending only on the displayed parameters, such that
\[
|\mathcal A_n|
\le C_\# n^{(d_xk+k^2+2k-1)/2}.
\]
In the fixed-dimensional exact-real model of \cref{obj:synth_16}, the work is the empirical-summary pass plus one fixed-size objective evaluation for each candidate, so
\[
\operatorname{Work}_n
\le
C_\#\left\{n+n^{(d_xk+k^2+2k-1)/2}\right\}.
\] % lean: prescribed_latticeOperationCount_polynomial_bound

\item Choose \(C_0\ge1\) from \cref{obj:lem:uniform-summary-concentration}.  Thus, for every \(P\in\mathcal M\), \(n\ge1\), and \(0<\eta<1/2\),
\[
Q_P^{(n)}
\left\{
d_S(\widehat S_n,S(P))
>
C_0L\sqrt{\frac{\log(C_0/\eta)}{n}}
\right\}
\le\eta .
\]
The assumptions imply \(d_z>0\), \(L_\tau>0\), and \(s_0>0\).  Therefore
\[
0<\frac{8L_\tau}{s_0}
\le
C_{\mathrm{lat}}
=
\max\!\left\{\frac{8L_\tau}{s_0},
(L_\tau+K_D+LK_f)B_{\mathrm{lat}}\right\}.
\]
Define
\[
C_{\mathrm{tail}}
:=
\max\!\left\{C_0,\mathrm e,C_{\mathrm{lat}}(C_0L+1)\right\},
\qquad
C:=\max\{C_\#,C_{\mathrm{tail}}\}.
\]
Then \(C>0\). % lean: prescribedLatticeConstant_pos

\item Fix \(n\ge1\).  The lattice factors are finite and nonempty: canonical coordinate columns furnish an element of \(\mathcal V_n\), a rounded scalar multiple of \(I_k\) furnishes an element of \(\mathcal R_n\), the inequality \(k\ell_n\le H_n\) furnishes an element of \(\mathcal P_n\), and \(0\in\mathcal T_n\).  Enumerate \(\mathcal A_n\) in duplicate-free lexicographic order as
\[
\vartheta_i=(V_i,R_i,p_i,\tau_i),
\qquad i=1,\ldots,|\mathcal A_n|.
\]
For a summary \(s\), put
\[
i_n(s):=
\min_{\mathrm{lex}}\operatorname*{arg\,min}_{1\le i\le|\mathcal A_n|}
J_n(\vartheta_i;s),
\qquad
\Lambda_n(s):=\lambda_{\vartheta_{i_n(s)}} ,
\]
where \(J_n\) is the criterion in \cref{obj:synth_37}.  Thresholded singular-value pseudoinversion is Borel, each score \(s\mapsto J_n(\vartheta_i;s)\) is Borel, and a finite first-minimizer rule over Borel scores is Borel.  Hence
\[
\widehat\lambda_n(\omega):=\Lambda_n(\widehat S_n(\omega))
\]
is a total Borel empirical-summary rule, with \(\widehat S_n\) as in \cref{obj:def:empirical-summary}, and it is the first minimizer prescribed by \cref{obj:def:structured-lattice-program}.  Every coordinate of every \(p_i\in\mathcal P_n\) is at least \(\pi_0\), so aggregating coordinates at repeated effect locations gives
\[
\operatorname{AtomFloor}(\pi_0,\widehat\lambda_n(\omega))
\]
for every sample \(\omega\). % lean: structuredLatticeEstimator_exists

\item Fix \(P\in\mathcal M\), and put
\[
G_P:=\operatorname{stack}(M_0(P),M_1(P)).
\]
\Cref{obj:lem:model-compressed-spectral-certificate} gives \(\operatorname{rank}(G_P)=k\) and the threshold characterization at \(S(P)\).  In addition, \cref{obj:lem:stacked-proxy-moment-signal-margin} gives the population signal margin; since the singular values are nonincreasing,
\[
\sigma_j(G_P)\ge s_0\quad (1\le j\le k),
\qquad
\sigma_j(G_P)=0\quad (j>k).
\]
In particular, \(G_P\) has exactly its first \(k\) singular values at least \(s_0/2\).  Now let \(s\) satisfy
\[
d_S(s,S(P))<s_0/2,
\]
and set \(G_s:=\operatorname{stack}(M_0(s),M_1(s))\).  The stacked-block comparison gives
\[
\|G_s-G_P\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le d_S(s,S(P))<s_0/2.
\]
Weyl's inequality yields
\[
|\sigma_j(G_s)-\sigma_j(G_P)|<s_0/2
\qquad\text{for every }j.
\]
Thus, for \(1\le j\le k\),
\[
\sigma_j(G_s)>s_0/2,
\]
while, for \(j>k\),
\[
\sigma_j(G_s)<s_0/2.
\]
This proves that \(G_s\) also has exactly its first \(k\) singular values at least \(s_0/2\) and all remaining singular values below \(s_0/2\). % lean: model_thresholdRecoversDimension_of_dS_lt

\item Fix a sample \(\omega\), and write
\[
s_\omega:=\widehat S_n(\omega),
\qquad
\varepsilon_{\omega,P}:=d_S(s_\omega,S(P)).
\]
Choose the population signal tuple
\[
B(P)=V_{\mathrm{pop}}R_{\mathrm{pop}}^\top,\qquad
p_{\mathrm{pop}}=(P(U=u))_{u=1}^k,\qquad
\tau_{\mathrm{pop}}=(\tau_u(P))_{u=1}^k .
\]
By \cref{obj:def:target-feature,obj:def:latent-mass}, and because the latent classes partition the record space with positive class masses,
\[
m_X(P)
=E_P[X]
=\sum_{u=1}^k P(U=u)E_P[X\mid U=u]
=B(P)p_{\mathrm{pop}}.
\]
The chosen signal factorization \(B(P)=V_{\mathrm{pop}}R_{\mathrm{pop}}^\top\) therefore gives
\[
m_X(P)=V_{\mathrm{pop}}R_{\mathrm{pop}}^\top p_{\mathrm{pop}}.
\]
Using \cref{obj:ass:anchor}, the first target-proxy coordinate has conditional mean one in every latent class:
\[
B(P)^\top e_1
=
\bigl(E_P[X_1\mid U=u]\bigr)_{u=1}^k
=\mathbf1_k.
\]
Since \(B(P)^\top=R_{\mathrm{pop}}V_{\mathrm{pop}}^\top\), this yields
\[
R_{\mathrm{pop}}V_{\mathrm{pop}}^\top e_1=\mathbf1_k.
\]
The proxy and outcome moment factorizations from
\cref{obj:lem:observed-proxy-moment-factorization,obj:lem:observed-outcome-proxy-moment-factorization} give, for each arm \(t\),
\[
M_t(P)=A_t\Gamma_tB(P)^\top,
\qquad
N_t(P)=A_t\Gamma_t\operatorname{diag}(\mu_{tu})B(P)^\top .
\]
The proxy-rank margin in \cref{obj:ass:proxy-rank-margin} supplies injectivity of the linear maps represented by \(A_t\) and \(B(P)\).  By \cref{obj:lem:latent-arm-weight-invertibility}, \(\Gamma_t\) is injective, so \(A_t\Gamma_t\) is injective.  Applying \cref{obj:lem:moore-penrose-product-cancellation} with \(C=A_t\Gamma_t\), \(B=B(P)\), and \(\mu=(\mu_{tu})_u\) yields
\[
M_t(P)^\dagger N_t(P)
=
\{B(P)^\dagger\}^\top\operatorname{diag}(\mu_{tu})B(P)^\top .
\]
Subtracting the two arms and using \(\tau_{\mathrm{pop},u}=\mu_{1u}-\mu_{0u}\), together with \(B(P)=V_{\mathrm{pop}}R_{\mathrm{pop}}^\top\), gives
\[
\mathcal D_P:=
M_1(P)^\dagger N_1(P)-M_0(P)^\dagger N_0(P)
=
V_{\mathrm{pop}}R_{\mathrm{pop}}^{-1}
\operatorname{diag}(\tau_{\mathrm{pop}})
R_{\mathrm{pop}}V_{\mathrm{pop}}^\top .
\]
Moreover,
\[
\sigma_k(R_{\mathrm{pop}})\ge\sigma_0,\qquad
\|R_{\mathrm{pop}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le\sqrt{k}L,
\]
\[
p_{\mathrm{pop},u}\ge2\pi_0,\qquad
\sum_{u=1}^k p_{\mathrm{pop},u}=1,\qquad
|\tau_{\mathrm{pop},u}|\le L_\tau ,
\]
where the effect envelope is supplied by \cref{obj:lem:latent-effect-envelope}.  The associated quotient law is
\[
\nu_P=\sum_{u=1}^k p_{\mathrm{pop},u}\delta_{\tau_{\mathrm{pop},u}} .
\] % lean: population_structured_tuple_exists

\item Recall that \(q_n=H_n^{-1}\).  The definition of \(H_n\) gives the four mesh inequalities
\[
\sqrt{d_xk}\,q_n\le\frac14,
\qquad
kq_n\le\frac{\sigma_0}{2},
\qquad
kq_n\le\sqrt{k}L,
\qquad
\lceil\pi_0^{-1}\rceil\le H_n .
\]
For the signal basis \(V_{\mathrm{pop}}\), each entry has absolute value at most one because its columns are orthonormal.  Coordinatewise clipped rounding therefore gives a grid matrix \(V_{n,\mathrm{raw}}\) with
\[
(V_{n,\mathrm{raw}})_{ij}=q_n z_{ij}\quad (z_{ij}\in\mathbb Z),
\qquad |(V_{n,\mathrm{raw}})_{ij}|\le1,
\qquad
\|V_{n,\mathrm{raw}}-V_{\mathrm{pop}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
\le \sqrt{d_xk}\,q_n .
\]
Weyl's inequality and the first mesh bound give
\[
\sigma_k(V_{n,\mathrm{raw}})\ge1-\sqrt{d_xk}\,q_n\ge\frac34\ge\frac12 .
\]
Let \(V_n^\circ\) be the polar factor \(V_{n,\mathrm{raw}}(V_{n,\mathrm{raw}}^\top V_{n,\mathrm{raw}})^{-1/2}\).  The polar-factor perturbation estimate at an orthonormal basis yields
\[
\|V_n^\circ-V_{\mathrm{pop}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
\le4\sqrt{d_xk}\,q_n=c_Vq_n .
\]
Next, since \(\|R_{\mathrm{pop}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le\sqrt{k}L\), coordinatewise clipped rounding in the radius \(2\sqrt{k}L\) gives \(R_n^\circ\) with grid entries and
\[
\|R_n^\circ-R_{\mathrm{pop}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le kq_n .
\]
Using \(kq_n\le\sigma_0/2\) and Weyl's inequality,
\[
\sigma_k(R_n^\circ)\ge\sigma_k(R_{\mathrm{pop}})-kq_n
\ge\frac{\sigma_0}{2},
\]
and using \(kq_n\le\sqrt{k}L\),
\[
\|R_n^\circ\|_{\mathrm{op}}
\le \|R_{\mathrm{pop}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\|R_n^\circ-R_{\mathrm{pop}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
\le2\sqrt{k}L .
\]
For the weight vector, set \(b_u:=\lfloor H_np_{\mathrm{pop},u}\rfloor\).  Since \(p_{\mathrm{pop},u}\ge2\pi_0\) and \(\lceil\pi_0^{-1}\rceil\le H_n\), the quantities \(x:=\pi_0H_n\) and \(y_u:=H_np_{\mathrm{pop},u}\) satisfy \(1\le x\) and \(2x\le y_u\).  Because \(y_u< b_u+1\), this implies \(x\le b_u\), hence
\[
\lceil\pi_0H_n\rceil\le b_u .
\]
The floor vector satisfies \(\sum_u b_u\le H_n\) and \(H_n\le\sum_u b_u+k\).  Choose a subset of indices of size \(H_n-\sum_u b_u\) and add one to \(b_u\) on that subset, obtaining integers \(a_u\) with
\[
\sum_{u=1}^k a_u=H_n,
\qquad
\lceil\pi_0H_n\rceil\le a_u,
\qquad
\left|\frac{a_u}{H_n}-p_{\mathrm{pop},u}\right|\le q_n .
\]
Thus \(p_n^\circ:=(a_u/H_n)_{u=1}^k\) belongs to \(\mathcal P_n\), and summing the squared coordinate errors gives
\[
\|p_n^\circ-p_{\mathrm{pop}}\|_2
=\left\{\sum_{u=1}^k\left(\frac{a_u}{H_n}-p_{\mathrm{pop},u}\right)^2\right\}^{1/2}
\le\sqrt{k}q_n .
\]
Finally, since \(|\tau_{\mathrm{pop},u}|\le L_\tau\), coordinatewise clipped rounding on the effect grid gives \(\tau_n^\circ\in\mathcal T_n\) with
\[
|\tau_{n,u}^\circ-\tau_{\mathrm{pop},u}|\le q_n
\qquad(1\le u\le k).
\]
Combining these four rounded components gives a well-formed lattice comparator
\[
\vartheta_n^\circ=(V_n^\circ,R_n^\circ,p_n^\circ,\tau_n^\circ)\in\mathcal A_n
\]
satisfying
\[
\|V_n^\circ-V_{\mathrm{pop}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le c_Vq_n,\qquad
\|R_n^\circ-R_{\mathrm{pop}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le kq_n,
\]
\[
\|p_n^\circ-p_{\mathrm{pop}}\|_2\le\sqrt{k}q_n,\qquad
|\tau_{n,u}^\circ-\tau_{\mathrm{pop},u}|\le q_n .
\] % lean: population_structuredLatticeComparator_exists

\item Assume first that
\[
\varepsilon_{\omega,P}<s_0/4 .
\]
For each treatment arm,
\[
\|M_t(s_\omega)-M_t(P)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le\varepsilon_{\omega,P},
\qquad
\|N_t(s_\omega)-N_t(P)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le\varepsilon_{\omega,P}.
\]
