The population armwise proxy block has rank \(k\), kth singular value at least \(s_0\), and \(\|N_t(P)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le L\).  If \(\widetilde M_t(s_\omega)\) denotes the rank-\(k\) truncation retained at threshold \(s_0/2\), singular-value perturbation gives
\[
(M_t(s_\omega))^\dagger_{\ge s_0/2}=\widetilde M_t(s_\omega)^\dagger,
\quad
\sigma_k(\widetilde M_t(s_\omega))\ge3s_0/4,
\quad
\|\widetilde M_t(s_\omega)-M_t(P)\|_{\mathrm{op}}
\le2\varepsilon_{\omega,P}.
\]
Define the thresholded empirical compressed operator, in the notation of \cref{obj:synth_37}, by
\[
\widehat D(s_\omega):=
M_1(s_\omega)^\dagger{}_{\ge s_0/2}N_1(s_\omega)
-
M_0(s_\omega)^\dagger{}_{\ge s_0/2}N_0(s_\omega).
\]
The equal-rank Moore--Penrose perturbation identity gives
\[
\|\mathcal U^\dagger-\mathcal V^\dagger\|_{\mathrm{op}}
\le
3\max\{\|\mathcal U^\dagger\|_{\mathrm{op}}^2,\|\mathcal V^\dagger\|_{\mathrm{op}}^2\}
\|\mathcal U-\mathcal V\|_{\mathrm{op}},
\]
and therefore
\[
\|\widehat D(s_\omega)-\mathcal D_P\|_{\mathrm{op}}
\le A_D\varepsilon_{\omega,P},
\qquad
\|m_X(s_\omega)-m_X(P)\|_2\le\varepsilon_{\omega,P}.
\]
For the comparator \(\vartheta_n^\circ\), the mean residual decomposes as
\[
V_n^\circ(R_n^\circ)^\top p_n^\circ
-
V_{\mathrm{pop}}R_{\mathrm{pop}}^\top p_{\mathrm{pop}}
=
(V_n^\circ-V_{\mathrm{pop}})(R_n^\circ)^\top p_n^\circ
+
V_{\mathrm{pop}}(R_n^\circ-R_{\mathrm{pop}})^\top p_n^\circ
+
V_{\mathrm{pop}}R_{\mathrm{pop}}^\top(p_n^\circ-p_{\mathrm{pop}}),
\]
so the bounds \(\|V_{\mathrm{pop}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le1\), \(\|p_n^\circ\|_2\le1\),
\(\|R_n^\circ\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le2\sqrt{k}L\), and
\(\|R_{\mathrm{pop}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le\sqrt{k}L\) give
\[
\|V_n^\circ(R_n^\circ)^\top p_n^\circ
-
V_{\mathrm{pop}}R_{\mathrm{pop}}^\top p_{\mathrm{pop}}\|_2
\le c_mq_n .
\]
Similarly,
\[
R_n^\circ(V_n^\circ)^\top e_1
-
R_{\mathrm{pop}}V_{\mathrm{pop}}^\top e_1
=
(R_n^\circ-R_{\mathrm{pop}})V_{\mathrm{pop}}^\top e_1
+
R_n^\circ(V_n^\circ-V_{\mathrm{pop}})^\top e_1,
\]
and \(\|e_1\|_2=1\), \(\|V_{\mathrm{pop}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le1\), and
\(\|R_n^\circ\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le2\sqrt{k}L\) yield
\[
\|R_n^\circ(V_n^\circ)^\top e_1-\mathbf 1_k\|_2\le c_bq_n .
\]
For the operator residual, write
\[
\begin{aligned}
&D_{\vartheta_n^\circ}-\mathcal D_P \\
&=(V_n^\circ-V_{\mathrm{pop}})(R_n^\circ)^{-1}\operatorname{diag}(\tau_n^\circ)R_n^\circ(V_n^\circ)^\top\\
&\quad+V_{\mathrm{pop}}\{(R_n^\circ)^{-1}-R_{\mathrm{pop}}^{-1}\}\operatorname{diag}(\tau_n^\circ)R_n^\circ(V_n^\circ)^\top\\
&\quad+V_{\mathrm{pop}}R_{\mathrm{pop}}^{-1}\operatorname{diag}(\tau_n^\circ-\tau_{\mathrm{pop}})R_n^\circ(V_n^\circ)^\top\\
&\quad+V_{\mathrm{pop}}R_{\mathrm{pop}}^{-1}\operatorname{diag}(\tau_{\mathrm{pop}})(R_n^\circ-R_{\mathrm{pop}})(V_n^\circ)^\top\\
&\quad+V_{\mathrm{pop}}R_{\mathrm{pop}}^{-1}\operatorname{diag}(\tau_{\mathrm{pop}})R_{\mathrm{pop}}(V_n^\circ-V_{\mathrm{pop}})^\top .
\end{aligned}
\]
The inverse identity
\[
(R_n^\circ)^{-1}-R_{\mathrm{pop}}^{-1}
=
(R_n^\circ)^{-1}(R_{\mathrm{pop}}-R_n^\circ)R_{\mathrm{pop}}^{-1}
\]
and the singular-value bounds give
\[
\|(R_n^\circ)^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le\frac{2}{\sigma_0},
\qquad
\|R_{\mathrm{pop}}^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le\frac1{\sigma_0},
\qquad
\|(R_n^\circ)^{-1}-R_{\mathrm{pop}}^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
\le\frac{2kq_n}{\sigma_0^2}.
\]
Bounding the five displayed summands with
\(\|\operatorname{diag}(\tau_n^\circ)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le L_\tau\),
\(\|\operatorname{diag}(\tau_{\mathrm{pop}})\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le L_\tau\), and
\(\|\operatorname{diag}(\tau_n^\circ-\tau_{\mathrm{pop}})\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le q_n\)
gives
\[
\|D_{\vartheta_n^\circ}-\mathcal D_P\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le c_Dq_n.
\]
Consequently
\[
J_n(\vartheta_n^\circ;s_\omega)
\le
(A_D+1)\varepsilon_{\omega,P}+c_{\mathrm{grid}}q_n .
\]
Let
\[
\widehat\vartheta_n(\omega)
=(\widehat V_n,\widehat R_n,\widehat p_n,\widehat\tau_n)
\]
be the first minimizer.  Minimality and the nonnegativity of the three terms in \(J_n\) imply
\[
\|D_{\widehat\vartheta_n}-\mathcal D_P\|_{\mathrm{op}}
\le(2A_D+1)\varepsilon_{\omega,P}+c_{\mathrm{grid}}q_n,
\]
\[
\|\widehat V_n\widehat R_n^\top\widehat p_n-m_X(P)\|_2
\le(A_D+2)\varepsilon_{\omega,P}+c_{\mathrm{grid}}q_n,
\]
and
\[
\|\widehat R_n\widehat V_n^\top e_1-\mathbf1_k\|_2
\le(A_D+1)\varepsilon_{\omega,P}+c_{\mathrm{grid}}q_n.
\]
Since
\[
B_{\mathrm{lat}}=2(A_D+1)+c_{\mathrm{grid}},
\]
all three residuals are bounded by
\[
B_{\mathrm{lat}}(\varepsilon_{\omega,P}+q_n).
\] % lean: selected_structuredLattice_population_residuals_le

\item Let \(\varphi_\omega\) be the attaining Kantorovich--Rubinstein potential for
\((\widehat\lambda_n(\omega),\nu_P)\) supplied by
\cref{obj:lem:kantorovich-rubinstein-attaining-potential}.  Thus
\[
\varphi_\omega(0)=0,\qquad \operatorname{Lip}(\varphi_\omega)\le1,
\]
and
\[
W_1(\widehat\lambda_n(\omega),\nu_P)
=
\left|
\int\varphi_\omega\,d\widehat\lambda_n(\omega)
-
\int\varphi_\omega\,d\nu_P
\right|.
\]
The selected structured operator and \(\mathcal D_P\), extended by zero on the orthogonal complement of their signal spaces, have real diagonalizations with spectra in \([-L_\tau,L_\tau]\).  Put
\[
\kappa:=\frac{4\sqrt{k}L}{\sigma_0}.
\]
For either signal factorization with coordinate matrix \(R_{\mathrm{coord}}\), the ambient eigenbasis uses the signal coordinates and an orthonormal basis on the orthogonal complement.  Hence its condition number is bounded by
\[
\operatorname{cond}\le
\max\{\|R_{\mathrm{coord}}^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}},1\}
\max\{\|R_{\mathrm{coord}}\|_{\mathrm{op}},1\}.
\]
For the selected lattice tuple, the defining lattice constraints give
\[
\sigma_k(\widehat R_n)\ge\sigma_0/2,
\qquad
\|\widehat R_n\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le2\sqrt{k}L,
\qquad
|\widehat\tau_{n,u}|\le L_\tau .
\]
Therefore
\[
\|\widehat R_n^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le\frac{2}{\sigma_0},
\qquad
\max\{\|\widehat R_n^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}},1\}\le\frac{2}{\sigma_0},
\qquad
\max\{\|\widehat R_n\|_{\mathrm{op}},1\}\le2\sqrt{k}L,
\]
where the last two inequalities use \(0<\sigma_0\le1\), \(2\le k\), and \(1\le L\).  Thus the selected diagonalization has condition number at most \(\kappa\).  For the population tuple,
\[
\sigma_k(R_{\mathrm{pop}})\ge\sigma_0,
\qquad
\|R_{\mathrm{pop}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le\sqrt{k}L,
\qquad
|\tau_{\mathrm{pop},u}|\le L_\tau,
\]
so the same calculation, with
\[
\|R_{\mathrm{pop}}^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le\frac1{\sigma_0}\le\frac2{\sigma_0},
\qquad
\max\{\|R_{\mathrm{pop}}\|_{\mathrm{op}},1\}\le2\sqrt{k}L,
\]
gives the condition-number bound \(\kappa\) for the population diagonalization as well.
For the diagonalizations
\[
D_{\widehat\vartheta_n}=\mathcal Z_{\mathrm{hat}}\Lambda_{\mathrm{hat}}\mathcal Z_{\mathrm{hat}}^{-1},
\qquad
\mathcal D_P=\mathcal Z_{\mathrm{pop}}\Lambda_{\mathrm{pop}}\mathcal Z_{\mathrm{pop}}^{-1},
\]
the divided-difference identity gives
\[
\{\varphi_\omega(\Lambda_{\mathrm{hat}})\mathcal Z_{\mathrm{hat}}^{-1}\mathcal Z_{\mathrm{pop}}
-\mathcal Z_{\mathrm{hat}}^{-1}\mathcal Z_{\mathrm{pop}}\varphi_\omega(\Lambda_{\mathrm{pop}})\}_{ij}
=
d_{ij}\{\Lambda_{\mathrm{hat}}\mathcal Z_{\mathrm{hat}}^{-1}\mathcal Z_{\mathrm{pop}}
-\mathcal Z_{\mathrm{hat}}^{-1}\mathcal Z_{\mathrm{pop}}\Lambda_{\mathrm{pop}}\}_{ij},
\qquad |d_{ij}|\le1,
\]
with \(d_{ij}=0\) when the two eigenvalues coincide.  The coordinate perturbation in the last display is
\[
\Lambda_{\mathrm{hat}}\mathcal Z_{\mathrm{hat}}^{-1}\mathcal Z_{\mathrm{pop}}
-\mathcal Z_{\mathrm{hat}}^{-1}\mathcal Z_{\mathrm{pop}}\Lambda_{\mathrm{pop}}
=
\mathcal Z_{\mathrm{hat}}^{-1}
(D_{\widehat\vartheta_n}-\mathcal D_P)
\mathcal Z_{\mathrm{pop}}.
\]
Since \(|d_{ij}|\le1\), each column of the Schur product has Euclidean norm bounded by the corresponding column norm of this coordinate perturbation.  The fixed-dimensional column estimate therefore gives
\[
\left\|
\varphi_\omega(\Lambda_{\mathrm{hat}})\mathcal Z_{\mathrm{hat}}^{-1}\mathcal Z_{\mathrm{pop}}
-\mathcal Z_{\mathrm{hat}}^{-1}\mathcal Z_{\mathrm{pop}}\varphi_\omega(\Lambda_{\mathrm{pop}})
\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}
\le
\sqrt{d_x}\,
\left\|
\mathcal Z_{\mathrm{hat}}^{-1}
(D_{\widehat\vartheta_n}-\mathcal D_P)
\mathcal Z_{\mathrm{pop}}
\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}.
\]
Multiplying by the exterior diagonalizer factors yields
\[
\begin{aligned}
\|\varphi_\omega(D_{\widehat\vartheta_n})-\varphi_\omega(\mathcal D_P)\|_{\mathrm{op}}
&\le
\sqrt{d_x}\,
\|\mathcal Z_{\mathrm{hat}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
\|\mathcal Z_{\mathrm{hat}}^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
\|D_{\widehat\vartheta_n}-\mathcal D_P\|_{\mathrm{op}}
\|\mathcal Z_{\mathrm{pop}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
\|\mathcal Z_{\mathrm{pop}}^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}} \\
&\le
\sqrt{d_x}\,\kappa^2
\|D_{\widehat\vartheta_n}-\mathcal D_P\|_{\mathrm{op}}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Because \(\kappa=4\sqrt{k}L/\sigma_0\), this is
\[
\|\varphi_\omega(D_{\widehat\vartheta_n})-\varphi_\omega(\mathcal D_P)\|_{\mathrm{op}}
\le K_f\|D_{\widehat\vartheta_n}-\mathcal D_P\|_{\mathrm{op}},
\qquad
K_f=\frac{16\sqrt{d_x}\,kL^2}{\sigma_0^2}.
\]
For the selected law, put
\[
\chi_{\widehat\vartheta,u}:=(\widehat R_n\widehat V_n^\top e_1)_u .
\]
Since \(\widehat V_n^\top\widehat V_n=I_k\) and \(\widehat R_n\widehat R_n^{-1}=I_k\), the functional calculus of the selected structured operator gives
\[
\varphi_\omega(D_{\widehat\vartheta_n})
=
\widehat V_n\widehat R_n^{-1}
\operatorname{diag}\{\varphi_\omega(\widehat\tau_{n,u}):1\le u\le k\}
\widehat R_n\widehat V_n^\top,
\]
because \(\varphi_\omega(0)=0\) on the orthogonal complement.  Therefore
\[
\begin{aligned}
&(\widehat V_n\widehat R_n^\top\widehat p_n)^\top
\varphi_\omega(D_{\widehat\vartheta_n})e_1 \\
&\quad=
\widehat p_n^\top\widehat R_n\widehat V_n^\top
\widehat V_n\widehat R_n^{-1}
\operatorname{diag}\{\varphi_\omega(\widehat\tau_{n,u})\}
\widehat R_n\widehat V_n^\top e_1 \\
&\quad=
\widehat p_n^\top
\operatorname{diag}\{\varphi_\omega(\widehat\tau_{n,u})\}
\widehat R_n\widehat V_n^\top e_1
=
\sum_{u=1}^k\widehat p_{n,u}\varphi_\omega(\widehat\tau_{n,u})\chi_{\widehat\vartheta,u}.
\end{aligned}
\]
The labelled integral of \(\widehat\lambda_n(\omega)\) is
\[
\int\varphi_\omega\,d\widehat\lambda_n(\omega)
=
\sum_{u=1}^k\widehat p_{n,u}\varphi_\omega(\widehat\tau_{n,u}),
\]
and hence
\[
\int\varphi_\omega\,d\widehat\lambda_n(\omega)
-
(\widehat V_n\widehat R_n^\top\widehat p_n)^\top
\varphi_\omega(D_{\widehat\vartheta_n})e_1
=
\sum_{u=1}^k
\widehat p_{n,u}\varphi_\omega(\widehat\tau_{n,u})(1-\chi_{\widehat\vartheta,u}).
\]
Because \(\|\widehat p_n\|_2\le1\) and
\[
|\varphi_\omega(\widehat\tau_{n,u})|
=
|\varphi_\omega(\widehat\tau_{n,u})-\varphi_\omega(0)|
\le|\widehat\tau_{n,u}|
\le L_\tau,
\]
Cauchy's inequality yields
\[
\left|
\int\varphi_\omega\,d\widehat\lambda_n(\omega)
-
(\widehat V_n\widehat R_n^\top\widehat p_n)^\top
\varphi_\omega(D_{\widehat\vartheta_n})e_1
\right|
\le
L_\tau\|\widehat R_n\widehat V_n^\top e_1-\mathbf1_k\|_2 .
\]
For the population tuple, functional calculus gives
\[
\varphi_\omega(\mathcal D_P)
=
V_{\mathrm{pop}}R_{\mathrm{pop}}^{-1}
\operatorname{diag}\{\varphi_\omega(\tau_{\mathrm{pop}})\}
R_{\mathrm{pop}}V_{\mathrm{pop}}^\top ,
\]
since \(\varphi_\omega(0)=0\) on the orthogonal complement.  Using
\(m_X(P)=V_{\mathrm{pop}}R_{\mathrm{pop}}^\top p_{\mathrm{pop}}\),
\(V_{\mathrm{pop}}^\top V_{\mathrm{pop}}=I_k\), and
\(R_{\mathrm{pop}}V_{\mathrm{pop}}^\top e_1=\mathbf1_k\), we obtain
\[
m_X(P)^\top\varphi_\omega(\mathcal D_P)e_1
=
\sum_{u=1}^k p_{\mathrm{pop},u}\varphi_\omega(\tau_{\mathrm{pop},u})
=
\int\varphi_\omega\,d\nu_P .
\]
Together with \(\|m_X(P)\|_2\le L\) and
\[
\|\varphi_\omega(D_{\widehat\vartheta_n})e_1\|_2
\le
\kappa L_\tau
=
K_D,
\]
the residual bounds from the previous step give, in the small-error case,
\[
W_1(\widehat\lambda_n(\omega),\nu_P)
\le
(L_\tau+K_D+LK_f)B_{\mathrm{lat}}(\varepsilon_{\omega,P}+q_n).
\]
If instead \(\varepsilon_{\omega,P}\ge s_0/4\), both laws are supported on
\([-L_\tau,L_\tau]\), so the diameter bound from \cref{obj:def:wasserstein-distance} gives
\[
W_1(\widehat\lambda_n(\omega),\nu_P)
\le2L_\tau
\le\frac{8L_\tau}{s_0}\varepsilon_{\omega,P}.
\]
Finally \(q_n\le n^{-1/2}\).  Combining the two cases with the definition of \(C_{\mathrm{lat}}\) proves, for every sample,
\[
W_1(\widehat\lambda_n(\omega),\nu_P)
\le
C_{\mathrm{lat}}
\left\{
d_S(\widehat S_n(\omega),S(P))+\frac1{\sqrt n}
\right\}.
\] % lean: prescribedEstimator_wass1_le

\item The choice \(C\ge C_\#\) gives the displayed candidate-count and work bounds with the same \(C\).  For the tail bound, fix \(0<\eta<1/2\).  Since
\[
C\ge C_0,\qquad C\ge\mathrm e,\qquad C\ge C_{\mathrm{lat}}(C_0L+1),
\]
we have
\[
\log(C_0/\eta)\le\log(C/\eta),
\qquad
1\le\log(C/\eta),
\qquad
\frac1{\sqrt n}\le\sqrt{\frac{\log(C/\eta)}{n}}.
\]
On the event
\[
d_S(\widehat S_n,S(P))
\le
C_0L\sqrt{\frac{\log(C_0/\eta)}{n}},
\]
the deterministic inequality gives
\[
W_1(\widehat\lambda_n,\nu_P)
\le
C_{\mathrm{lat}}(C_0L+1)
\sqrt{\frac{\log(C/\eta)}{n}}
\le
C\sqrt{\frac{\log(C/\eta)}{n}}.
\]
Therefore
\[
\left\{
W_1(\widehat\lambda_n,\nu_P)>
C\sqrt{\frac{\log(C/\eta)}{n}}
\right\}
\subseteq
\left\{
d_S(\widehat S_n,S(P))>
C_0L\sqrt{\frac{\log(C_0/\eta)}{n}}
\right\}.
\]
Applying \cref{obj:lem:uniform-summary-concentration} under \(Q_P^{(n)}\) yields
\[
Q_P^{(n)}
\left\{
W_1(\widehat\lambda_n,\nu_P)>
C\sqrt{\frac{\log(C/\eta)}{n}}
\right\}
\le\eta .
\] % lean: polynomial_lattice_law_estimator
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:thm:collision-uniform-root-n}]
\begin{enumerate}
\item Let
\[
\mathscr S=\{S(P'):P'\in\mathcal M\},
\qquad
\mathcal K=\overline{\mathscr S}
\]
in the finite-dimensional summary space equipped with \(d_S\).  By
\cref{obj:lem:uniform-summary-concentration}, choose a simultaneous
concentration constant \(C_0\ge 1\).  By
\cref{obj:prop:polynomial-net-law-estimator}, choose a positive lattice
tail constant \(C_{\mathrm{tail}}\) associated with the structured-lattice
estimator.  By \cref{obj:thm:gap-free-positive-measure-modulus}, choose
\(C_{\mathrm{mod}}>0\) and a continuous extension
\[
\overline F:\mathcal K\to \mathcal P_k([-L_\tau,L_\tau])
\]
such that
\[
W_1\{\overline F(s),\overline F(s')\}
\le
C_{\mathrm{mod}}\,d_S(s,s'),
\qquad s,s'\in\mathcal K,
\]
and
\[
\overline F(S(Q))=\nu_Q,
\qquad Q\in\mathcal M .
\]
Set
\[
C=
\max\{2C_{\mathrm{tail}},\,
      \max(2C_0,\,4C_{\mathrm{mod}}C_0L)\}.
\]
Then \(C>0\), and \(C\) depends on
\((k,d_x,d_z,L,\pi_0,\sigma_0)\).% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.ProxyEffectlawEigencollisionFrontier.collision_uniform_root_n

\item Fix \(n\ge1\).  The empirical summary \(\widehat S_n\) is a Borel
sample map: its coordinates are finite sums of Borel coordinate maps,
treatment-arm indicators, products, and the total empty-arm normalizers in
\cref{obj:def:empirical-summary}.  Applying
\cref{obj:prop:polynomial-net-law-estimator} at this \(n\), choose the
structured-lattice estimator \(\widehat\lambda_n\).  Its finite list contains
each well-formed candidate once, in lexicographic order, and the returned law is
\[
\widehat\lambda_n(\omega)=\lambda_{\widehat\vartheta_n(\omega)},
\]
where \(\widehat\vartheta_n(\omega)\) is the first lexicographic minimizer of
\(J_n(\vartheta;\widehat S_n(\omega))\) over the structured lattice in
\cref{obj:def:structured-lattice-program}, with threshold
\(\pi_0\sigma_0^2/2\).  The same result gives Borel measurability of
\(\widehat\lambda_n\) and, for every \(P\in\mathcal M\) and every
\(0<\zeta<1/2\),
\[
Q_P^{(n)}
\left\{
W_1(\widehat\lambda_n,\nu_P)>
C_{\mathrm{tail}}
\sqrt{\frac{\log(C_{\mathrm{tail}}/\zeta)}{n}}
\right\}
\le \zeta .
\]% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.ProxyEffectlawEigencollisionFrontier.polynomial_lattice_law_estimator

\item \Cref{obj:prop:summary-closure-compact} gives compactness of
\(\mathcal K\).  If \(\mathcal K=\varnothing\), take the selector
\[
\Pi(s)=0
\]
for every ambient summary \(s\), and define the repaired estimator by the
zero-law branch in \cref{obj:def:summary-space-repair}.  If
\(\mathcal K\ne\varnothing\), identify the finite-dimensional summary space
with Euclidean space.  The loss \((s,q)\mapsto d_S(q,s)\) is continuous by
\cref{obj:lem:summary-metric-continuity}, so
\cref{obj:lem:borel-nearest-point-selector} gives a Borel map \(\Pi\) such
that
\[
\Pi(s)\in\mathcal K,
\qquad
d_S(\Pi(s),s)\le d_S(q,s)
\quad\text{for all }q\in\mathcal K .
\]
With this selector, set
\[
\widehat\nu_n(\omega)
=
\begin{cases}
\delta_0, & \mathcal K=\varnothing,\\
\overline F\{\Pi(\widehat S_n(\omega))\}, & \mathcal K\ne\varnothing .
\end{cases}
\]
Continuity of \(\overline F\), Borel measurability of \(\Pi\), and Borel
measurability of \(\widehat S_n\) make \(\widehat\nu_n\) a total Borel
estimator into \(\mathcal P_k([-L_\tau,L_\tau])\).% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.ProxyEffectlawEigencollisionFrontier.compactLoss_selector_of_homeomorph

\item Fix \(P\in\mathcal M\).  Then \(S(P)\in\mathscr S\subseteq\mathcal K\),
so \(\mathcal K\ne\varnothing\).  For every sample \(\omega\), nearest-summary
optimality gives
\[
d_S\{\Pi(\widehat S_n(\omega)),\widehat S_n(\omega)\}
\le
d_S\{S(P),\widehat S_n(\omega)\}
=
d_S\{\widehat S_n(\omega),S(P)\}.
\]
By the triangle inequality for \(d_S\),
\[
\begin{aligned}
d_S\{\Pi(\widehat S_n(\omega)),S(P)\}
&\le
d_S\{\Pi(\widehat S_n(\omega)),\widehat S_n(\omega)\}
+d_S\{\widehat S_n(\omega),S(P)\}  \\
&\le
2d_S\{\widehat S_n(\omega),S(P)\}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Since \(\overline F(S(P))=\nu_P\), the Lipschitz extension bound gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
W_1(\widehat\nu_n(\omega),\nu_P)
&\le
C_{\mathrm{mod}}\,
d_S\{\Pi(\widehat S_n(\omega)),S(P)\}  \\
&\le
2C_{\mathrm{mod}}\,d_S\{\widehat S_n(\omega),S(P)\}.
\end{aligned}
\]% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.ProxyEffectlawEigencollisionFrontier.collision_uniform_root_n

\item Fix \(0<\eta<1/2\), and define
\[
\Theta_\lambda
=
C_{\mathrm{tail}}
\sqrt{\frac{\log(C_{\mathrm{tail}}/(\eta/2))}{n}},
\qquad
\Theta_S
=
C_0L
\sqrt{\frac{\log(C_0/(\eta/2))}{n}},
\]
\[
\Theta_C
=
C\sqrt{\frac{\log(C/\eta)}{n}} .
\]
The definition of \(C\) implies
\[
2C_{\mathrm{tail}}\le C,
\qquad
2C_0\le C,
\qquad
4C_{\mathrm{mod}}C_0L\le C .
\]
Hence
\[
\frac{C_{\mathrm{tail}}}{\eta/2}
=
\frac{2C_{\mathrm{tail}}}{\eta}
\le
\frac{C}{\eta},
\qquad
\frac{C_0}{\eta/2}
=
\frac{2C_0}{\eta}
\le
\frac{C}{\eta}.
\]
Monotonicity of \(\log\) and of the square root gives
\[
\Theta_\lambda\le\Theta_C
\]
and
\[
2C_{\mathrm{mod}}\Theta_S\le\Theta_C .
\]
Therefore
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\left\{
\max\!\left[
W_1(\widehat\lambda_n,\nu_P),
W_1(\widehat\nu_n,\nu_P)
\right]>\Theta_C
\right\}  \\
&\qquad\subseteq
\{W_1(\widehat\lambda_n,\nu_P)>\Theta_\lambda\}
\cup
\{d_S(\widehat S_n,S(P))>\Theta_S\}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Indeed, on the complement of the right-hand union, the lattice error is at most
\(\Theta_\lambda\le\Theta_C\), while the repaired-summary error is at most
\(2C_{\mathrm{mod}}\Theta_S\le\Theta_C\).% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.ProxyEffectlawEigencollisionFrontier.collision_uniform_root_n

\item Apply the lattice tail bound with \(\zeta=\eta/2\) and
\cref{obj:lem:uniform-summary-concentration} with \(\zeta=\eta/2\).  The
displayed set inclusion and the union bound yield
\[
\begin{aligned}
&Q_P^{(n)}
\left\{
\max\!\left[
W_1(\widehat\lambda_n,\nu_P),
W_1(\widehat\nu_n,\nu_P)
\right]>
C\sqrt{\frac{\log(C/\eta)}{n}}
\right\} \\
&\qquad\le
Q_P^{(n)}\{W_1(\widehat\lambda_n,\nu_P)>\Theta_\lambda\}
+
Q_P^{(n)}\{d_S(\widehat S_n,S(P))>\Theta_S\} \\
&\qquad\le
\eta/2+\eta/2
=\eta .
\end{aligned}
\]
The arm-count conclusion is also supplied by
\cref{obj:lem:uniform-summary-concentration}: for each
\(t\in\{0,1\}\),
\[
Q_P^{(n)}\{N_{t,n}=0\}\le (1-k\pi_0)^n .
\]% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.ProxyEffectlawEigencollisionFrontier.collision_uniform_root_n
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:thm:honest-root-n-confidence}]
\begin{enumerate}
\item By \cref{obj:lem:uniform-summary-concentration}, choose \(C_0\ge1\) such that, for every \(L\ge1\), \(n\ge1\), \(P\in\mathcal M\), and \(0<\alpha<1/2\),
\[
Q_P^{(n)}
\left\{
d_S(\widehat S_n,S(P))>
C_0L\sqrt{\frac{\log(C_0/\alpha)}{n}}
\right\}
\le \alpha .
\]
Fix \(L\ge1\), and put
\[
L_\tau=\frac{4L\sqrt{d_z}}{\sigma_0}.
\]
Let
\[
\mathcal K=\overline{\{S(P):P\in\mathcal M\}}
\]
be the summary closure from \cref{obj:def:summary-closure}.  By \cref{obj:thm:gap-free-positive-measure-modulus}, choose \(C_{\mathrm{mod}}>0\) and a continuous extension
\[
\overline F:\mathcal K\to \mathcal P_k([-L_\tau,L_\tau])
\]
such that
\[
W_1\{\overline F(\chi),\overline F(\chi')\}
\le C_{\mathrm{mod}}d_S(\chi,\chi')
\qquad(\chi,\chi'\in\mathcal K),
\]
and
\[
\overline F(S(P))=\nu_P
\qquad(P\in\mathcal M).
\]
By \cref{obj:prop:polynomial-net-law-estimator}, the prescribed structured-lattice constant \(C_{\mathrm{lat}}\) is positive.  For each \(n\ge1\), \cref{obj:prop:polynomial-net-law-estimator} supplies a measurable structured-lattice estimator \(\widehat\lambda_n\), generated by the finite exhaustive duplicate-free lexicographic list of well-formed lattice candidates and by the first minimizer of \(J_n(\vartheta;\widehat S_n)\), such that, for every sample \(\mathsf o\in O_{d_x,d_z}^{n}\) and every \(P\in\mathcal M\),
\[
W_1\bigl(\widehat\lambda_n(\mathsf o),\nu_P\bigr)
\le
C_{\mathrm{lat}}
\left\{
d_S(\widehat S_n(\mathsf o),S(P))+(\sqrt n)^{-1}
\right\}.
\]
The same cited result gives, for every sample,
\[
\operatorname{AtomFloor}\bigl(\pi_0,\widehat\lambda_n(\mathsf o)\bigr).
\]
For \(0<\alpha<1/2\), define
\[
r_{n,\alpha}
=
C_0L\sqrt{\frac{\log(C_0/\alpha)}{n}},
\qquad
R_{n,\alpha}
=
C_{\mathrm{lat}}\left(r_{n,\alpha}+(\sqrt n)^{-1}\right).
\]
The parameter domain gives \(r_{n,\alpha}>0\), and \(C_{\mathrm{lat}}>0\) gives \(R_{n,\alpha}>0\).  % lean: honest_root_n_confidence

\item Construct the repair selector \(\Pi\).  By \cref{obj:prop:summary-closure-compact}, \(\mathcal K\) is compact.  If \(\mathcal K=\varnothing\), set
\[
\Pi(s)=0
\qquad\text{for every ambient summary }s.
\]
If \(\mathcal K\ne\varnothing\), then \cref{obj:lem:summary-metric-continuity} and \cref{obj:lem:borel-nearest-point-selector} give a Borel map \(\Pi\) such that, for every ambient summary \(s\),
\[
\Pi(s)\in\mathcal K,
\qquad
d_S(s,\Pi(s))\le d_S(s,q)\quad(q\in\mathcal K).
\]
Together with \(\overline F\), this is the repair datum of \cref{obj:def:summary-space-repair}; the induced repaired summary-to-law map is measurable, and the displayed Lipschitz bound for \(\overline F\) is retained on \(\mathcal K\).  % lean: summaryRepair_with_modulus

\item Fix a sample \(\mathsf o\in O_{d_x,d_z}^{n}\).  If \(\mathcal K=\varnothing\), then \(\mathcal C_{n,\alpha}(\mathsf o)=\{\delta_0\}\), hence \(\mathcal C_{n,\alpha}(\mathsf o)\) is nonempty.  If \(\mathcal K\ne\varnothing\), then \(\Pi(\widehat S_n(\mathsf o))\in\mathcal K\), and
\[
d_S\{\Pi(\widehat S_n(\mathsf o)),\Pi(\widehat S_n(\mathsf o))\}
=0
\le 2r_{n,\alpha}.
\]
Thus
\[
\overline F\{\Pi(\widehat S_n(\mathsf o))\}\in\mathcal C_{n,\alpha}(\mathsf o),
\]
so \(\mathcal C_{n,\alpha}(\mathsf o)\) is nonempty.  The algorithmic set is nonempty because
\[
\operatorname{AtomFloor}\bigl(\pi_0,\widehat\lambda_n(\mathsf o)\bigr),
\qquad
W_1\{\widehat\lambda_n(\mathsf o),\widehat\lambda_n(\mathsf o)\}=0\le R_{n,\alpha}.
\]
By \cref{obj:def:wasserstein-confidence-set}, \(m_\star=\pi_0\) and membership in \(\mathcal C^{\mathrm{alg}}_{n,\alpha}\) is membership in the atom-floor subclass together with
\[
W_1(\nu,\widehat\lambda_n(\mathsf o))\le R_{n,\alpha}.
\]
By \cref{obj:def:wasserstein-distance}, this last inequality is equivalent to the existence of a finite nonnegative transport plan from \(\nu\) to \(\widehat\lambda_n(\mathsf o)\) whose absolute-distance cost is at most \(R_{n,\alpha}\).  This gives the asserted finite constrained transport representation.  % lean: confidenceSets_constrainedRepresentation

\item Fix \(P\in\mathcal M\).  Since \(S(P)\in\mathcal K\), the closure \(\mathcal K\) is nonempty.  Define the bad event
\[
\mathsf{Bad}_P
=
\left\{
\mathsf o\in O_{d_x,d_z}^{n}:
r_{n,\alpha}<d_S(\widehat S_n(\mathsf o),S(P))
\right\}.
\]
The concentration bound gives
\[
Q_P^{(n)}(\mathsf{Bad}_P)\le \alpha .
\]
The empirical summary is Borel by \cref{obj:def:empirical-summary}, and \((s,q)\mapsto d_S(s,q)\) is continuous by \cref{obj:lem:summary-metric-continuity}; hence \(\mathsf{Bad}_P\) is measurable.  Therefore
\[
Q_P^{(n)}(\mathsf{Bad}_P^{\,c})
=
1-Q_P^{(n)}(\mathsf{Bad}_P)
\ge 1-\alpha .
\]
For every \(\mathsf o\in\mathsf{Bad}_P^{\,c}\),
\[
d_S(\widehat S_n(\mathsf o),S(P))\le r_{n,\alpha}.
\]
Nearest-summary optimality gives
\[
d_S\{\widehat S_n(\mathsf o),\Pi(\widehat S_n(\mathsf o))\}
\le
d_S\{\widehat S_n(\mathsf o),S(P)\}
\le r_{n,\alpha},
\]
and the triangle inequality gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
d_S\{S(P),\Pi(\widehat S_n(\mathsf o))\}
&\le
d_S\{S(P),\widehat S_n(\mathsf o)\}
+
d_S\{\widehat S_n(\mathsf o),\Pi(\widehat S_n(\mathsf o))\}  \\
&\le 2r_{n,\alpha}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Since \(\overline F(S(P))=\nu_P\), the summary \(S(P)\) itself witnesses
\[
\nu_P\in\mathcal C_{n,\alpha}(\mathsf o).
\]
% lean: honest_root_n_confidence

\item On the same sample \(\mathsf o\in\mathsf{Bad}_P^{\,c}\), \cref{obj:prop:polynomial-net-law-estimator} gives
\[
W_1\{\widehat\lambda_n(\mathsf o),\nu_P\}
\le
C_{\mathrm{lat}}\{r_{n,\alpha}+(\sqrt n)^{-1}\}
=
R_{n,\alpha}.
\]
By symmetry of \(W_1\),
\[
W_1\{\nu_P,\widehat\lambda_n(\mathsf o)\}
\le R_{n,\alpha}.
\]
It remains to verify the atom floor for \(\nu_P\).  For each latent class \(u\), \cref{obj:ass:latent-arm-positivity} as included in \cref{obj:def:model-class} gives
\[
P(U=u,T=0)\ge\pi_0,
\qquad
P(U=u,T=1)\ge\pi_0.
\]
The two treatment cells partition the latent class, so
\[
p_u=P(U=u)=P(U=u,T=0)+P(U=u,T=1)\ge2\pi_0\ge\pi_0,
\]
where \(p_u\) is the latent mass from \cref{obj:def:latent-mass}.  For the raw latent law
\[
\sum_{u=1}^{k}p_u\delta_{\tau_u},
\]
the aggregate mass at any distinct support value \(\zeta\) is
\[
\sum_{u:\tau_u=\zeta}p_u\ge\pi_0,
\]
because the displayed sum contains at least one latent class.  Passing to the quotient law \(\nu_P\) of \cref{obj:def:quotient-law} only aggregates equal atom locations, so \cref{obj:def:atom-floor} gives
\[
\operatorname{AtomFloor}(\pi_0,\nu_P).
\]
Thus
\[
\nu_P\in\mathcal C^{\mathrm{alg}}_{n,\alpha}(\mathsf o).
\]
Combining the two memberships on \(\mathsf{Bad}_P^{\,c}\),
\[
1-\alpha
\le
Q_P^{(n)}
\left\{
\nu_P\in
\mathcal C_{n,\alpha}\cap\mathcal C^{\mathrm{alg}}_{n,\alpha}
\right\}.
\]
% lean: quotientLaw_atomFloor

\item Fix a sample \(\mathsf o\).  For the theoretical diameter, take any
\[
\xi_1,\xi_2\in\mathcal C_{n,\alpha}(\mathsf o).
\]
If \(\mathcal K=\varnothing\), then both elements equal the zero-law fallback, so
\[
W_1(\xi_1,\xi_2)=0\le4C_{\mathrm{mod}}r_{n,\alpha}.
\]
If \(\mathcal K\ne\varnothing\), then by \cref{obj:def:wasserstein-confidence-set} there are \(\chi_1,\chi_2\in\mathcal K\) such that
\[
\xi_j=\overline F(\chi_j),
\qquad
d_S\{\chi_j,\Pi(\widehat S_n(\mathsf o))\}\le2r_{n,\alpha}
\quad(j=1,2).
\]
By symmetry and the triangle inequality,
\[
\begin{aligned}
d_S(\chi_1,\chi_2)
&\le
d_S\{\chi_1,\Pi(\widehat S_n(\mathsf o))\}
+
d_S\{\Pi(\widehat S_n(\mathsf o)),\chi_2\}  \\
&\le4r_{n,\alpha}.
\end{aligned}
\]
The Lipschitz bound for \(\overline F\) gives
\[
W_1(\xi_1,\xi_2)
\le
C_{\mathrm{mod}}d_S(\chi_1,\chi_2)
\le
4C_{\mathrm{mod}}r_{n,\alpha}.
\]
Taking the supremum over all pairs in the nonempty set \(\mathcal C_{n,\alpha}(\mathsf o)\) yields
\[
\operatorname{diam}_{W_1}(\mathcal C_{n,\alpha})
\le
4C_{\mathrm{mod}}r_{n,\alpha}.
\]
% lean: honest_root_n_confidence

\item For the algorithmic diameter, take any
\[
\xi_1,\xi_2\in\mathcal C^{\mathrm{alg}}_{n,\alpha}(\mathsf o).
\]
By \cref{obj:def:wasserstein-confidence-set},
\[
W_1\{\xi_1,\widehat\lambda_n(\mathsf o)\}\le R_{n,\alpha},
\qquad
W_1\{\xi_2,\widehat\lambda_n(\mathsf o)\}\le R_{n,\alpha}.
\]
By symmetry and the triangle inequality,
\[
\begin{aligned}
W_1(\xi_1,\xi_2)
&\le
W_1\{\xi_1,\widehat\lambda_n(\mathsf o)\}
+
W_1\{\widehat\lambda_n(\mathsf o),\xi_2\}  \\
&\le2R_{n,\alpha}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Taking the supremum over all pairs in the nonempty set \(\mathcal C^{\mathrm{alg}}_{n,\alpha}(\mathsf o)\) gives
\[
\operatorname{diam}_{W_1}(\mathcal C^{\mathrm{alg}}_{n,\alpha})
\le
2R_{n,\alpha}.
\]
% lean: honest_root_n_confidence
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:thm:cluster-adaptive-report}]
\begin{enumerate}
\item By \cref{obj:lem:uniform-summary-concentration}, choose \(C_0\) with
\(C_0\ge1\) such that, for every \(L\ge1\), \(n\ge1\), \(P\in\mathcal M\),
and \(0<\eta<1/2\),
\[
Q_P^{(n)}
\left\{
d_S(\widehat S_n,S(P))
>
C_0L\sqrt{\frac{\log(C_0/\eta)}{n}}
\right\}
\le \eta .
\]
Fix \(L\ge1\).  With the displayed definitions of
\(L_\tau,s_0,c_V,K_D,A_D,K_f,c_D,c_m,c_b,c_{\mathrm{grid}},B_{\mathrm{lat}}\),
\cref{obj:prop:polynomial-net-law-estimator} gives
\[
C_{\mathrm{lat}}>0
\]
and, for every \(n\ge1\), a Borel structured-lattice estimator
\(\widehat\lambda_n\) obtained from the exhaustive duplicate-free lexicographic
list of well-formed candidates and the first minimizer of
\(J_n(\vartheta;\widehat S_n)\).  It also gives, for every sample \(\omega\),
\[
\operatorname{AtomFloor}(\pi_0,\widehat\lambda_n(\omega)),
\]
and, for every \(P\in\mathcal M\),
\[
W_1(\widehat\lambda_n(\omega),\nu_P)
\le
C_{\mathrm{lat}}
\left\{
d_S(\widehat S_n(\omega),S(P))+\frac1{\sqrt n}
\right\}.
\]
The extension \(\overline F\) on \(\mathcal K\) is supplied by
\cref{obj:thm:gap-free-positive-measure-modulus}.  By
\cref{obj:prop:summary-closure-compact}, \(\mathcal K\) is compact, and by
\cref{obj:lem:summary-metric-continuity}, \((s,q)\mapsto d_S(s,q)\) is
continuous.  If \(\mathcal K\ne\emptyset\), \cref{obj:lem:borel-nearest-point-selector}
supplies a Borel selector \(\Pi\) satisfying
\[
\Pi(s)\in\mathcal K,
\qquad
d_S(\Pi(s),s)\le d_S(q,s)\quad(q\in\mathcal K).
\]
If \(\mathcal K=\emptyset\), set \(\Pi(s)=0\) for every ambient summary \(s\).
Together with \(\overline F\), these maps are repair data of the form specified
in \cref{obj:def:summary-space-repair}; the induced sample-to-law map is Borel
measurable by composition in the nonempty case and by the constant fallback in
the empty case. % lean: summaryRepair_with_modulus

\item Fix \(0<\alpha<1/2\), \(P\in\mathcal M\), and \(n\ge1\).  Define
\[
r_{n,\alpha}
=
C_0L\sqrt{\frac{\log(C_0/\alpha)}{n}},
\qquad
R_{n,\alpha}
=
C_{\mathrm{lat}}\left(r_{n,\alpha}+\frac1{\sqrt n}\right),
\qquad
\rho_{n,\alpha}=\frac{R_{n,\alpha}}{\pi_0}.
\]
Since \(C_{\mathrm{lat}}>0\), \(C_0\ge1\), \(L\ge1\), \(n\ge1\), and
\(0<\alpha<1/2\), we have
\[
R_{n,\alpha}>0,
\qquad
\rho_{n,\alpha}>0.
\]
The event
\[
E_P=\left\{\omega:d_S(\widehat S_n(\omega),S(P))\le r_{n,\alpha}\right\}
\]
is the complement of the deviation event in the first display with
\(\eta=\alpha\), and hence
\[
Q_P^{(n)}(E_P)\ge1-\alpha .
\]
For every \(\omega\in E_P\), the deterministic lattice bound gives
\[
W_1(\widehat\lambda_n(\omega),\nu_P)
\le
C_{\mathrm{lat}}
\left(r_{n,\alpha}+\frac1{\sqrt n}\right)
=
R_{n,\alpha}. % lean: uniform_summary_concentration
\]

\item The representative law \(\nu_P\) has atom floor \(\pi_0\).  Indeed, for
each latent class \(u\),
\[
p_u(P)=P(U=u,T=0)+P(U=u,T=1)\ge \pi_0+\pi_0\ge\pi_0
\]
by the latent-arm positivity component of \(P\in\mathcal M\).  After coincident
latent effects are aggregated as in \cref{obj:def:quotient-law}, every distinct
support atom has aggregate mass at least \(\pi_0\).  Since \(W_1\) is symmetric
on represented atomic laws, the preceding step gives
\[
W_1(\nu_P,\widehat\lambda_n(\omega))\le R_{n,\alpha}.
\]
Thus
\[
\nu_P\in\mathcal C^{\mathrm{alg}}_{n,\alpha},
\]
because \cref{obj:def:wasserstein-confidence-set} defines
\[
\mathcal C^{\mathrm{alg}}_{n,\alpha}
=
\left\{
\xi:
\operatorname{AtomFloor}(\pi_0,\xi)
\ \text{and}\
W_1(\xi,\widehat\lambda_n(\omega))\le R_{n,\alpha}
\right\}. % lean: quotientLaw_atomFloor
\]

\item We shall use the following finite-transport implication.  If two finite
atomic probability laws \(\chi\) and \(\chi'\) have atom floor \(m>0\) and
\[
W_1(\chi,\chi')\le m\rho ,
\]
then
\[
\operatorname{dist}(x,\operatorname{supp}\chi')\le\rho
\quad(x\in\operatorname{supp}\chi),
\qquad
\operatorname{dist}(y,\operatorname{supp}\chi)\le\rho
\quad(y\in\operatorname{supp}\chi').
\]
For the first assertion, put
\[
d_\chi(x):=\operatorname{dist}(x,\operatorname{supp}\chi').
\]
If \(d_\chi(x)>\rho\), then every coupling in
\cref{obj:def:wasserstein-distance} moves the aggregate mass at \(x\), which is
at least \(m\), by distance at least \(d_\chi(x)\).  Hence every transport cost
is at least \(m d_\chi(x)>m\rho\), contradicting the displayed Wasserstein
bound.  The second assertion follows by applying the same argument after
transposing a coupling.  Applying this implication with
\[
m=\pi_0,\qquad
\chi=\widehat\lambda_n(\omega),\qquad
\chi'=\nu_P,\qquad
\rho=\rho_{n,\alpha},
\]
and using \(\pi_0\rho_{n,\alpha}=R_{n,\alpha}\), gives mutual
\(\rho_{n,\alpha}\)-closeness of the two supports. % lean: support_close_of_wass1_le

\item The components \(\widehat{\mathscr K}_{n,\alpha}\) are the connected
components of \(\operatorname{supp}(\widehat\lambda_n(\omega))\) under links of
length at most \(4\rho_{n,\alpha}\).  For such a component \(C\),
\[
K_C(P)
=
\{x\in\operatorname{supp}(\nu_P):\operatorname{dist}(x,C)\le\rho_{n,\alpha}\}.
\]
The mutual support closeness from the previous step implies that every
\(x\in\operatorname{supp}(\nu_P)\) belongs to at least one such set.  If \(x\)
belonged to two components, choose center atoms in the two components within
\(\rho_{n,\alpha}\) of \(x\).  These two center atoms are within
\(2\rho_{n,\alpha}\le4\rho_{n,\alpha}\) of each other, so they lie in the same
connected component.  Hence
\[
x\in\operatorname{supp}(\nu_P)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\exists!\,C\in\widehat{\mathscr K}_{n,\alpha}\ \text{with}\ x\in K_C(P).
\]
For every component \(C\), reverse support closeness supplies a true atom within
\(\rho_{n,\alpha}\) of any center atom in \(C\), so \(K_C(P)\ne\emptyset\).
The inclusion
\[
K_C(P)\subseteq\operatorname{supp}(\nu_P)
\]
is part of the displayed definition of \(K_C(P)\).  If \(x\in K_C(P)\), then
some \(y\in C\) satisfies \(|x-y|\le\rho_{n,\alpha}\), and therefore
\[
\min C-\rho_{n,\alpha}\le x\le \max C+\rho_{n,\alpha},
\]
which is the reported support interval in \cref{obj:def:cluster-report}.  Since
\(\nu_P\in\mathcal C^{\mathrm{alg}}_{n,\alpha}\),
\[
\nu_P(K_C(P))\in
\left\{v:\exists \xi\in\mathcal C^{\mathrm{alg}}_{n,\alpha},\
v=\xi(K_C(\xi))\right\}.
\]
Consequently
\[
\inf_{\xi\in\mathcal C^{\mathrm{alg}}_{n,\alpha}}\xi(K_C(\xi))
\le
\nu_P(K_C(P))
\le
\sup_{\xi\in\mathcal C^{\mathrm{alg}}_{n,\alpha}}\xi(K_C(\xi)),
\]
which proves the mass-interval containment. % lean: cluster_deterministic_report

\item Fix \(\xi\in\mathcal C^{\mathrm{alg}}_{n,\alpha}\).  Then
\[
\operatorname{AtomFloor}(\pi_0,\xi),
\qquad
W_1(\xi,\widehat\lambda_n(\omega))\le R_{n,\alpha},
\]
and the triangle inequality gives
\[
W_1(\xi,\nu_P)
\le
W_1(\xi,\widehat\lambda_n(\omega))
+
W_1(\widehat\lambda_n(\omega),\nu_P)
\le 2R_{n,\alpha}.
\]
The transport implication applied to \(\xi\) and \(\widehat\lambda_n(\omega)\)
shows that their supports are mutually \(\rho_{n,\alpha}\)-close.  For a
component \(C\), write
\[
K_C^\xi:=K_C(\xi),
\qquad
K_C^P:=K_C(P),
\qquad
\Delta_C^{\mathrm r}:=(\Delta_C(P))_{\mathrm{real}}
\]
when \(\Delta_C(P)<+\infty\), where \(\Delta_C^{\mathrm r}\) is the finite real
external gap.

If \(\Delta_C(P)=+\infty\), then
\[
\operatorname{supp}(\nu_P)\setminus K_C^P=\emptyset .
\]
The partition just proved makes \(C\) the unique empirical component associated
with the true support.  The same partition argument for \(\xi\) gives
\[
\operatorname{supp}(\xi)\subseteq K_C^\xi.
\]
Thus
\[
\xi(K_C^\xi)=1=\nu_P(K_C^P).
\]

Now suppose \(\Delta_C(P)<+\infty\).  The finite sets \(K_C^P\) and
\(\operatorname{supp}(\nu_P)\setminus K_C^P\) are nonempty in this case, so
\[
\Delta_C^{\mathrm r}>0.
\]
Let \(\gamma\) be any transport plan from \(\xi\) to \(\nu_P\).  If
\[
\mathscr A_C^\xi:=\{i:x_i^\xi\in K_C^\xi\},
\qquad
\mathscr A_C^P:=\{j:x_j^P\in K_C^P\},
\]
then marginal balance gives
\[
\xi(K_C^\xi)-\nu_P(K_C^P)
=
\sum_{i\in\mathscr A_C^\xi}\sum_{j\notin\mathscr A_C^P}\gamma_{ij}
-
\sum_{i\notin\mathscr A_C^\xi}\sum_{j\in\mathscr A_C^P}\gamma_{ij}.
\]
Every positive-mass crossing pair in these two sums is separated by at least
\(\Delta_C^{\mathrm r}-2\rho_{n,\alpha}\).  For the first crossing case
\(i\in\mathscr A_C^\xi\), \(j\notin\mathscr A_C^P\), choose
\(z\in C\) with
\[
|x_i^\xi-z|\le\rho_{n,\alpha}.
\]
Since \(z\) is a center atom, support closeness from the center to \(\nu_P\)
gives \(y\in\operatorname{supp}(\nu_P)\) with
\[
|z-y|\le\rho_{n,\alpha}.
\]
Then \(y\in K_C^P\).  Because \(x_j^P\notin K_C^P\), the definition of
\(\Delta_C(P)\) gives
\[
\Delta_C^{\mathrm r}\le |y-x_j^P|.
\]
Also
\[
|y-x_i^\xi|\le |y-z|+|z-x_i^\xi|\le2\rho_{n,\alpha},
\]
and therefore
\[
\Delta_C^{\mathrm r}-2\rho_{n,\alpha}
\le |x_i^\xi-x_j^P|.
\]

For the second crossing case \(i\notin\mathscr A_C^\xi\),
\(j\in\mathscr A_C^P\), support closeness from \(\xi\) to the center gives a
center atom \(z\) with
\[
|x_i^\xi-z|\le\rho_{n,\alpha}.
\]
Let \(C_z\) be the empirical component containing \(z\).  Support closeness from
the center to \(\nu_P\) gives \(y\in\operatorname{supp}(\nu_P)\) with
\[
|z-y|\le\rho_{n,\alpha},
\]
so \(y\in K_{C_z}(P)\).  If \(y\in K_C(P)\), the uniqueness of the true
association would force \(C_z=C\); then \(z\in C\), and
\[
\operatorname{dist}(x_i^\xi,C)\le |x_i^\xi-z|\le\rho_{n,\alpha},
\]
contradicting \(i\notin\mathscr A_C^\xi\).  Hence
\[
y\notin K_C(P).
\]
Since \(x_j^P\in K_C(P)\), the external-gap definition yields
\[
\Delta_C^{\mathrm r}\le |x_j^P-y|.
\]
Together with
\[
|y-x_i^\xi|\le |y-z|+|z-x_i^\xi|\le2\rho_{n,\alpha},
\]
this gives
\[
\Delta_C^{\mathrm r}-2\rho_{n,\alpha}
\le |x_i^\xi-x_j^P|.
\]
The preceding crossing estimate and the displayed marginal identity imply
\[
(\Delta_C^{\mathrm r}-2\rho_{n,\alpha})
\left|\xi(K_C^\xi)-\nu_P(K_C^P)\right|
\le
\operatorname{cost}(\gamma).
\]
Taking an optimal plan and using \(W_1(\xi,\nu_P)\le2R_{n,\alpha}\), if
\(4\rho_{n,\alpha}<\Delta_C^{\mathrm r}\), then
\[
\left|\xi(K_C^\xi)-\nu_P(K_C^P)\right|
\le
\frac{2R_{n,\alpha}}{\Delta_C^{\mathrm r}-2\rho_{n,\alpha}}
\le
\frac{4R_{n,\alpha}}{\Delta_C^{\mathrm r}}.
\]
Since \(4\rho_{n,\alpha}=4R_{n,\alpha}/\pi_0<\Delta_C^{\mathrm r}\) and
\(\pi_0\le1\), this is bounded by
\[
\frac{4}{\pi_0}
\min\left\{1,\frac{R_{n,\alpha}}{\Delta_C^{\mathrm r}}\right\}.
\]
If \(4\rho_{n,\alpha}\ge\Delta_C^{\mathrm r}\), then both cluster masses lie in
\([0,1]\), so their difference has absolute value at most \(1\).  When
\(R_{n,\alpha}/\Delta_C^{\mathrm r}\ge1\), this is at most \(4/\pi_0\).  When
\(R_{n,\alpha}/\Delta_C^{\mathrm r}<1\), the inequality
\(\Delta_C^{\mathrm r}\le4R_{n,\alpha}/\pi_0\) gives
\[
\frac{\pi_0}{4}\le\frac{R_{n,\alpha}}{\Delta_C^{\mathrm r}},
\]
and hence
\[
1\le
\frac{4}{\pi_0}\frac{R_{n,\alpha}}{\Delta_C^{\mathrm r}}.
\]
Thus, in all finite-gap cases,
\[
\left|\xi(K_C(\xi))-\nu_P(K_C(P))\right|
\le
\frac{4}{\pi_0}
\min\left\{1,\frac{R_{n,\alpha}}{\Delta_C(P)}\right\},
\]
with the top-gap case giving equality of the two masses. % lean: cluster_candidate_mass_error

\item For a fixed component \(C\), let
\[
\operatorname{Val}(C)
=
\left\{v:\exists \xi\in\mathcal C^{\mathrm{alg}}_{n,\alpha},\
v=\xi(K_C(\xi))\right\}.
\]
The preceding step bounds every \(v\in\operatorname{Val}(C)\) within
\[
B_C^{\mathrm{err}}
:=
\frac{4}{\pi_0}
\min\left\{1,\frac{R_{n,\alpha}}{\Delta_C(P)}\right\}
\]
of \(\nu_P(K_C(P))\) when \(\Delta_C(P)<+\infty\), and within \(0\) when
\(\Delta_C(P)=+\infty\).  Since
\(\nu_P(K_C(P))\in\operatorname{Val}(C)\), the elementary extremal implication
\[
\bigl(|v-v_0|\le B\ \text{for all }v\in\operatorname{Val}(C),\
v_0\in\operatorname{Val}(C)\bigr)
\Longrightarrow
\sup\operatorname{Val}(C)-\inf\operatorname{Val}(C)\le2B
\]
applies to the endpoints of \(I_C\).  Therefore, if \(\Delta_C(P)=+\infty\),
then
\[
\operatorname{length}(I_C)=0,
\]
and, for every component,
\[
\operatorname{length}(I_C)
\le
\frac{8}{\pi_0}
\min\left\{1,\frac{R_{n,\alpha}}{\Delta_C(P)}\right\},
\]
with the displayed right side read as zero when
\(\Delta_C(P)=+\infty\). % lean: cluster_deterministic_report

\item If \(K_C(P)=\{x\}\), every center atom \(z\in C\) is within
\(\rho_{n,\alpha}\) of a true atom \(y\).  That true atom satisfies
\(y\in K_C(P)\), and hence \(y=x\).  Thus
\[
|z-x|\le\rho_{n,\alpha}\qquad(z\in C).
\]
Applying this to the minimum and maximum points of the finite component \(C\)
gives
\[
\max C-\min C\le2\rho_{n,\alpha}.
\]
The reported support interval therefore has length
\[
(\max C+\rho_{n,\alpha})-(\min C-\rho_{n,\alpha})
\le4\rho_{n,\alpha}. % lean: cluster_deterministic_report
\]

\item In the same singleton case, the external gap dominates the global effect
gap.  If \(\Delta_C(P)<+\infty\), then for every
\(y\in\operatorname{supp}(\nu_P)\setminus K_C(P)\),
\[
\delta(P)\le |x-y|,
\]
because \(x\) and \(y\) are distinct positive-mass support points of
\(\nu_P\).  Taking the infimum over such \(y\) gives
\[
\delta(P)\le \Delta_C(P).
\]
If \(\Delta_C(P)=+\infty\), the preceding zero-width conclusion applies.  Since
\(R_{n,\alpha}\ge0\) and \(\pi_0>0\), substituting the finite-gap comparison
into the external-gap interval bound gives
\[
\operatorname{length}(I_C)
\le
\frac{8}{\pi_0}
\min\left\{1,\frac{R_{n,\alpha}}{\delta(P)}\right\}. % lean: cluster_singleton_width_from_external
\]

\item Finally, \cref{obj:def:wasserstein-distance} defines \(W_1\) as the
minimum finite transport cost over couplings with the prescribed marginals.
Therefore, for every represented atomic law \(\xi\),
\[
\xi\in\mathcal C^{\mathrm{alg}}_{n,\alpha}
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\operatorname{AtomFloor}(\pi_0,\xi)
\ \text{and}\
\exists\gamma\ \text{with transport cost at most }R_{n,\alpha}
\text{ from }\xi\text{ to }\widehat\lambda_n(\omega).
\]
The forward implication takes an optimal finite transport plan attaining
\(W_1\); the reverse implication uses that any feasible plan upper-bounds the
minimum.  This is the finite constrained representation of
\(\mathcal C^{\mathrm{alg}}_{n,\alpha}\). % lean: confidenceSets_constrainedRepresentation
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:thm:labeled-weight-upper}]
\begin{enumerate}
\item
Let \(C_{\mathrm{quo}}>0\) be the constant supplied by \cref{obj:thm:collision-uniform-root-n}.  Define
\[
\chi_{\mathrm{tail}}:=\max\{C_{\mathrm{quo}},2\},\qquad
\beta_{\mathrm{mean}}
:=\chi_{\mathrm{tail}}\sqrt{\log(2\chi_{\mathrm{tail}})}
+\frac{\chi_{\mathrm{tail}}^2\sqrt{\pi}}{2},
\]
\[
\kappa_{\mathrm{ord}}:=8+\frac{16}{\pi_0},
\qquad
C:=\max\{2,\kappa_{\mathrm{ord}}\beta_{\mathrm{mean}}\}.
\]
The hypotheses give \(\chi_{\mathrm{tail}}\ge2\), \(\log(2\chi_{\mathrm{tail}})>0\), \(\beta_{\mathrm{mean}}>0\), and \(\kappa_{\mathrm{ord}}>0\), hence \(C>0\).  Fix \(n\ge1\).  By \cref{obj:thm:collision-uniform-root-n}, choose the measurable nearest-summary quotient-law estimator \(\widehat\nu_n\) from \cref{obj:def:summary-space-repair}.  Set
\[
\widehat p_n^{\uparrow}(\omega):=
p^{\uparrow}\!\left(\widehat\nu_n(\omega)\right),
\qquad \omega\in O_{d_x,d_z}^n ,
\]
which is exactly the ordered-weight estimator of \cref{obj:def:labeled-weight-estimator}.  Its measurability follows from the measurability of \(\widehat\nu_n\) and the Borel ordered-mass map; its values lie in the probability simplex by the ordered-mass construction, including its barycentric fallback branch. % lean: labeled_weight_upper

\item
Fix \(g\) with \(0<g\le1/4\), and fix \(P\in\mathcal M(g)\).  Write
\[
Z_P(\omega):=
W_1\!\left(\widehat\nu_n(\omega),\nu_P\right),
\qquad \omega\in O_{d_x,d_z}^n ,
\]
where \(\nu_P\) is the quotient latent-effect law of \cref{obj:def:quotient-law}.  The support radius \(L_\tau=4L\sqrt{d_z}/\sigma_0\) gives
\[
0\le Z_P(\omega)\le 2L_\tau
\]
for every \(\omega\), so \(Z_P\) is integrable under \(Q_P^{(n)}\).  For every \(0<\eta<1/2\), the tail bound in \cref{obj:thm:collision-uniform-root-n} and the inequality \(C_{\mathrm{quo}}\le\chi_{\mathrm{tail}}\) give
\[
Q_P^{(n)}\!\left\{
\chi_{\mathrm{tail}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(\chi_{\mathrm{tail}}/\eta)}{n}}
< Z_P
\right\}
\le \eta .
\]
Indeed, the displayed event is contained in the corresponding collision-uniform event because
\[
C_{\mathrm{quo}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(C_{\mathrm{quo}}/\eta)}{n}}
\le
\chi_{\mathrm{tail}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(\chi_{\mathrm{tail}}/\eta)}{n}} .
\]
To integrate this tail, put
\[
b_{\mathrm{mean}}:=
\chi_{\mathrm{tail}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(2\chi_{\mathrm{tail}})}{n}},
\qquad
\varrho_{\mathrm{mean}}:=\frac{n}{\chi_{\mathrm{tail}}^2}.
\]
For \(x>b_{\mathrm{mean}}\), define
\[
\eta_x:=\chi_{\mathrm{tail}}\exp(-\varrho_{\mathrm{mean}}x^2).
\]
Then \(0<\eta_x<1/2\) and
\[
\chi_{\mathrm{tail}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(\chi_{\mathrm{tail}}/\eta_x)}{n}}=x.
\]
Thus
\[
Q_P^{(n)}\{Z_P>x\}\le
\chi_{\mathrm{tail}}\exp(-\varrho_{\mathrm{mean}}x^2),
\qquad x>b_{\mathrm{mean}}.
\]
The layer-cake identity and the Gaussian tail bound yield
\[
E_{Q_P^{(n)}}[Z_P]
\le
b_{\mathrm{mean}}
+\chi_{\mathrm{tail}}\int_{b_{\mathrm{mean}}}^{\infty}
\exp(-\varrho_{\mathrm{mean}}x^2)\,dx
\le
\frac{\beta_{\mathrm{mean}}}{\sqrt n}.
\] % lean: integral_le_of_sqrt_log_tail

\item
Let
\[
\nu_P^{\mathrm{slot}}
:=
\bigl((p_u(P))_{u=1}^k,(\tau_u(P))_{u=1}^k\bigr)
\]
be the \(k\)-slot representative of \(\nu_P\).  By \cref{obj:lem:ordered-masses-measure-invariance},
\[
p^{\uparrow}(P)=p^{\uparrow}\!\left(\nu_P^{\mathrm{slot}}\right),
\]
because \(\nu_P^{\mathrm{slot}}\) and \(\nu_P\) represent the same aggregated atomic law.  The gap-stratum assumptions give, for every latent class \(u\),
\[
\pi_0\le P(U=u,T=0)\le P(U=u)=p_u(P),
\]
and \cref{obj:def:gap-stratum} gives distinct latent effects and
\[
\frac g2\le |\tau_u(P)-\tau_v(P)|\qquad (u\ne v).
\]
Consequently, if \(\xi=\sum_{a=1}^k \widetilde p_a\delta_{\widetilde\tau_a}\) is any valid \(k\)-slot atomic law and
\[
W_1\!\left(\nu_P^{\mathrm{slot}},\xi\right)
<
\frac{\pi_0(g/2)}{4},
\]
then
\[
\sum_{j=1}^k
\left|p_j^{\uparrow}(\xi)-p_j^{\uparrow}(\nu_P^{\mathrm{slot}})\right|
\le
\frac{8}{g}\,
W_1\!\left(\nu_P^{\mathrm{slot}},\xi\right).
\]
For this last implication, take an optimal transport plan \(\gamma\).  Each true atom \(\tau_u(P)\) must have a positive-mass atom \(\widetilde\tau_{\varphi(u)}\) of \(\xi\) within distance \(g/8\); otherwise the mass \(p_u(P)\ge\pi_0\) transported from that atom alone would cost at least \(\pi_0(g/2)/4\).  The map \(\varphi\) is injective, hence bijective, because two \(g/8\)-close images of distinct \(g/2\)-separated true atoms would violate the triangle inequality.  It also preserves the increasing order.  Therefore
\[
\sum_{j=1}^k
\left|p_j^{\uparrow}(\xi)-p_j^{\uparrow}(\nu_P^{\mathrm{slot}})\right|
=
\sum_{u=1}^k
\left|\widetilde p_{\varphi(u)}-p_u(P)\right|.
\]
With
\[
\operatorname{diag}_{\gamma}:=\sum_{u=1}^k\gamma_{u,\varphi(u)},
\qquad
\operatorname{off}_{\gamma}:=1-\operatorname{diag}_{\gamma},
\]
the row and column marginal identities give
\[
\sum_{u=1}^k
\left|\widetilde p_{\varphi(u)}-p_u(P)\right|
\le 2\,\operatorname{off}_{\gamma}.
\]
All off-matching mass moves at least \(g/4\), so
\[
\frac g4\,\operatorname{off}_{\gamma}
\le W_1\!\left(\nu_P^{\mathrm{slot}},\xi\right),
\]
and the displayed local stability bound follows. % lean: gapStratum_orderedMasses_l1_le

\item
Apply the preceding bound to the representative \(\xi_\omega\) of \(\widehat\nu_n(\omega)\).  Since \(\xi_\omega\) and \(\widehat\nu_n(\omega)\) represent the same law,
\[
W_1\!\left(\nu_P^{\mathrm{slot}},\xi_\omega\right)=Z_P(\omega).
\]
If
\[
Z_P(\omega)<\frac{\pi_0(g/2)}4,
\]
then
\[
\sum_{j=1}^k
\left|\widehat p_{n,j}^{\uparrow}(\omega)-p_j^{\uparrow}(P)\right|
\le
\frac{8 Z_P(\omega)}{g}
\le
\frac{\kappa_{\mathrm{ord}} Z_P(\omega)}{g}.
\]
If instead \(Z_P(\omega)\ge \pi_0(g/2)/4\), the simplex diameter gives
\[
\sum_{j=1}^k
\left|\widehat p_{n,j}^{\uparrow}(\omega)-p_j^{\uparrow}(P)\right|
\le 2
\le
\frac{16}{\pi_0}\frac{Z_P(\omega)}{g}
\le
\frac{\kappa_{\mathrm{ord}} Z_P(\omega)}{g}.
\]
Thus, for every sample \(\omega\),
\[
\sum_{j=1}^k
\left|\widehat p_{n,j}^{\uparrow}(\omega)-p_j^{\uparrow}(P)\right|
\le
\frac{\kappa_{\mathrm{ord}} Z_P(\omega)}{g}.
\] % lean: labeled_weight_upper

\item
Integrating the samplewise inequality and using the mean bound for \(Z_P\),
\[
E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[
\left\|\widehat p_n^{\uparrow}-p^{\uparrow}(P)\right\|_1
\right]
\le
\frac{\kappa_{\mathrm{ord}}}{g}E_{Q_P^{(n)}}[Z_P]
\le
\kappa_{\mathrm{ord}}\beta_{\mathrm{mean}}\,
(\sqrt n\,g)^{-1}.
\]
Independently, both \(\widehat p_n^{\uparrow}(\omega)\) and \(p^{\uparrow}(P)\) lie in the simplex, hence
\[
\left\|\widehat p_n^{\uparrow}(\omega)-p^{\uparrow}(P)\right\|_1\le2
\]
for every \(\omega\), and therefore
\[
E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[
\left\|\widehat p_n^{\uparrow}-p^{\uparrow}(P)\right\|_1
\right]\le2 .
\] % lean: simplex_l1_le_two

\item
If \((\sqrt n\,g)^{-1}\le1\), the preceding rate bound and
\(\kappa_{\mathrm{ord}}\beta_{\mathrm{mean}}\le C\) give
\[
E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[
\left\|\widehat p_n^{\uparrow}-p^{\uparrow}(P)\right\|_1
\right]
\le
C(\sqrt n\,g)^{-1}
=
C\min\{1,(\sqrt n\,g)^{-1}\}.
\]
If \(1\le(\sqrt n\,g)^{-1}\), the simplex-diameter bound and \(2\le C\) give
\[
E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[
\left\|\widehat p_n^{\uparrow}-p^{\uparrow}(P)\right\|_1
\right]
\le
C
=
C\min\{1,(\sqrt n\,g)^{-1}\}.
\]
The constant \(C\) depends only on \(k,d_x,d_z,L,\pi_0,\sigma_0\), and the construction above supplies the required estimator for every \(n\ge1\). % lean: labeled_weight_upper
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:prop:two-class-witness-valid}]
\begin{enumerate}
\item Fix \(0\le \varepsilon\le 1/8\).  For \(\mathfrak b\in\{0,1\}\), write
\[
\operatorname{Ber}_{\theta}(\mathfrak b)
=
\begin{cases}
\theta,& \mathfrak b=1,\\
1-\theta,& \mathfrak b=0.
\end{cases}
\]
The law in \cref{obj:def:two-class-witness} is the finite law on points
\[
X=(1,\mathfrak x)^\top,\qquad
Z=(1,\mathfrak z)^\top,\qquad
Y(0)=\mathfrak y_0,\qquad
Y(1)=\mathfrak y_1,\qquad
Y=Y(T),
\]
where \(\mathfrak t,\mathfrak x,\mathfrak z,\mathfrak y_0,\mathfrak y_1\in\{0,1\}\).  Its elementary mass at
\((u,\mathfrak t,\mathfrak x,\mathfrak z,\mathfrak y_0,\mathfrak y_1)\), for \(u\in\{1,2\}\), is
\[
\theta^U_u
\operatorname{Ber}_{\theta^T_u}(\mathfrak t)
\operatorname{Ber}_{\theta^X_u}(\mathfrak x)
\operatorname{Ber}_{\theta^Z_{u,\mathfrak t}}(\mathfrak z)
\operatorname{Ber}_{1/4}(\mathfrak y_0)
\operatorname{Ber}_{\theta^1_u}(\mathfrak y_1),
\]
with
\[
(\theta^U_1,\theta^U_2)=\left(\frac25,\frac35\right),\quad
(\theta^T_1,\theta^T_2)=\left(\frac25,\frac35\right),\quad
(\theta^X_1,\theta^X_2)=\left(\frac15,\frac45\right),
\]
\[
\theta^Z_{1,0}=\frac3{10},\quad
\theta^Z_{2,0}=\frac7{10},\quad
\theta^Z_{1,1}=\frac7{20},\quad
\theta^Z_{2,1}=\frac34,\quad
\theta^1_1=\frac12-\varepsilon,\quad
\theta^1_2=\frac12+\varepsilon .
\]
The perturbation range gives
\[
\frac38\le \frac12-\varepsilon\le \frac12,
\qquad
\frac12\le \frac12+\varepsilon\le \frac58,
\]
so every displayed Bernoulli factor is a probability mass function.  Summing successively over
\(\mathfrak y_1,\mathfrak y_0,\mathfrak z,\mathfrak x,\mathfrak t\) leaves \(\theta^U_u\), and
\(\theta^U_1+\theta^U_2=1\).  Thus \(P_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{wit}}\) is a probability law. % lean: witnessLaw_isProbabilityMeasure

\item The parameter domain in \cref{obj:def:model-class} is satisfied because
\[
2\le 2,\qquad 2\le 2,\qquad 2\le 2,\qquad
1\le 2,\qquad
0<\frac1{10}\le \frac1{2\cdot2},\qquad
0<\frac1{10}\le 1 .
\]
The finite product form gives the conditional factorization requirements.  For bounded measurable
test functions \(\psi_Z,\psi_{XY},\psi_X,\psi_{YT},\psi_{Y_t},\psi_T\),
\[
E[\psi_Z(Z)\psi_{XY}(X,Y)\mid U=u,T=t]
=
E[\psi_Z(Z)\mid U=u,T=t]\,
E[\psi_{XY}(X,Y)\mid U=u,T=t],
\]
\[
E[\psi_X(X)\psi_{YT}(Y,T)\mid U=u]
=
E[\psi_X(X)\mid U=u]\,
E[\psi_{YT}(Y,T)\mid U=u],
\]
and
\[
E[\psi_{Y_t}(Y(t))\psi_T(T)\mid U=u]
=
E[\psi_{Y_t}(Y(t))\mid U=u]\,
E[\psi_T(T)\mid U=u].
\]
These identities give \cref{obj:ass:reference-proxy-separation,obj:ass:target-proxy-separation,obj:ass:latent-ignorability}.  The construction gives \(Y=Y(T)\) and \(X_1=1\) almost surely, which are \cref{obj:ass:consistency,obj:ass:anchor}.  Since \(X,Z\in\{(1,0)^\top,(1,1)^\top\}\) and \(Y\in\{0,1\}\),
\[
\|X\|_2\le \sqrt2\le 2,\qquad
\|ZX^\top\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le \|Z\|_2\|X\|_2\le 2,\qquad
\|YZX^\top\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le 2,
\]
so \cref{obj:ass:bounded-x,obj:ass:bounded-proxy-product,obj:ass:bounded-outcome-proxy-product} hold with \(L=2\).  The latent-arm masses are
\[
P_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{wit}}(U=1,T=0)=\frac6{25},\quad
P_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{wit}}(U=1,T=1)=\frac4{25},\quad
P_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{wit}}(U=2,T=0)=\frac6{25},\quad
P_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{wit}}(U=2,T=1)=\frac9{25},
\]
and each is at least \(1/10\), giving \cref{obj:ass:latent-arm-positivity}.  The feature matrices are
\[
B=\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\[2pt]1/5&4/5\end{pmatrix},\qquad
A_0=\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\[2pt]3/10&7/10\end{pmatrix},\qquad
A_1=\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\[2pt]7/20&3/4\end{pmatrix}.
\]
For every \(\upsilon=(\upsilon_1,\upsilon_2)\in\mathbb R^2\),
\[
\frac1{100}\|\upsilon\|_2^2\le \|B\upsilon\|_2^2,\qquad
\frac1{100}\|\upsilon\|_2^2\le \|A_0\upsilon\|_2^2,\qquad
\frac1{100}\|\upsilon\|_2^2\le \|A_1\upsilon\|_2^2.
\]
Indeed, after multiplying the three differences by \(100,100,400\), respectively, the differences equal
\[
103\left(\upsilon_1+\frac{116}{103}\upsilon_2\right)^2
+\frac{3333}{103}\upsilon_2^2,
\]
\[
108\left(\upsilon_1+\frac{121}{108}\upsilon_2\right)^2
+\frac{1343}{108}\upsilon_2^2,
\]
and
\[
445\left(\upsilon_1+\frac{505}{445}\upsilon_2\right)^2
+\frac{4264}{89}\upsilon_2^2.
\]
Thus the smallest singular value of each of \(B,A_0,A_1\) is at least \(1/10\), proving \cref{obj:ass:proxy-rank-margin}.  Hence
\[
P_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{wit}}\in\mathcal M
\quad\text{with}\quad
L=2,\quad \pi_0=\frac1{10},\quad \sigma_0=\frac1{10}.
\] % lean: witness_ucvmwModel

\item \Cref{obj:lem:witness-observed-arm-mass} gives
\[
P_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{wit}}(T=0)=\frac{12}{25}=0.48,\qquad
P_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{wit}}(T=1)=\frac{13}{25}=0.52 .
\]
Also, \cref{obj:lem:witness-target-feature-determinant,obj:lem:witness-reference-feature-determinant} give
\[
\det(B)=\frac35=0.6,\qquad
\det(A_0)=\det(A_1)=\frac25=0.4 .
\]
It remains to compute the observable proxy moment determinants.  For \(\iota,\jmath\in\{1,2\}\) and
\(\mathfrak t\in\{0,1\}\), put
\[
x_1(\mathfrak x)=1,\qquad x_2(\mathfrak x)=\mathfrak x,\qquad
z_1(\mathfrak z)=1,\qquad z_2(\mathfrak z)=\mathfrak z .
\]
After summing out \(\mathfrak y_0\) and \(\mathfrak y_1\), \cref{obj:def:observable-population-moments} gives the conditional-normalized finite sum
\[
M_{\mathfrak t}(P_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{wit}})_{\iota\jmath}
=
\frac{
\sum_{u=1}^{2}\sum_{\mathfrak x,\mathfrak z\in\{0,1\}}
\theta^U_u\operatorname{Ber}_{\theta^T_u}(\mathfrak t)
\operatorname{Ber}_{\theta^X_u}(\mathfrak x)
\operatorname{Ber}_{\theta^Z_{u,\mathfrak t}}(\mathfrak z)
z_\iota(\mathfrak z)x_\jmath(\mathfrak x)}
{\sum_{u=1}^{2}\theta^U_u\operatorname{Ber}_{\theta^T_u}(\mathfrak t)} .
\]
Since the \(\mathfrak x\)- and \(\mathfrak z\)-sums are Bernoulli means, this is
\[
M_{\mathfrak t}(P_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{wit}})_{\iota\jmath}
=
\sum_{u=1}^{2}
P_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{wit}}(U=u\mid T=\mathfrak t)\,
A_{\mathfrak t}(\iota,u)B(\jmath,u).
\]
For \(\mathfrak t=0\), the conditional latent weights are \((1/2,1/2)\), and for
\(\mathfrak t=1\) they are \((4/13,9/13)\).  Therefore
\[
M_0(P_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{wit}})
=
\begin{pmatrix}
1&1/2\\[2pt]
1/2&31/100
\end{pmatrix},
\qquad
M_1(P_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{wit}})
=
\begin{pmatrix}
1&8/13\\[2pt]
163/260&142/325
\end{pmatrix}.
\]
Taking \(2\times2\) determinants,
\[
\det(M_0(P_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{wit}}))
=
\frac{31}{100}-\frac14
=
\frac3{50}
=
0.06,
\]
and
\[
\det(M_1(P_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{wit}}))
=
\frac{142}{325}-\frac8{13}\frac{163}{260}
=
\frac{216}{4225}
=
\frac25\cdot\frac35\cdot\frac{36}{169}.
\] % lean: witness_observedProxyMoment_det

\item \Cref{obj:lem:witness-latent-mass} gives
\[
p_1(P_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{wit}})=\frac25,\qquad
p_2(P_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{wit}})=\frac35,
\]
and \cref{obj:lem:witness-latent-effect} gives
\[
\tau_1(P_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{wit}})=\frac14-\varepsilon,\qquad
\tau_2(P_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{wit}})=\frac14+\varepsilon .
\]
Since \(\nu_P\) in \cref{obj:def:quotient-law} places latent mass \(p_u\) at latent effect \(\tau_u\),
\[
\nu_{P_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{wit}}}
=
\frac25\,\delta_{1/4-\varepsilon}
+
\frac35\,\delta_{1/4+\varepsilon}
=
0.4\,\delta_{0.25-\varepsilon}
+
0.6\,\delta_{0.25+\varepsilon}.
\] % lean: quotientLaw

\item At \(\varepsilon=0\), the singular-value inequalities in the second step give injectivity, hence
nonsingularity, of \(B,A_0,A_1\).  The determinant identities in the third step give nonsingularity of
\(M_0(P_0^{\mathrm{wit}})\) and \(M_1(P_0^{\mathrm{wit}})\).  The quotient-law identity in the fourth step becomes
\[
\nu_{P_0^{\mathrm{wit}}}
=
\frac25\,\delta_{1/4}+\frac35\,\delta_{1/4}
=
\delta_{1/4}
=
\delta_{0.25}.
\]
Finally, \cref{obj:lem:witness-latent-mass} gives
\[
p_1(P_0^{\mathrm{wit}})=\frac25,\qquad
p_2(P_0^{\mathrm{wit}})=\frac35,
\]
and these two latent-class masses are distinct. % lean: two_class_witness_valid
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:thm:matching-local-lower-bounds}]
\begin{enumerate}
\item Define
\[
a:=\min\left\{\frac1{3200},\frac{\sqrt{c_{\mathrm{loc}}}}{3200}\right\},
\qquad
c:=\frac a8,
\qquad
C:=16000 .
\]
Since \(0<c_{\mathrm{loc}}<1\),
\[
0<a\le \frac1{3200}\le\frac18,\qquad c>0,\qquad C>0,
\]
and
\[
16000a^2\le c_{\mathrm{loc}},
\qquad
16000a^2\le \frac14\le\frac25 .
\]
Fix \(n\ge1\), and put
\[
\varepsilon_n:=\frac{a}{\sqrt n}.
\]
Then \(0\le\varepsilon_n\le1/8\), and
\[
16000\varepsilon_n^2
=
\frac{16000a^2}{n}
\le
\frac{c_{\mathrm{loc}}}{n}.
\]

Let
\[
\Omega_{\mathrm{vis}}:=\{0,1\}^4,
\qquad
\zeta=(t,x,z,y)\in\Omega_{\mathrm{vis}},
\]
and let
\[
\phi(t,x,z,y):=(t,(1,x)^\top,(1,z)^\top,y)\in O_{2,2},
\]
with \(O_{2,2}\) as in \cref{obj:def:observed-record-space}.  For \(q\in[0,1]\) write
\[
\operatorname{Bern}_q(1)=q,\qquad
\operatorname{Bern}_q(0)=1-q .
\]
For \(0\le\varepsilon\le1/8\), define the finite visible witness cell mass by
\[
\psi^{\mathrm{wit}}_{\varepsilon}(t,x,z,y)
=
\sum_{u=1}^{2}
p_u\,\operatorname{Bern}_{\theta_u}(t)\,
\operatorname{Bern}_{\beta_u}(x)\,
\operatorname{Bern}_{\alpha_{t,u}}(z)
\begin{cases}
\operatorname{Bern}_{1/4}(y),& t=0,\\
\operatorname{Bern}_{1/2+\operatorname{sgn}_u\varepsilon}(y),& t=1,
\end{cases}
\]
where
\[
(p_1,p_2)=\left(\frac25,\frac35\right),\quad
(\theta_1,\theta_2)=\left(\frac25,\frac35\right),\quad
(\beta_1,\beta_2)=\left(\frac15,\frac45\right),
\]
\[
(\alpha_{0,1},\alpha_{0,2})=\left(\frac3{10},\frac7{10}\right),\quad
(\alpha_{1,1},\alpha_{1,2})=\left(\frac7{20},\frac34\right),\quad
(\operatorname{sgn}_1,\operatorname{sgn}_2)=(-1,1).
\]
The pushforward of this finite law by \(\phi\) is \((P_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{wit}})_O\), the observed-data marginal of \cref{obj:def:observed-margin}.  At \(\varepsilon=0\), every visible cell is bounded below by the first-class contribution:
\[
\psi^{\mathrm{wit}}_{0}(\zeta)
\ge
\frac25\cdot\frac25\cdot\frac15\cdot\frac14\cdot\frac14
=
\frac1{500}
\ge
\frac1{1000}.
\]
Only the treated outcome factor varies with \(\varepsilon\), and hence
\[
\left|\psi^{\mathrm{wit}}_{\varepsilon}(\zeta)
-\psi^{\mathrm{wit}}_{0}(\zeta)\right|
\le \varepsilon .
\]
For Kullback--Leibler divergence as in \cref{obj:synth_6}, the elementary finite-carrier comparison is
\[
D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\mathfrak r\Vert\mathfrak s)
\le
\sum_{\zeta}\frac{(\mathfrak r(\zeta)-\mathfrak s(\zeta))^2}{\mathfrak s(\zeta)}
\]
whenever \(\mathfrak r\) and \(\mathfrak s\) are probability vectors on a finite carrier and
\(\mathfrak s(\zeta)>0\) for every \(\zeta\); this follows from \(\log x\le x-1\).  Therefore
\[
D_{\mathrm{KL}}\!\left((P_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{wit}})_O\Vert(P_0^{\mathrm{wit}})_O\right)
\le
\sum_{\zeta\in\Omega_{\mathrm{vis}}}
\frac{\{\psi^{\mathrm{wit}}_{\varepsilon}(\zeta)-\psi^{\mathrm{wit}}_{0}(\zeta)\}^2}
{\psi^{\mathrm{wit}}_{0}(\zeta)}
\le
16\cdot1000\,\varepsilon^2
=
16000\varepsilon^2,
\]
where the first inequality also uses data processing under \(\phi\).  Taking
\(\varepsilon=\varepsilon_n\), \cref{obj:prop:two-class-witness-valid} gives
\(P_0^{\mathrm{wit}},P_{\varepsilon_n}^{\mathrm{wit}}\in\mathcal M\), and the displayed KL bound places both laws in \(\mathcal L_n^\nu\) from \cref{obj:def:local-quotient-experiment}. % lean: witnessLaw_observed_kl_bound

\item We use the following two-point expected-risk inequality.  Let \(\mathsf R_1\ll\mathsf R_0\) be one-observation laws with integrable log-likelihood ratio and suppose
\[
nD_{\mathrm{KL}}(\mathsf R_1\Vert\mathsf R_0)\le\frac25 .
\]
Tensorization and Pinsker's inequality give
\[
\left\|\mathsf R_1^{\otimes n}-\mathsf R_0^{\otimes n}\right\|_{\mathrm{TV}}
\le
\sqrt{\frac12D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\mathsf R_1^{\otimes n}\Vert\mathsf R_0^{\otimes n})}
\le\frac12 .
\]
For an estimator \(\widehat\theta\) in a metric space with targets \(\Theta_1,\Theta_0\), set
\[
\Delta:=d(\Theta_1,\Theta_0),
\qquad
\mathcal B_1:=\left\{d(\widehat\theta,\Theta_1)\ge\frac{\Delta}{2}\right\},
\qquad
\mathcal B_0:=\left\{d(\widehat\theta,\Theta_0)\ge\frac{\Delta}{2}\right\}.
\]
The triangle inequality gives \(\mathcal B_1^{c}\subseteq\mathcal B_0\) when \(\Delta>0\), and the case \(\Delta=0\) is immediate.  Thus
\[
\mathsf R_1^{\otimes n}(\mathcal B_1)+\mathsf R_0^{\otimes n}(\mathcal B_0)
\ge
1-\left\|\mathsf R_1^{\otimes n}-\mathsf R_0^{\otimes n}\right\|_{\mathrm{TV}}
\ge\frac12,
\]
so one of the two probabilities is at least \(1/4\).  Since
\[
\frac{\Delta}{2}\,\Pr\!\left\{d(\widehat\theta,\Theta_i)\ge\frac{\Delta}{2}\right\}
\le
E\,d(\widehat\theta,\Theta_i),
\]
one of the two expected risks is at least \(\Delta/8\).  For simplex-valued estimators the same argument applied to one coordinate gives
\[
\frac{|p_1(j)-p_0(j)|}{8}
\le
E_{\mathsf R_1^{\otimes n}}\!\left[\|\widehat p-p_1\|_1\right]
\quad\text{or}\quad
\frac{|p_1(j)-p_0(j)|}{8}
\le
E_{\mathsf R_0^{\otimes n}}\!\left[\|\widehat p-p_0\|_1\right],
\]
because \(|\widehat p(j)-p_i(j)|\le\|\widehat p-p_i\|_1\).  The \(W_1\) loss is bounded on the stated compact support radius, and the simplex \(\ell^1\) loss is bounded by \(2\), so the expectations used below are finite. % lean: twoPoint_expectedMetricRisk_lower

\item Apply the two-point inequality with
\[
\mathsf R_1=(P_{\varepsilon_n}^{\mathrm{wit}})_O,
\qquad
\mathsf R_0=(P_0^{\mathrm{wit}})_O .
\]
The KL calculation gives
\[
nD_{\mathrm{KL}}(\mathsf R_1\Vert\mathsf R_0)
\le
16000a^2
\le
\frac25 .
\]
By \cref{obj:prop:two-class-witness-valid},
\[
\nu_{P_0^{\mathrm{wit}}}=\delta_{1/4},
\qquad
\nu_{P_{\varepsilon_n}^{\mathrm{wit}}}
=
\frac25\delta_{1/4-\varepsilon_n}
+
\frac35\delta_{1/4+\varepsilon_n}.
\]
Every coupling from the point mass at \(1/4\) to the second law transports total mass one over distance \(\varepsilon_n\).  Hence, using \cref{obj:def:wasserstein-distance},
\[
W_1\!\left(\nu_{P_0^{\mathrm{wit}}},\nu_{P_{\varepsilon_n}^{\mathrm{wit}}}\right)
=
\varepsilon_n
=
\frac a{\sqrt n}.
\]
Thus every measurable quotient-law estimator \(\widetilde\nu_n\) has one of the two local witness laws satisfying
\[
E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[W_1(\widetilde\nu_n,\nu_P)\right]
\ge
\frac{\varepsilon_n}{8}
=
\frac{c}{\sqrt n}.
\]
This proves the quotient-law risk assertion. % lean: witness_quotientLaw_wass1

\item Fix \(g\in(0,1/4]\), and define
\[
h:=h(n,g):=a\min\left\{1,\frac1{\sqrt n\,g}\right\},
\]
the calibrated displacement of \cref{obj:synth_31}.  Since \(n\ge1\) and \(g>0\),
\[
x_{n,g}:=\sqrt n\,g>0,
\qquad
0<\min\{1,x_{n,g}^{-1}\}\le1.
\]
Together with \(a>0\), this gives
\[
0<h\le a\le\frac1{3200}\le\frac1{100},
\qquad
|h|\le\frac1{100}.
\]
In particular \(h\in[-1,1]\), so \(h\) lies in the tangent-amplitude domain.  Moreover,
\[
x_{n,g}\min\{1,x_{n,g}^{-1}\}\le1,
\]
and therefore
\[
n g^2 h^2
=
a^2\left(x_{n,g}\min\{1,x_{n,g}^{-1}\}\right)^2
\le
a^2 .
\]
Consequently
\[
16000g^2h^2\le\frac{c_{\mathrm{loc}}}{n}.
\]
The hypotheses of \cref{obj:lem:path-local-weight-experiment} are satisfied for \(P_{g,h}\), and also for \(P_{g,0}\).  Hence
\[
P_{g,h},P_{g,0}\in\mathcal L_{n,g}^{p}.
\] % lean: calibratedDisplacement_sample_signal_sq_le

\item We prove the labelled-path KL and ordered-mass certificates.  For \((t,x,z,y)\in\Omega_{\mathrm{vis}}\), define
\[
\psi^{\mathrm{path}}_{g,h}(t,x,z,y)
=
\begin{cases}
\displaystyle
\sum_{u=1}^{2}\sum_{y_0=0}^{1}
P_{g,h}(U=u,T=1,X_2=x,Z_2=z,Y(0)=y_0,Y(1)=y),
& t=1,\\[1.2em]
\displaystyle
\sum_{u=1}^{2}\sum_{y_1=0}^{1}
P_{g,h}(U=u,T=0,X_2=x,Z_2=z,Y(0)=y,Y(1)=y_1),
& t=0.
\end{cases}
\]
The pushforward of this finite law by \(\phi\) is \((P_{g,h})_O\).  At \(h=0\), \cref{obj:synth_14} gives \(P_{g,0}=P_{g/2}^{\mathrm{wit}}\).  For \(t=0\), the first latent class contributes at least
\[
\frac6{25}\cdot\frac15\cdot\frac3{10}\cdot\frac14
=
\frac{18}{5000}
\ge
\frac1{1000}.
\]
For \(t=1\), because \(0\le g\le1/4\), both treated outcome probabilities lie in \([3/8,5/8]\), and the first latent class contributes at least
\[
\frac4{25}\cdot\frac15\cdot\frac7{20}\cdot\frac38
=
\frac{21}{5000}
\ge
\frac1{1000}.
\]
Therefore
\[
\psi^{\mathrm{path}}_{g,0}(t,x,z,y)\ge\frac1{1000}
\qquad
\text{for all }(t,x,z,y)\in\Omega_{\mathrm{vis}} .
\]

For \(|h|\le1/100\), all denominators appearing in \cref{obj:synth_14} are bounded away from zero; for example
\[
2/5+h\ge39/100,\qquad
3/5-h\ge59/100,\qquad
15h+4\ge77/20,\qquad
5h+3\ge59/20 .
\]
Substituting the displayed path probabilities of \cref{obj:synth_14}, summing out the inactive potential outcome, and reducing the four \((x,z)\) cases gives
\[
\sum_{u=1}^{2}P_{g,h}(U=u,T=1,X_2=x,Z_2=z)
=
\sum_{u=1}^{2}P_{g,0}(U=u,T=1,X_2=x,Z_2=z),
\]
and
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\sum_{u=1}^{2}\operatorname{sgn}_u
P_{g,h}(U=u,T=1,X_2=x,Z_2=z)
-
\sum_{u=1}^{2}\operatorname{sgn}_u
P_{g,0}(U=u,T=1,X_2=x,Z_2=z)  \\
&\hspace{3em}=
-2\kappa_{xz}h,
\end{aligned}
\]
where \(\operatorname{sgn}_1=-1\), \(\operatorname{sgn}_2=1\), and
\[
\kappa_{11}=\frac9{100},\qquad
\kappa_{10}=\frac3{100},\qquad
\kappa_{01}=\frac9{25},\qquad
\kappa_{00}=\frac3{25}.
\]
Since the treated outcome mass is
\[
\operatorname{Bern}_{1/2+\operatorname{sgn}_u g/2}(y)
=
\frac12+(2y-1)\frac{\operatorname{sgn}_u g}{2},
\]
the visible-cell displacement is
\[
\psi^{\mathrm{path}}_{g,h}(t,x,z,y)-\psi^{\mathrm{path}}_{g,0}(t,x,z,y)
=
\mathbf 1\{t=1\}(1-2y)\kappa_{xz}gh .
\]
Thus
\[
\left|\psi^{\mathrm{path}}_{g,h}(t,x,z,y)-\psi^{\mathrm{path}}_{g,0}(t,x,z,y)\right|
\le g|h|.
\]
The same finite KL--chi-square bound, now applied with the KL divergence of \cref{obj:synth_6}, and data processing under \(\phi\) give
\[
D_{\mathrm{KL}}\!\left((P_{g,h})_O\Vert(P_{g,0})_O\right)
\le
\sum_{\zeta\in\Omega_{\mathrm{vis}}}
\frac{\{\psi^{\mathrm{path}}_{g,h}(\zeta)-\psi^{\mathrm{path}}_{g,0}(\zeta)\}^2}
{\psi^{\mathrm{path}}_{g,0}(\zeta)}
\le
16000g^2h^2
=
C g^2h^2.
\]

The latent effects on the path are
\[
\tau_1(P_{g,h})=\frac14-\frac g2,
\qquad
\tau_2(P_{g,h})=\frac14+\frac g2,
\]
so their order is fixed for \(g>0\).  Also \(|h|\le1/100\) gives
\[
\frac25+h>0,\qquad \frac35-h>0.
\]
Using \cref{obj:def:ordered-mass-target},
\[
p^{\uparrow}(P_{g,h})=\left(\frac25+h,\frac35-h\right),
\qquad
p^{\uparrow}(P_{g,0})=\left(\frac25,\frac35\right),
\]
and hence
\[
\sum_i\left|p_i^{\uparrow}(P_{g,h})-p_i^{\uparrow}(P_{g,0})\right|
=
|h|+|-h|
=
2|h|.
\] % lean: pathLaw_observed_kl_bound

\item Apply the coordinate form of the two-point inequality to
\[
\mathsf R_1=(P_{g,h})_O,
\qquad
\mathsf R_0=(P_{g,0})_O,
\]
and to the ordered coordinate corresponding to the lower effect.  The preceding bounds give
\[
nD_{\mathrm{KL}}(\mathsf R_1\Vert\mathsf R_0)
\le
16000\,n g^2h^2
\le
16000a^2
\le
\frac25,
\]
and
\[
\left|p^{\uparrow}_1(P_{g,h})-p^{\uparrow}_1(P_{g,0})\right|=h .
\]
Therefore every measurable simplex-valued ordered-weight estimator \(\widetilde p_n\) has one of the two local laws satisfying
\[
E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[\|\widetilde p_n-p^{\uparrow}(P)\|_1\right]
\ge
\frac h8
=
\frac a8\min\left\{1,\frac1{\sqrt n\,g}\right\}
=
c\min\left\{1,\frac1{\sqrt n\,g}\right\}.
\]
The quotient-law certificate follows from the witness KL calculation with \(\varepsilon=\varepsilon_n\):
\[
D_{\mathrm{KL}}\!\left((P_{a/\sqrt n}^{\mathrm{wit}})_O\Vert(P_0^{\mathrm{wit}})_O\right)
\le
16000\frac{a^2}{n}
\le
\frac{16000}{n}
=
\frac Cn,
\]
and from
\[
\frac c{\sqrt n}
\le
\frac a{\sqrt n}
=
W_1\!\left(\nu_{P_0^{\mathrm{wit}}},\nu_{P_{a/\sqrt n}^{\mathrm{wit}}}\right).
\]
The labelled-path membership, KL bound, tangent-amplitude statement, and ordered-mass identity were established above for every \(g\in(0,1/4]\).  All four asserted conclusions hold with the constants \(a,c,C\) fixed at the start. % lean: matching_local_lower_bounds
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:prop:same-class-quotient-minimax}]
Apply \cref{obj:thm:matching-local-lower-bounds} with local radius \(1/4\). It gives a constant \(c_{\nu}>0\) such that, for every \(n\ge 1\) and every measurable quotient-law estimator \(\widetilde\nu_n\), there is a probability law \(P\) in the local quotient experiment \(\mathcal L_n^\nu\) satisfying
\[
  \frac{c_{\nu}}{\sqrt n}
  \le
  E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[
    W_1\!\left(\widetilde\nu_n,\nu_P\right)
  \right].
\]
By \cref{obj:def:local-quotient-experiment}, this law belongs to the fixed model class \(\mathcal M\). Hence
\[
  \sup_{P\in\mathcal M}
  E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[
    W_1\!\left(\widetilde\nu_n,\nu_P\right)
  \right]
  \ge
  \frac{c_{\nu}}{\sqrt n}.
\]
Taking the infimum over the same admissible estimators gives
\[
  \inf_{\widetilde\nu_n}
  \sup_{P\in\mathcal M}
  E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[
    W_1\!\left(\widetilde\nu_n,\nu_P\right)
  \right]
  \ge
  \frac{c_{\nu}}{\sqrt n}.
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.ProxyEffectlawEigencollisionFrontier.matching_local_lower_bounds

\begin{enumerate}
\item
For the upper bound, \cref{obj:thm:collision-uniform-root-n} applies at
\(k=d_x=d_z=2\), \(L=2\), \(\pi_0=1/10\), and \(\sigma_0=1/10\). Thus there is a constant \(C_{\mathrm{col}}>0\) such that, for every \(n\ge 1\), there is a measurable structured-lattice estimator
\[
  \widehat\lambda_n:O_{2,2}^n\to
  \mathcal P_{\le 2}([-L_\tau,L_\tau])
\]
and a measurable repair estimator \(\widehat\nu_n^{\mathrm{rep}}\) such that, for every \(P\in\mathcal M\) and every \(0<\eta<1/2\),
\[
  Q_P^{(n)}\!\left\{
    \max\!\left(
      W_1(\widehat\lambda_n,\nu_P),
      W_1(\widehat\nu_n^{\mathrm{rep}},\nu_P)
    \right)
    >
    C_{\mathrm{col}}
    \sqrt{\frac{\log(C_{\mathrm{col}}/\eta)}{n}}
  \right\}
  \le \eta .
\]
Set
\[
  \overline C_{\mathrm{col}}:=\max\{C_{\mathrm{col}},2\}.
\]
For \(0<\eta<1/2\),
\[
  C_{\mathrm{col}}
  \sqrt{\frac{\log(C_{\mathrm{col}}/\eta)}{n}}
  \le
  \overline C_{\mathrm{col}}
  \sqrt{\frac{\log(\overline C_{\mathrm{col}}/\eta)}{n}},
\]
because \(C_{\mathrm{col}}\le\overline C_{\mathrm{col}}\), the logarithm is monotone on positive arguments, and \(n\ge1\). Therefore, with
\[
  Z_P(\omega):=
  W_1\!\left(\widehat\lambda_n(\omega),\nu_P\right),
  \qquad \omega\in O_{2,2}^n,
\]
we have the uniform tail bound
\[
  Q_P^{(n)}\!\left\{
    Z_P>
    \overline C_{\mathrm{col}}
    \sqrt{\frac{\log(\overline C_{\mathrm{col}}/\eta)}{n}}
  \right\}
  \le \eta .
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.ProxyEffectlawEigencollisionFrontier.collision_uniform_root_n

\item
The one-Wasserstein loss is nonnegative, so \(Z_P(\omega)\ge0\). Define
\[
  \beta_n:=
  \overline C_{\mathrm{col}}
  \sqrt{\frac{\log(2\overline C_{\mathrm{col}})}{n}},
  \qquad
  \chi_n:=
  \frac{n}{\overline C_{\mathrm{col}}^2}.
\]
Since \(\overline C_{\mathrm{col}}\ge2\), both \(\beta_n\) and \(\chi_n\) are positive. For every \(\zeta>\beta_n\), put
\[
  \eta_\zeta:=
  \overline C_{\mathrm{col}}\exp(-\chi_n\zeta^2).
\]
Then \(0<\eta_\zeta<1/2\), and
\[
  \overline C_{\mathrm{col}}
  \sqrt{\frac{\log(\overline C_{\mathrm{col}}/\eta_\zeta)}{n}}
  =
  \overline C_{\mathrm{col}}
  \sqrt{\frac{\chi_n\zeta^2}{n}}
  =
  \zeta .
\]
Substitution in the preceding tail bound gives
\[
  Q_P^{(n)}\{Z_P>\zeta\}
  \le
  \overline C_{\mathrm{col}}\exp(-\chi_n\zeta^2),
  \qquad \zeta>\beta_n .
\]
Using the nonnegative-tail identity and the trivial bound
\(Q_P^{(n)}\{Z_P>\zeta\}\le1\) for \(0<\zeta\le\beta_n\),
\[
\begin{aligned}
  E_{Q_P^{(n)}}[Z_P]
  &\le
  \beta_n+
  \overline C_{\mathrm{col}}
  \int_{\beta_n}^{\infty}\exp(-\chi_n\zeta^2)\,d\zeta  \\
  &\le
  \beta_n+
  \overline C_{\mathrm{col}}
  \int_{0}^{\infty}\exp(-\chi_n\zeta^2)\,d\zeta  \\
  &=
  \beta_n+
  \overline C_{\mathrm{col}}\frac{\sqrt{\pi/\chi_n}}{2}  \\
  &=
  \frac{
    \overline C_{\mathrm{col}}\sqrt{\log(2\overline C_{\mathrm{col}})}
    +
    \overline C_{\mathrm{col}}^2\sqrt{\pi}/2
  }{\sqrt n}.
\end{aligned}
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.ProxyEffectlawEigencollisionFrontier.integral_le_of_sqrt_log_tail

\item
Let
\[
  C_{\mathrm{mean},\nu}:=
  \overline C_{\mathrm{col}}\sqrt{\log(2\overline C_{\mathrm{col}})}
  +
  \overline C_{\mathrm{col}}^2\sqrt{\pi}/2,
  \qquad
  C_{\nu}:=\max\{C_{\mathrm{mean},\nu},\,2c_{\nu}\}.
\]
The first term is positive, so \(C_{\nu}>0\), and \(c_{\nu}<C_{\nu}\). The estimator \(\widehat\lambda_n\) is admissible for the infimum, and the preceding display holds uniformly in \(P\in\mathcal M\). Hence, for every \(n\ge1\),
\[
  \inf_{\widetilde\nu_n}
  \sup_{P\in\mathcal M}
  E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[
    W_1\!\left(\widetilde\nu_n,\nu_P\right)
  \right]
  \le
  \sup_{P\in\mathcal M} E_{Q_P^{(n)}}[Z_P]
  \le
  \frac{C_{\mathrm{mean},\nu}}{\sqrt n}
  \le
  \frac{C_{\nu}}{\sqrt n}.
\]
Taking \(c=c_{\nu}\) and \(C=C_{\nu}\) gives \(0<c<C\) and both displayed minimax inequalities.
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.ProxyEffectlawEigencollisionFrontier.same_class_quotient_minimax
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:prop:same-class-labeled-minimax}]
For \(n\ge 1\) and \(0<g\le 1/4\), set
\[
\beta_{n,g}:=\min\left\{1,\frac{1}{\sqrt n\,g}\right\}.
\]
Let \(\mathsf{Est}_n\) be the class of measurable simplex-valued labelled weight estimators based on \(n\) observed records in the specialized model
\[
k=d_x=d_z=2,\qquad L=2,\qquad \pi_0=\frac1{10},\qquad \sigma_0=\frac1{10},
\]
and define
\[
\mathsf{Risk}_{n,g}
:=
\inf_{\widetilde p_n\in\mathsf{Est}_n}
\sup_{P\in\mathcal M(g)}
E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[
\left\|\widetilde p_n-p^{\uparrow}(P)\right\|_1
\right].
\]

\begin{enumerate}
\item Apply \cref{obj:thm:matching-local-lower-bounds} with \(c_{\mathrm{loc}}=1/4\). It gives a constant \(\kappa_{\mathrm{lo}}>0\) such that, for every \(n\ge1\), every \(0<g\le1/4\), and every \(\widetilde p_n\in\mathsf{Est}_n\), there is a law \(P\in\mathcal L_{n,g}^{p}\) satisfying
\[
\kappa_{\mathrm{lo}}\beta_{n,g}
\le
E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[
\left\|\widetilde p_n-p^{\uparrow}(P)\right\|_1
\right].
\]
By \cref{obj:def:local-weight-experiment}, \(\mathcal L_{n,g}^{p}\subseteq\mathcal M(g)\). Hence, for every admissible \(\widetilde p_n\),
\[
\sup_{P\in\mathcal M(g)}
E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[
\left\|\widetilde p_n-p^{\uparrow}(P)\right\|_1
\right]
\ge
\kappa_{\mathrm{lo}}\beta_{n,g},
\]
and taking the infimum over \(\widetilde p_n\in\mathsf{Est}_n\) yields
\[
\mathsf{Risk}_{n,g}\ge \kappa_{\mathrm{lo}}\beta_{n,g}.
\]
% lean: matching_local_lower_bounds

\item Apply \cref{obj:thm:labeled-weight-upper} with
\[
k=d_x=d_z=2,\qquad L=2,\qquad \pi_0=\frac1{10},\qquad \sigma_0=\frac1{10}.
\]
It gives a constant \(\kappa_{\mathrm{up}}>0\) such that, for every \(n\ge1\), there is an estimator
\[
\widehat p_n^{\uparrow}\in\mathsf{Est}_n
\]
satisfying
\[
E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[
\left\|\widehat p_n^{\uparrow}-p^{\uparrow}(P)\right\|_1
\right]
\le
\kappa_{\mathrm{up}}\beta_{n,g}
\]
for every \(0<g\le1/4\) and every \(P\in\mathcal M(g)\). The target is the ordered aggregate mass vector of the quotient law \(\nu_P\) from \cref{obj:def:quotient-law}: \cref{obj:def:ordered-mass-target} defines it as \(p^{\uparrow}(P)\), and \cref{obj:lem:ordered-masses-measure-invariance} makes this ordered vector depend only on the represented probability law. Therefore
\[
\mathsf{Risk}_{n,g}
\le
\sup_{P\in\mathcal M(g)}
E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[
\left\|\widehat p_n^{\uparrow}-p^{\uparrow}(P)\right\|_1
\right]
\le
\kappa_{\mathrm{up}}\beta_{n,g}.
\]
% lean: labeled_weight_upper

\item Define
\[
c:=\min\left\{\kappa_{\mathrm{lo}},\frac{\kappa_{\mathrm{up}}}{2}\right\},
\qquad
C:=\kappa_{\mathrm{up}}.
\]
Since \(\kappa_{\mathrm{lo}}>0\) and \(\kappa_{\mathrm{up}}>0\),
\[
0<c
\quad\text{and}\quad
c\le\frac{\kappa_{\mathrm{up}}}{2}<\kappa_{\mathrm{up}}=C,
\]
so \(0<c<C\). Combining the bounds from the preceding two steps gives, for every \(n\ge1\) and \(0<g\le1/4\),
\[
c\min\left\{1,\frac{1}{\sqrt n\,g}\right\}
\le
\mathsf{Risk}_{n,g}
\le
C\min\left\{1,\frac{1}{\sqrt n\,g}\right\}.
\]
This is the asserted minimax inequality.
% lean: same_class_labeled_minimax
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:thm:published-vmw-converse-transfer}]
\begin{enumerate}
\item Apply \cref{obj:thm:matching-local-lower-bounds} with
\[
c_{\mathrm{loc}}=\frac14,
\]
which lies in the domain of \cref{obj:def:local-kl-radius}.  This gives constants \(a,c\in\mathbb R\), and an auxiliary positive Kullback--Leibler constant, such that
\[
0<a\le \frac18,
\qquad
0<c,
\]
and all conclusions of \cref{obj:thm:matching-local-lower-bounds} hold for every \(n\ge1\).  In the present specialization,
\[
L_\tau
=\frac{4L\sqrt{d_z}}{\sigma_0}
=\frac{4\cdot2\sqrt2}{1/10}.
\]

We record the published-scope implication used for the endpoint laws.  Let \(P\in\mathcal M\) for
\[
k=d_x=d_z=2,\qquad L=2,\qquad \pi_0=\sigma_0=\frac1{10}.
\]
The restrictions in \cref{obj:def:model-class} supply the qualitative proxy separations, causal consistency, armwise latent ignorability, anchor normalization, boundedness, latent-arm positivity, and proxy-rank margin.  The proxy-rank margin gives
\[
\sigma_2(A_0)\ge \frac1{10},
\qquad
\sigma_2(A_1)\ge \frac1{10},
\qquad
\sigma_2(B)\ge \frac1{10},
\]
so the three proxy feature matrices have full column rank.  Latent-arm positivity gives
\[
P(U=u,T=t)\ge \frac1{10}>0,
\qquad
u\in\{1,2\},\quad t\in\{0,1\}.
\]
Thus \(P\) satisfies the qualitative structural conditions in \cref{obj:synth_11}.  The cited VMW model-scope correspondence, \citep[Assumptions 1--2 and Section 4.2]{VirkMazaheriWu2026}, identifies these qualitative conditions with membership in the published VMW model predicate attached to \cref{obj:def:published-vmw-regimes}.

It remains to verify VMW Assumption 4 in \cref{obj:synth_18}.  Put
\[
M_{\mathrm{stack}}:=\operatorname{stack}(M_0(P),M_1(P)).
\]
Choose a singular-value decomposition
\[
M_{\mathrm{stack}}=\sum_j s_j\ell_j r_j^\top,
\qquad
s_1\ge s_2\ge\cdots .
\]
By \cref{obj:prop:observed-vmw-margin-inclusion},
\[
s_2(M_{\mathrm{stack}})=\sigma_2(M_{\mathrm{stack}})
\ge \pi_0\sigma_0^2=\frac1{1000}>0.
\]
Let \(V=(r_1,r_2)\).  Then the columns of \(V\) are orthonormal and satisfy
\[
M_{\mathrm{stack}}^\top M_{\mathrm{stack}}r_j=s_j^2r_j,
\qquad j=1,2,
\]
so \(V\) is a population top-two right singular basis.  Since \(s_j>0\),
\[
r_j=s_j^{-1}M_{\mathrm{stack}}^\top\ell_j,
\qquad j=1,2.
\]
For \(y=(y_0,y_1)\in\mathbb R^{d_z}\oplus\mathbb R^{d_z}\),
\[
M_{\mathrm{stack}}^\top y=M_0(P)^\top y_0+M_1(P)^\top y_1.
\]
Hence each \(r_j\) lies in the signal space generated by the two armwise row spaces.  The factorization in \cref{obj:prop:observed-vmw-margin-inclusion} gives rank at most \(2\) for this signal space, while \(s_2(M_{\mathrm{stack}})>0\) gives rank at least \(2\).
